### 01_doc_application_application_h

<a id="01-doc-application-application-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/01_doc_application_application_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::Application Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::Application` is an abstract base class that defines the main interface for an AI Assistant application. It provides a standardized structure for application life-cycle management and event handling.

## Usage

Inherit from `Application` to implement an AI Assistant's main workflow. Override its pure virtual functions to define startup, update, event processing, and shutdown behaviors.

## Public Member Functions

- **virtual ~Application() = default;**  
  Virtual destructor. Ensures proper cleanup in derived classes.

- **virtual void OnStart() = 0;**  
  Pure virtual function. Invoked when the application starts. Override this to implement startup logic.

- **virtual void OnUpdate() = 0;**  
  Pure virtual function. Called regularly to update application state. Override to implement main loop logic.

- **virtual void OnEvent(std::shared_ptr<Event>& event) = 0;**  
  Pure virtual function. Handles incoming events. Override to process events.

- **virtual void OnShutdown() = 0;**  
  Pure virtual function. Called during application shutdown. Override to implement cleanup tasks.

- **virtual bool IsFinished() const = 0;**  
  Pure virtual function. Returns `true` if the application has finished execution.

- **std::string const& GetFatalStartupMessage() const**  
  Returns a reference to a string containing a fatal error message if startup fails.

## Protected Members

- **std::string m_FatalStartupMessage;**  
  Stores a fatal startup error message. Accessible in derived classes to set or retrieve initialization errors.

## Notes

- This class requires including `"event/event.h"` for the `Event` type.
- All public methods except the destructor and `GetFatalStartupMessage()` are pure virtual and must be implemented in derived classes.  
- `m_FatalStartupMessage` should be set in the derived class if a startup error occurs; `GetFatalStartupMessage()` can then be used for error reporting.

## Example

```cpp
class MyApplication : public AIAssistant::Application {
public:
    void OnStart() override { /* initialization code */ }
    void OnUpdate() override { /* main loop code */ }
    void OnEvent(std::shared_ptr<Event>& event) override { /* event handling */ }
    void OnShutdown() override { /* cleanup code */ }
    bool IsFinished() const override { return finished; }
private:
    bool finished = false;
};
```

## License

MIT License. See source code for details.

### 02_doc_application_assistant_assistantController_h

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/02_doc_application_assistant_assistantController_h/docu.output.md`



# AssistantController C++ Class Documentation

## Purpose

`AssistantController` manages the `/ws/assistant` WebSocket endpoint for an AI assistant. It handles user messages, slash commands, sessions, background AI calls, tool execution (with approval flow), message delivery to clients, and integration with workflows, memory, and workspace file indexing.

---

## Key Responsibilities

- **WebSocket Session Management**: Track connections, assign active sessions, handle open/close events.
- **Message Handling**: Parse and process incoming user messages, commands, session actions, and approvals.
- **AI Calls**: Dispatch user queries and tool prompts to AI models on background threads, support multi-step interactions including tool use.
- **Tool Execution & Approval**: Parse tool calls in AI responses, execute tools, request user approval if required, and manage execution results.
- **Slash Commands**: Implement commands such as `/help`, `/status`, `/runs`, `/log`, `/memory`, `/index`, `/sessions`, `/new`, and `/clear`.
- **Memory & File Indexing**: Augment prompts with relevant memories and file summaries to improve AI responses.
- **Response Delivery**: Queue and batch-send JSON message responses to all connected WebSocket assistant clients.
- **Lifecycle Management**: Support controlled shutdown of all background tasks and termination of sessions and approvals.

---

## Main Methods

### Public

- `AssistantController()`
  - Constructor. Initializes memory store, workspace index, tool registry, and runs initial workspace scan.
- `~AssistantController()`
  - Destructor. Calls `Shutdown()`.
- `void OnOpen(crow::websocket::connection&)`
  - Called on new client connection.
- `void OnClose(crow::websocket::connection&)`
  - Called on client disconnection.
- `void OnMessage(crow::websocket::connection&, std::string const&)`
  - Called for each message from the frontend. Dispatches to relevant handlers for message types.
- `void DrainPendingMessages()`
  - Delivers all pending batched messages to clients. Invoked after message processing.
- `void Shutdown()`
  - Signals shutdown, joins background threads, closes connections, cancels approvals.
- `void SetWorkflowRegistry(WorkflowRegistry*)`
  - Optionally sets the workflow registry pointer for command-support and tool integration.
- `void SetWorkflowRuntimeManager(WorkflowRuntimeManager*)`
  - Optionally sets the workflow runtime manager pointer.

### Private (Main Functionality)

- **Session Handling**
  - `AssistantSession* GetSession(std::string const&)`: Find or load a session by ID.
  - `AssistantSession* CreateSession()`: Make a new session.
  - `std::filesystem::path GetSessionsDir() const`: Returns sessions directory.

- **Message & Command Handling**
  - `void HandleUserMessage(...)`: Handle normal user input or slash command.
  - `void HandleCommand(...)`: Execute slash command and send response.
  - `void HandleListSessions(...)`: List all session IDs.
  - `void HandleResumeSession(...)`: Activate an existing session for the client.
  - `void HandleNewSession(...)`: Create a new session and activate it.
  - `void HandleGetHistory(...)`: Return deduplicated user messages.
  - `void HandleCompletionRequest(...)`: Return completions for slash commands, workflows, or history.

- **AI Call Handling**
  - `void RunAiCallAsync(...)`: Dispatch an AI call on a background thread, with tool-use loop.
  - `bool RunSingleAiCall(...)`: Blocking call, runs AI inference with given prompt data via the queue-file pipeline.
  - `std::string ValidateResponse(...)`: Checks AI response relevance and file existence.

- **Tool Execution/Aproval**
  - `bool MakeToolAiCall(...)`: Used by tools that require an auxiliary AI call.
  - `bool RequestToolApproval(...)`: Blocks until tool execution is approved or denied by user.
  - `void HandleApprovalResponse(...)`: Handles approval/deny response from frontend.

- **Message Queue**
  - `void QueueMessage(const std::string&)`: Enqueue a message for delivery.
  - `void DrainPendingMessages()`: Batch-send all pending messages to clients.

- **Slash Command Implementations**
  - `std::string HandleHelpCommand()`
  - `std::string HandleStatusCommand()`
  - `std::string HandleRunsCommand()`
  - `std::string HandleLogCommand(const std::string&)`
  - `std::string HandleMemoryCommand(const std::string&)`
  - `std::string HandleIndexCommand(const std::string&)`

- **Thread & Lifecycle**
  - `void JoinFinishedThreads()`: (Background cleanup for finished threads.)

---

## Data Members

### State

- **Client Management**
  - `std::mutex m_ClientsMutex`
  - `std::unordered_set<crow::websocket::connection*> m_Clients`
  - `std::unordered_map<crow::websocket::connection*, ClientState> m_ClientStates`
    - Tracks per-connection state, e.g., active session ID

- **Sessions**
  - `std::mutex m_SessionsMutex`
  - `std::unordered_map<std::string, std::unique_ptr<AssistantSession>> m_Sessions`

- **Message Queue**
  - `std::mutex m_PendingMutex`
  - `std::vector<std::string> m_PendingMessages`

- **Threads**
  - `std::mutex m_ThreadsMutex`
  - `std::vector<std::thread> m_BackgroundThreads`
  - `std::atomic<bool> m_ShuttingDown`
  - `std::atomic<int> m_NextRequestSeq`

- **Tools, Approval, and Memory**
  - `ToolRegistry m_ToolRegistry`
  - `MemoryStore m_MemoryStore`
  - `WorkspaceIndexer m_WorkspaceIndexer`
  - `WorkflowRegistry* m_WorkflowRegistry`
  - `WorkflowRuntimeManager* m_WorkflowRuntimeManager`

- **Tool Approvals**
  - `std::mutex m_ApprovalsMutex`
  - `std::unordered_map<std::string, std::shared_ptr<PendingApproval>> m_PendingApprovals`
  - `std::atomic<int> m_NextApprovalSeq`

- **Constants**
  - `AI_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS = 120000`
  - `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS = 10`
  - `APPROVAL_TIMEOUT_S = 60`

---

## Notes on Design/Usage

- All user messages and commands are asynchronous; AI calls and tool runs happen on background threads.
- Supports multiple connected WebSocket clients, each with individual session tracking.
- All responses are queued and sent in batches to minimize concurrency issues.
- Tool execution is tightly integrated with user approval flow and slash commands.
- Prompts and context are extended with relevant user memories and file summaries for higher response quality.
- Class assumes certain global/singleton pointers are set (`App::g_App`, `Core::g_Core`) for core services.
- Designed to be robust against malformed input and includes relevant error messages for failures.

---

## Thread Safety

- All major shared structures are protected by mutexes.
- Background threads are joined on shutdown.
- Approval flows are synchronized to ensure tool execution blocks until user response or timeout.

---

## Typical Workflow

1. Client connects (`OnOpen`).
2. Client sends message (`OnMessage`), which is parsed and dispatched:
   - User text: resolves session, records message, launches AI call (`RunAiCallAsync`).
   - Slash command: executes and sends result.
   - Approval: unblocks pending tool execution.
3. AI call runs in background, possibly triggers tool calls with approval flow.
4. Responses/tool results/messages are queued.
5. At end of message handler, queue is drained and batch-sent to all clients (`DrainPendingMessages`).
6. Client disconnects (`OnClose`).
7. On shutdown, all threads closed and state cleaned up (`Shutdown`).

---

## Extension Points

- Slash command set can be extended for more system control.
- Tools and workflows can be registered via the appropriate registries.
- Memory and file indexer can be plugged into alternative sources.

---

## Related Classes

- `AssistantSession`: Manages per-session conversation state and persistence.
- `ToolRegistry`: Handles tool definitions, parsing, and execution.
- `MemoryStore`: Stores and retrieves long-term user/AI memories.
- `WorkspaceIndexer`: Tracks files and summaries for context retrieval.
- `WorkflowRegistry`: Register workflows available to the assistant.
- `WorkflowRuntimeManager`: Manages running workflow instances.

---

## Summary

`AssistantController` is a central orchestrator for AI chat interactions via WebSocket. It coordinates client messaging, AI calls, tool usage, system commands, session management, data context augmentation, and system lifecycle, enabling interactive and extensible human/AI workflows.

### 03_doc_application_assistant_assistantMemory_h

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/03_doc_application_assistant_assistantMemory_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::MemoryStore — Documentation

**Overview**

`AIAssistant::MemoryStore` is a thread-safe, persistent memory store class implemented in C++. It stores, retrieves, searches, and manages small textual memory entries, each identified by a unique key. Data is stored and loaded from a JSON file on disk.

This class is designed to support both AI agents and humans by providing fast recall and storage for information relevant to conversations, topics, or sessions.

---

## MemoryEntry Structure

- **id**: Unique memory entry identifier (auto-generated).
- **key**: Short label or topic for the memory. Acts as a unique identifier within the store.
- **value**: Memory content (plain text).
- **tags**: List of string tags for classification or search.
- **createdAt**: Creation or last modification timestamp (ISO-8601 UTC).
- **sourceSessionId**: Optional session identifier that created the memory.

---

## Public Methods

### Constructor

**MemoryStore(std::filesystem::path storePath)**  
Initializes a new memory store, loading existing entries from the specified JSON file.

---

### Save

**std::string Save(const std::string& key, const std::string& value, const std::vector<std::string>& tags = {}, const std::string& sessionId = {})**

- Adds a new memory entry or updates an existing one (matched by key).
- Updates `value`, `tags`, `createdAt`, and optionally `sourceSessionId`.
- Persists changes to disk immediately.
- Returns the unique entry id.

---

### Delete

**bool Delete(const std::string& key)**

- Removes a memory entry by its key.
- Returns `true` if the entry was found and deleted, otherwise `false`.
- Persists changes to disk immediately.

---

### Recall

**std::vector<MemoryEntry> Recall(const std::string& query) const**

- Searches for memories matching the given query in key, value, or tags using keyword overlap.
- Query is tokenized into words; results are scored and sorted by relevance.
- Returns all results with a nonzero score; if query is empty, returns all memories.

---

### ListAll

**std::vector<MemoryEntry> ListAll() const**

- Returns all memory entries for summary display.
- No search or filtering performed.

---

### GetRelevant

**std::vector<MemoryEntry> GetRelevant(const std::string& userMessage, int maxResults = 5) const**

- Returns up to `maxResults` most relevant memories for the given user message.
- Uses same keyword-overlap scoring as `Recall`.
- Results are sorted by relevance.

---

### ClearAll

**void ClearAll()**

- Deletes all memories from the store.
- Persists changes to disk immediately.

---

### Size

**size_t Size() const**

- Returns the current number of stored entries.

---

## Thread Safety

All public methods lock an internal mutex. Simultaneous use from multiple threads is supported.

---

## Persistence

- Memory entries are persisted as a JSON array to the file provided in the constructor.
- File is read at construction and updated after each change (`Save`, `Delete`, `ClearAll`).

---

## Internal Functionality

- Key and tag matching are case-insensitive.
- Entries are scored based on keyword matches in `key`, `value`, and `tags`. The `key` field is weighted higher.
- Each entry's `createdAt` timestamp is updated on save/update.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::MemoryStore store("memories.json");
store.Save("project_x", "Details about project X", {"work", "project"}, "session_1");
auto results = store.Recall("project");
for (const auto& entry : results) {
    std::cout << entry.key << ": " << entry.value << std::endl;
}
```

---

## Dependencies

- C++17 or higher
- simdjson library (for JSON parsing)

---

## License

MIT License — see source file headers for details.


---

### 04_doc_application_assistant_assistantSession_h

<a id="04-doc-application-assistant-assistantsession-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/04_doc_application_assistant_assistantSession_h/docu.output.md`



# AssistantSession C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

The `AIAssistant::AssistantSession` class manages the storage, retrieval, and organization of conversation history between a user and an AI assistant. It provides thread-safe operations for session creation, loading, message handling, and persistent storage using append-only JSONL files.

All public methods are thread-safe. Each session is mapped to a persistent JSONL file in a user-specified directory.

---

## Data Structures

### `AssistantTurn`
Represents one turn in a conversation.

- `std::string role`  
  Role of sender: "user", "assistant", or "system".
- `std::string text`  
  Message content.
- `std::string timestamp`  
  ISO 8601 UTC timestamp when the message was added.

---

## Public Interface

### Constructors

- `AssistantSession(const std::filesystem::path& sessionsDir)`
  - Creates a new session. Generates a unique session ID. Initializes a new empty JSONL file in `sessionsDir`.

- `AssistantSession(const std::filesystem::path& sessionsDir, const std::string& sessionId)`
  - Loads an existing session from a JSONL file in `sessionsDir` corresponding to `sessionId`.

---

### Methods

#### Getters

- `const std::string& GetSessionId() const`
  - Returns the unique session ID.

- `const std::string& GetStartedAt() const`
  - Returns the timestamp when the session started (timestamp of first turn).

- `size_t GetTurnCount() const`
  - Returns total number of turns in the session.

#### Add Messages

- `void AddUserMessage(const std::string& text)`
  - Appends a user-sent message to the conversation and persists it.

- `void AddAssistantMessage(const std::string& text)`
  - Appends an assistant-sent message to the conversation and persists it.

#### Turn Retrieval

- `std::vector<AssistantTurn> GetRecentTurns(size_t maxTokens) const`
  - Returns the most recent turns whose combined text token estimate (text length / 4) does not exceed `maxTokens`. Turns are ordered oldest to newest.

- `std::vector<AssistantTurn> GetAllTurns() const`
  - Returns all turns in order.

#### Session Enumeration

- `static std::vector<std::string> ListSessions(const std::filesystem::path& sessionsDir)`
  - Returns all available session IDs found in `sessionsDir`, sorted most recent first.

---

## Private/Internal Methods

- `void AppendTurn(const AssistantTurn& turn)`
  - Internal. Adds a turn in memory and appends as JSONL to the session file.

- `void LoadFromFile()`
  - Internal. Loads all turns from the session file.

- `static std::string GenerateSessionId()`
  - Generates a unique session ID using timestamp and an atomic counter.

- `static std::string NowIso8601()`
  - Returns current UTC time in ISO 8601 format.

---

## Persistence

- Each session is stored as a `.jsonl` (newline-delimited JSON) file in the provided directory.
- Each line contains a single message with keys: `role`, `text`, `ts`.
- File writes are append-only for durability.

---

## Thread Safety

- All public methods are protected by a mutex.
- Safe for concurrent access from multiple threads.

---

## Notes

- No external JSON library dependency; performs minimal JSON escaping and parsing internally.
- Session IDs encode wall-clock time and a counter for ordering and uniqueness.
- Handles directory creation and missing-file conditions gracefully.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::AssistantSession session("/path/to/assistant/sessions");
session.AddUserMessage("Hello, Assistant!");
session.AddAssistantMessage("Hello, User! How can I help?");
auto turns = session.GetAllTurns();
```

---

## License

MIT License © 2025 JC Technolabs

### 05_doc_application_assistant_assistantTools_h

<a id="05-doc-application-assistant-assistanttools-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/05_doc_application_assistant_assistantTools_h/docu.output.md`



# ToolRegistry Class — Documentation

## Overview

The `ToolRegistry` class is a core part of the `AIAssistant` namespace. It defines, registers, and dispatches a set of tools (actions) that can be invoked by AI or humans to interact with the JarvisAgent system, workflows, files, and persistent memories. The class provides APIs for tool introspection, execution, and parsing of tool calls from AI responses. It implements strict permission and security checks and supports both read-only and mutating (side-effecting) operations. It relies on other subsystems (e.g., `MemoryStore`, `WorkflowRegistry`, `WorkflowRuntimeManager`) to handle their respective domains.

---

## Purpose

- **Unified tool registry:** Central location for defining and implementing all tools accessible to AI/human users in the assistant system.
- **Execution and argument handling:** Maps tool calls (by name) to the underlying implementations, including argument validation and error reporting.
- **Security:** Enforces deny-lists and argument validation for sensitive or mutating operations.
- **Integration:** Supports other subsystems (workflow engine, memory persistence, file system, AI calls, etc.), requiring references to several manager classes.
- **AI toolcall parsing:** Extracts tool instructions embedded in AI-generated text.

---

## Main Types

### ToolDef

Defines a tool:
- `name`: Unique string name of the tool (used for invocation).
- `description`: Short, human-readable description.
- `argsDescription`: Human-readable explanation of tool arguments.
- `requiresApproval`: True if the tool mutates system state and requires user approval.

### ToolCall

Represents a parsed tool call:
- `name`: Tool name to invoke.
- `args`: Key-value argument map.

### ToolResult

Represents the result of a tool execution:
- `toolName`: Name of the tool executed.
- `ok`: `true` if successful, `false` otherwise.
- `output`: Text result or error message.

---

## Key APIs

### Construction and Setup

- `ToolRegistry()`: Registers all supported tools and their handlers.
- `SetWorkflowRegistry(WorkflowRegistry*)`, `SetWorkflowRuntimeManager(WorkflowRuntimeManager*)`, `SetMemoryStore(MemoryStore*)`, `SetWorkspaceIndexer(WorkspaceIndexer*)`: Inject references to underlying subsystems for workflow/memory/file actions.
- `SetAiCallFn(AiCallFn fn)`: Injects a blocking AI call function used for tools requiring AI summarization or code generation.

### Listing and Description

- `GetToolDefs() const`: Returns all registered tool definitions for documentation or building AI prompts.
- `BuildToolDescriptions() const`: Returns a formatted string describing all registered tools and their arguments (for inclusion in system prompts).

### Tool Execution

- `ToolResult Execute(const ToolCall&)`: Executes a tool call by dispatching to the correct handler. Returns a result struct.
- Each tool (e.g., `ExecReadFile`, `ExecRunShell`) receives validated arguments and implements its behavior and security restrictions.

### AI Tool Call Parsing

- `static std::vector<ToolCall> ParseToolCalls(const std::string& responseText, std::string& outCleanText)`: Scans AI response text for `<tool_call>...</tool_call>` blocks, parses and removes them from the original string, returning the decoded tool calls.

---

## Tool Categories and Security

Tools are categorized by risk:

- **L1 (status/workflow queries):** Read-only, always callable
- **L2 (readonly file/memory/tools):** Reads files, searches, saves/recalls memory, gets file summaries.
  - Explicit deny-lists prevent path traversal, sensitive file access (e.g., `config.json`, keys, assistant memory, etc).
- **L3 (mutating/actions):** Write/edit files, execute arbitrary shell commands, modify workflows/scripts, or runtime control (pause/resume/stop).  
  - These always require explicit user approval (`requiresApproval=true`).
  - All mutating operations perform extra validation and backup existing files before modification.
  - Shell execution is restricted by blocklists (dangerous patterns).

---

## Tool Implementation Summary

**Key tool implementations:**

- `get_system_status`: Returns system status (version, uptime, workflow/stats).
- `list_workflows`: Lists all defined workflows.
- `get_run_status`: Returns state/details of a workflow run.
- `list_recent_runs`: Lists recent and active workflow runs.
- `get_task_output`: Gets stdout/stderr for a workflow task instance.
- `run_workflow`: Starts a workflow run (requires approval).
- `read_file`: Reads a file's contents within security controls.
- `search_files`: Searches for text patterns in files using ripgrep/grep.
- `list_files`: Lists directory contents (with excluded noisy directories).
- **Memory tools:** save, recall, list, and delete persistent memory records.
- `get_file_summary` / `get_folder_summary`: Returns (possibly AI-generated) summaries of code files/folders for code understanding.
- **Mutating tools:** `run_shell`, `write_file`, `edit_file` for system changes (all require approval and validation).
- **Runtime control:** `workflow_pause`, `workflow_resume`, `workflow_stop`, `workflow_clean`, `get_dashboard_status`.
- **JCWF workflow development tools:** read/explain/validate/generate/fix JCWF workflow files; read/write development plans; write scripts to scripts/ directory. Backups are made automatically, and JCWF generation/fix uses AI with proper prompt templates.

---

## Static Helpers

- `IsPathDenied(const std::string&)`: Returns true if a path is on a read/write deny-list.
- `TruncateOutput(const std::string&, size_t)`: Truncates large outputs or file reads.
- Internal: helper functions for JSON parsing, path normalization, blocklist checks.

---

## Usage Scenarios

1. **Assistant Tool Configuration:** On startup, the ToolRegistry is constructed. Subsystem pointers (memory, workflow manager, etc.) are set.
2. **AI Prompt Construction:** `BuildToolDescriptions()` provides human-readable tool descriptions for injection into AI prompts, to teach the assistant what it can do.
3. **AI Tool Call Execution:** When the assistant's LLM emits a `<tool_call>...</tool_call>` or equivalent, `ParseToolCalls()` extracts the call, and `Execute()` runs it safely.
4. **Human Command Execution:** User-initiated actions (e.g., via UI CLI, API) are dispatched through the same `ToolRegistry` for consistent permission/security and reporting.
5. **Automated Workflow Automation:** Mutating or AI-driven code generation and workflow editing is mediated through tools wrapping file/zip/jcwf script manipulation.

---

## Security and Best Practices

- **Never allow arbitrary file access:** Paths are normalized, blocked against sensitive files, traversal, and absolute paths.
- **All risky actions require explicit approval:** The system (not the AI) prompts the user, ensuring no blind destructive operations.
- **AI-controlled tools share the same APIs as human access:** Ensures consistent security model.
- **Backups on destructive changes:** File/system edits backup prior state.
- **All outputs truncated to safe limits:** Prevents log and reply flooding.

---

## Extensibility

- New tools can be added by updating the tool definition/registration list and implementing the corresponding `Exec*` method.
- Changes to required arguments, doc descriptions, and access permissions are centrally managed.
- Tool call parsing assumes simple `<tool_call>...</tool_call>` blocks with a JSON object containing `name` and `args`.

---

## Integration Points

- Other components: `MemoryStore`, `WorkspaceIndexer`, `WorkflowRegistry`, and `WorkflowRuntimeManager` are not owned by ToolRegistry but are set via setter APIs.
- `AiCallFn`: Required for tools needing AI-generated content.
- File-system and shell integration use portable logic, including platform-specific process execution.

---

## Summary

The `ToolRegistry` class is the assistant's central action manager, enabling rapid, safe, and extensible interfaces for both AI-driven and human-initiated workflows, file/memory operations, workflow automation, and development tooling, with strict security and robust argument handling. All features are discoverable through built-in prompt/documentation APIs and tool result reporting is consistent and error-resilient.


---

### 06_doc_application_assistant_contextAssembler_h

<a id="06-doc-application-assistant-contextassembler-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/06_doc_application_assistant_contextAssembler_h/docu.output.md`



# ContextAssembler Class Documentation

## Overview

`ContextAssembler` is a C++ class responsible for assembling structured prompts to be sent to an AI assistant within the JarvisAgent workflow system. It consolidates various sources of information—system prompt, conversation history, user instructions, and tool descriptions—into a standardized format, facilitating effective AI interactions.

## Namespace

`AIAssistant`

## Key Types

### AssembledPrompt

A `struct` representing the complete prompt components:

- `stng`: System prompt, containing settings and reference information.
- `task`: Task instructions.
- `cntx`: Conversation context/history.
- `prob`: User's current message or problem statement.

## Public Interface

### static AssembledPrompt Assemble(
std::vector<AssistantTurn> const& recentTurns,
std::string const& userMessage,
std::string const& toolDescriptions = {}
)

**Purpose:**  
Builds an `AssembledPrompt` using the provided conversation history (`recentTurns`), the user's current message (`userMessage`), and optionally, available tool descriptions (`toolDescriptions`).  
It injects all required information for an AI call in the JarvisAgent context.

**Parameters:**
- `recentTurns`: List of previous conversation turns (`AssistantTurn` objects) for context.
- `userMessage`: The latest message from the user.
- `toolDescriptions` (optional): Details of available tools, appended to the system prompt if provided.

**Returns:**
- `AssembledPrompt`: Struct with all fields populated as required for an AI call.

## Internal Implementation

### System Prompt Construction

- **BuildSystemPrompt()**  
  Returns a multi-section system prompt with usage guidelines, workflow references, command handling policies, memory management instructions, and system feature documentation.  
  Tool descriptions are injected if provided.

### Conversation Context

- **BuildConversationContext(std::vector<AssistantTurn> const& turns)**  
  Formats up to the given number of previous turns into the context section as alternating "User:" and "Assistant:" entries.

### Prompt Assembly

- The system prompt (`stng`) is always included.
- Task instructions (`task`) prompt the AI to respond concisely to the user.
- Conversation context (`cntx`) gives session continuity, providing relevant history.
- User problem (`prob`) is set as the new user message.

## Usage Example

```cpp
std::vector<AssistantTurn> history = ...;  // Existing conversation turns
std::string userMessage = "How do I create a new workflow?";
std::string toolDesc = "Tool: jcwf_generate, ..."; // Optional

AIAssistant::AssembledPrompt prompt =
    AIAssistant::ContextAssembler::Assemble(history, userMessage, toolDesc);

// Prompt is ready to be sent to the AI backend
```

## Notes

- The prompt structure aligns with JarvisAgent's file queue conventions (STNG/TASK/CNTX/PROB files).
- The design ensures that the AI assistant receives all necessary reference materials and context for accurate, safe, and efficient responses.
- Strict compliance rules are embedded in the system prompt to ensure predictable AI behavior and avoid unsafe or speculative outputs.

## See Also

- `AssistantTurn` struct: Represents a single user/assistant message in conversation history.
- JarvisAgent documentation for queue folder architecture and AI tool conventions.

## License

This class is licensed under the MIT License (see source comments for detail).

### 07_doc_application_assistant_workspaceIndexer_h

<a id="07-doc-application-assistant-workspaceindexer-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/07_doc_application_assistant_workspaceIndexer_h/docu.output.md`



# WorkspaceIndexer C++ Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `assistant/workspaceIndexer.h`  
**Purpose:** Fast and thread-safe source file indexing and AI-summary caching for a workspace.

---

## Overview

`WorkspaceIndexer` indexes source files within specified workspace directories, maintaining metadata and optional AI-generated summaries for each file. It enables fast lookup, summary retrieval, and semantic file search. The index is persisted to disk and updated incrementally across sessions. Thread safety is ensured by mutex locking on all public methods.

---

## FileIndexEntry Structure

Represents a single source file in the index.

- `std::string relativePath`: File path relative to workspace root.
- `std::string extension`: File extension (e.g., `.cpp`, `.h`).
- `uint64_t sizeBytes`: File size in bytes.
- `int64_t mtimeNs`: Filesystem modification time (nanoseconds since epoch).
- `std::string summary`: AI-generated file summary (empty if not cached).
- `int64_t summaryMtimeNs`: `mtimeNs` when summary was generated (0 if not summarized).

---

## Main Responsibilities

- Scans and indexes files in configured directories, skipping known vendor and temporary folders.
- On startup, loads a persistent index (`assistant/index/file_index.jsonl`), preserving and revalidating cached summaries.
- Updates the index only for changed, added, or deleted files.
- Caches AI-generated summaries per-file, invalidating/staling them when their source file changes.
- Supports efficient, thread-safe queries and lookups by path or by keyword relevance.
- Supports reading file contents (with size/trust limits) for summarization.
- Exposes statistics (file count, summary count, scan time).

---

## Thread Safety

All public methods use a mutex (`std::mutex m_Mutex`).

---

## Key Methods

### Constructors

- **WorkspaceIndexer(std::filesystem::path indexDir)**  
  Initialize indexer using directory `indexDir` (usually `assistant/index/`).

### Indexing

- **void ScanWorkspace()**  
  Full scan: loads previous index, scans for changes in configured directories, adds/updates/removes index entries, and writes updated index. Preserves summaries if files are unchanged.

### File Summary Operations

- **std::string GetFileSummary(const std::string& relativePath) const**  
  Returns cached summary for the specified file path, or empty string if not cached or stale.

- **void SetFileSummary(const std::string& relativePath, const std::string& summary)**  
  Stores a new AI-generated summary for the specified file and updates summary timestamp.

### File Content Access

- **static std::string ReadFileContent(const std::string& relativePath, size_t maxBytes = 32768)**  
  Reads up to `maxBytes` of file content (with workspace scope restrictions). Returns empty string on error or disallowed path.

### Semantic Search

- **std::vector<FileIndexEntry> GetRelevantFiles(const std::string& query, int maxResults = 5) const**  
  Returns up to `maxResults` indexed files with summaries matching query keywords, sorted by relevance.

### Stats and Accessors

- **size_t FileCount() const**  
  Returns the total number of indexed files.

- **size_t SummaryCount() const**  
  Returns count of files with cached summaries.

- **std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point LastScanTime() const**  
  Returns timestamp of last workspace scan.

- **std::vector<FileIndexEntry> GetAllEntries() const**  
  Returns every current file index entry.

---

## Index Update and Persistence

- Index is stored as JSONL at `assistant/index/file_index.jsonl`.
- On each scan, existing index entries (and summaries) are reused unless the source file has changed.
- New, updated, and deleted files trigger index changes, and summaries are invalidated if the file changes.
- Uses fast file mtime check for update detection.
- Skips indexing certain directories (`node_modules`, `.git`, etc.) and only accepts known code file extensions.

---

## Limitations

- Only a pre-configured set of directories and file extensions are indexed.
- Summaries are invalidated solely based on file `mtime`.
- File content reads are restricted to prevent directory traversal attacks (paths outside workspace are blocked).
- Max summary cache size per file is limited by practical limits (no explicit size management).

---

## Typical Usage Flow

1. **Create `WorkspaceIndexer` instance** at startup, specifying index directory.
2. **Call `ScanWorkspace()`** to populate the in-memory index (loads from disk and updates as needed).
3. **Call `GetFileSummary()`** to retrieve AI summaries for files (optionally as context for AI tools).
4. **Call `SetFileSummary()`** after generating or updating a summary.
5. **Use `GetRelevantFiles()`** to find files semantically matching a search query.
6. **Periodically monitor with `FileCount()`, `SummaryCount()`, and `LastScanTime()`** for UI or integration purposes.

---

## Example: Getting a File's Summary

```cpp
AIAssistant::WorkspaceIndexer indexer("assistant/index");
indexer.ScanWorkspace();
std::string summary = indexer.GetFileSummary("engine/math/vec3.h");
```

---

## Example: Storing a New Summary

```cpp
indexer.SetFileSummary("engine/math/vec3.h", "Handles 3D vector math operations.");
```

---

## Example: Semantic File Search

```cpp
auto results = indexer.GetRelevantFiles("3d vector math");
for (const auto& entry : results) {
    // use entry.relativePath or entry.summary
}
```

---

## Summary

`WorkspaceIndexer` is a utility for codebases requiring file inventory, fast file queries, and efficient AI-context integration, with robust threading and persistence. Use when you need to reliably track and cache metadata/summaries about source files in a workspace.


---

### 08_doc_application_cloud_azureBlobCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="08-doc-application-cloud-azureblobcloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/08_doc_application_cloud_azureBlobCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# AzureBlobCloudTaskExecutor C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`AzureBlobCloudTaskExecutor` is a task executor class for performing Azure Blob Storage upload and download operations within a workflow system. It implements the `ICloudTaskExecutor` interface and handles JCWF tasks of type `"azure_blob_upload"` and `"azure_blob_download"` using the Azure Blob Storage API.

---
## Class Definition

**Namespace**: `AIAssistant`

**Base Class**: `ICloudTaskExecutor`

```cpp
class AzureBlobCloudTaskExecutor : public ICloudTaskExecutor
```
---
## Supported Operations

- **Upload:** Uploads a local file to Azure Blob Storage.
- **Download:** Downloads a blob from Azure Blob Storage to a local file.

---
## Task Parameters

Task parameters are provided as a JSON object under `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`. The following keys are supported:

| Parameter     | Type     | Required | Description                                     |
|---------------|----------|----------|-------------------------------------------------|
| `connection`  | string   | yes      | Named CloudConnection to use for authentication. |
| `container`   | string   | no       | Blob container name. If not specified, uses the connection’s default container. |
| `blob_name`   | string   | yes      | Blob name (path) within the container.           |
| `local_path`  | string   | yes      | Local file path (relative to working directory). |
| `operation`   | string   | yes      | `"upload"` or `"download"`.                     |

---
## Authentication

Supports the following credential types:
- **Azure Shared Key**
- **OAuth2 Bearer Token**

Authentication headers are set accordingly before making HTTP requests to Azure Blob Storage.

---
## Key Methods

### ExecuteCloud (Main Task Execution)

#### Signature

```cpp
bool ExecuteCloud(
   WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition,
   WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
   TaskDef const& taskDefinition,
   TaskInstanceState& taskState,
   CloudConnection const& connection,
   CloudCredentials const& credentials,
   TaskCancellationToken const& cancellationToken
) override;
```

#### Description

Performs upload or download operation as specified in the task parameters:
- Parses JSON parameters.
- Validates required fields.
- Upload:
    - Reads local file.
    - Uploads to specified Azure Blob container and blob name using HTTP PUT and appropriate headers.
- Download:
    - Downloads specified blob to the local path.
    - Ensures local output directory exists.
- Updates the task state with result, status, and logs any errors.

#### Errors/Failures

- Fails if required parameters are missing.
- Fails if authentication, file I/O, or network operations fail.
- Error messages are set in `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`.

---
## Helper Functions

- **SetAuthHeaders**: Constructs HTTP authorization headers based on credential type.
- **AzureBlobRequest**: Performs HTTP requests (e.g., PUT for upload) with curl and handles responses.
- **AzureBlobDownload**: Downloads a blob using HTTP GET to a file, with size and timeout limits.

---
## Limits and Constants

- Maximum download size: 256 MB.
- HTTP timeout: 300 seconds.
- Standard output capture is truncated to 1024 characters.

---
## Logging & Output

- Logs successful operations.
- Writes a JSON response into the task working directory using `WriteResponseJson`.

---
## Example Usage

A workflow task JSON for uploading might include:
```json
{
    "connection": "azure_conn_1",
    "container": "mycontainer",
    "blob_name": "data/output.csv",
    "local_path": "output/output.csv",
    "operation": "upload"
}
```

A download task differs only in `"operation": "download"` and relevant path changes.

---
## License

Copyright (c) 2026 JC Technolabs  
License: GPL-3.0

---
## Summary

`AzureBlobCloudTaskExecutor` is used by JCWF workflow tasks to upload and download files between local storage and Azure Blob Storage, handling all HTTP interaction, authentication, and result management within a workflow engine context.


---

### 09_doc_application_cloud_azureBlobConnector_h

<a id="09-doc-application-cloud-azureblobconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/09_doc_application_cloud_azureBlobConnector_h/docu.output.md`



# AzureBlobConnector Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**File:** `cloud/azureBlobConnector.h`, `cloud/azureBlobConnector.cpp`  
**Base Class:** `ICloudConnector`

## Overview

The `AzureBlobConnector` class provides integration with Azure Blob Storage.  
It allows establishing and validating connections, authenticating via Azure Shared Key or OAuth2 credentials, and constructing appropriate endpoint URLs.

## Key Responsibilities

- Identifies as the Azure Blob connector type.
- Resolves and validates credentials for Azure Blob Storage.
- Tests Azure Blob Storage connectivity and permissions.
- Builds endpoint URLs for Azure Blob Storage based on connection parameters.

## Connection Parameters (`CloudConnection.m_Params`)

| Parameter        | Description                                                                     | Required |
|------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------|
| `account_name`   | Azure Storage account name                                                       | Yes      |
| `container`      | Default blob container name (can be overridden per-task)                         | Yes      |

## Authentication

- `CloudConnection.m_AuthType`  
  - `AzureSharedKey` (default): Uses a Base64-encoded account key.
  - `OAuth2`: Uses Azure AD OAuth2 tokens.
- `CloudConnection.m_KeyName`: Credential identifier (for KeyManager or OAuthTokenManager).

## Endpoint

- `CloudConnection.m_Endpoint`  
  - If specified, used as the base URL.
  - If not, defaults to:  
    `https://{account_name}.blob.core.windows.net`

## Public Methods

### `std::string GetType() const override`

Returns the string identifier `"azure_blob"`.

---

### `bool ResolveCredentials(const CloudConnection &connection, CloudCredentials &credentials, std::string &errorMessage) override`

Resolves credentials based on the connection's `m_KeyName` and `m_AuthType`.

- For `OAuth2`:
  - Fetches an OAuth token using `OAuthTokenManager`.
- For `AzureSharedKey`:
  - Fetches the account key from `KeyManager`.
  - Expects the key in `api_key` (Base64) or `password`.
  - Stores account name in credentials.

Returns `true` on success; otherwise, returns `false` and sets `errorMessage`.

---

### `bool TestConnection(const CloudConnection &connection, std::string &errorMessage) override`

Tests connection and credentials by issuing an HTTP GET to  
`/{container}?restype=container` at the Azure Blob endpoint.

- Checks for required parameters and credentials.
- Signs requests as needed.
- Returns `true` if the Blob container can be accessed;  
  Otherwise, returns `false` and sets `errorMessage`.

---

### `static std::string BuildEndpointUrl(const CloudConnection &connection)`

Builds and returns the Azure Blob endpoint URL.

- Uses `m_Endpoint` if present (trailing `/` stripped).
- Otherwise, constructs the URL with `account_name`:  
  `https://{account_name}.blob.core.windows.net`

---

## Usage Summary

1. Provide a `CloudConnection` with `"account_name"` and `"container"` in `m_Params`.
2. Set the authentication type (`AzureSharedKey` or `OAuth2`) and reference credentials.
3. Use `TestConnection` to verify the setup.
4. Use `BuildEndpointUrl` to get the storage endpoint.

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::AzureBlobConnector connector;
CloudConnection connection;
// Populate connection parameters and credentials...

std::string error;
if (connector.TestConnection(connection, error)) {
    // Connection succeeded
} else {
    // Handle error (details in 'error')
}
```

---

## See Also

- `ICloudConnector`
- `CloudConnection`
- `CloudCredentials`
- `KeyManager`
- `OAuthTokenManager`
- [`Azure Blob Storage documentation`](https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/storage/blobs/storage-blobs-introduction)


---

### 100_doc_application_workflow_workflowRuntimeManager_h

<a id="100-doc-application-workflow-workflowruntimemanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/100_doc_application_workflow_workflowRuntimeManager_h/docu.output.md`



# WorkflowRuntimeManager C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`WorkflowRuntimeManager` is a tick-based, thread-safe runtime for managing and executing workflows in the AIAssistant system. It orchestrates the lifecycle of workflow runs, supporting asynchronous, non-blocking operation. The class is responsible for the dispatch, execution, monitoring, and completion of workflows, handling task dependencies, control flow (including branching and per-item fan-out), cancellation, pausing, stopping, AI request coordination, artifact cleanup, and sub-workflow propagation. Designed to be invoked periodically from the main thread.

---

## Key Concepts

- **Workflow Run**: A single execution instance of a workflow definition.
- **Task**: A step in a workflow. May be of various types (ai_call, shell, python, etc).
- **Per-Item Tasks**: Tasks that fan out to multiple instances, one per item produced by a filter.
- **Controlflow**: Conditional branching within a workflow (normal and error paths).
- **External Waiting**: Tasks that require asynchronous completion (e.g., waiting for AI response or file event).
- **Observers**: Optionally notifies observers when a workflow run completes or fails.

---

## Thread Safety

All public methods are thread-safe unless otherwise noted. The manager ensures that access to internal structures is synchronized.

---

## Main Lifecycle Methods

| Method | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `void Start()` | Mark the runtime as running (can accept and process runs). |
| `void Stop()` | Initiate shutdown: cancel all runs, clear pending runs, wait for workers to finish, clean up resources. |
| `void SignalStop()` | Begin shutdown by marking as stopped, requesting cancellation of all active runs. Non-blocking. |
| `void WaitStop()` | Waits for all worker/future tasks to finish before cleanup. Called after `SignalStop()`. |
| `bool Update()` | Advances the runtime: starts pending runs, harvests completions, transitions state, dispatches new work. Returns `true` if any workflow run changes state. Must be called periodically (`tick`). |
| `void SetRegistry(const WorkflowRegistry*)` | Set the workflow registry used for lookups. Must be set before use. |

---

## Workflow Run Management

| Method | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `void EnqueueWorkflowRun(const std::string&)` | Enqueue new workflow run by workflow ID (non-blocking). |
| `std::string EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId(const std::string&)` | Enqueue run, return assigned run ID (unique per run). |
| `std::string EnqueueWorkflowRunWithContextAndGetRunId(const std::string&, const std::string&, const ContextMap&)` | Enqueue a run with a given run ID (or auto-generate) and initial context. For integration scenarios. |
| `bool TryGetActiveRun(const std::string& runId, WorkflowRun& outRun)` | Retrieve latest state of an active (still running) run. |
| `bool TryGetLastRun(const std::string& workflowId, WorkflowRun& outRun)` | Get snapshot of last completed run for a workflow ID. |
| `bool TryGetRunById(const std::string& runId, WorkflowRun& outRun)` | Find any run (active or finished) by run ID. |
| `std::vector<WorkflowRun> GetActiveRunsSnapshot() const` | Get all active (running or pending) runs. |
| `std::unordered_map<std::string, WorkflowRun> GetLastRunsSnapshot() const` | Get last finished run per workflow. |
| `void GetRunCounters(uint64_t& outCompleted, uint64_t& outFailed) const` | Get the total completed and failed run counts. |

---

## Run Control

| Method | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `bool RequestCancelRun(const std::string& runId)` | Request cancellation for a running run. Attempts best-effort, non-blocking cancellation. Returns true if successful. |
| `bool RequestPauseRun(const std::string& runId)` | Request pause (no new tasks dispatched). In-flight tasks are still harvested. Returns true if successful. |
| `bool RequestResumeRun(const std::string& runId)` | Resume a paused run. Returns true if successful. |
| `bool RequestStopRun(const std::string& runId)` | Request cooperative stop: in-flight work allowed to finish, new tasks not dispatched. Returns true if successful. |

---

## Clean-Up & Housekeeping

| Method | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `bool CleanWorkflow(const std::string& workflowId, std::string& outErrorMessage)` | Deletes all output artifacts produced by the specified workflow, including files, working directories, and queue folders. Refuses if workflow is currently running. Outputs error messages if cleanup fails. |
| `void SetRunTerminalObserver(RunTerminalObserver observer)` | Register a callback for when a run reaches a terminal state (completed, failed, cancelled, stopped). |

---

## Heartbeat & Watchdog

| Method | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `bool Heartbeat(const std::string& taskInstanceId)` | Resets the inactivity timer (watchdog) for a specific task instance. Used by REST endpoints / live integrations. |

---

## Sub-Workflow Support

| Method | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `void RegisterSubWorkflowLink(childRunId, parentRunId, parentTaskInstanceId)` | Register relationship so child completion propagates to parent workflow task. |
| (internal) | On each tick, sub-workflow completions are detected and parent tasks marked as succeeded/failed accordingly. |
| (internal) | If a parent run is cancelled, propagates cancellation to all child runs. |

---

## Implementation Notes

- **Task Execution**: Uses a thread pool for scheduling non-blocking work; actual task execution is performed asynchronously.
- **AI Requests**: Supports ai_call tasks via a central `AiRequestPool`, which is drained inside `Update()`.
- **Controlflow / Branching**: Supports conditional execution of tasks based on outcome of other tasks, including both "normal" and "error" branches.
- **Per-Item Fan-Out**: For tasks with `per_item` mode, dispatches child task instances after filter evaluation.
- **Freshness Checking**: Tasks and per-item children can be skipped if their outputs are already up-to-date, using configurable freshness logic.
- **External Waiting and Timeouts**: Tasks can be placed in a "WaitingExternal" state (e.g., waiting for AI responses or external events); timeouts in this state are enforced.
- **Dataflow and Output Injection**: Automatically injects upstream task outputs (including structured AI outputs) into downstream inputs.
- **Graceful Shutdown**: Ensures all active operations are cancelled and no further work is dispatched on stop.
- **Thread Synchronization**: All access to mutable state is protected by mutexes.

---

## Typical Usage Pattern

1. Instantiate `WorkflowRuntimeManager`.
2. Set the registry via `SetRegistry()`.
3. Call `Start()`.
4. Enqueue workflow runs with `EnqueueWorkflowRun...` methods.
5. Periodically call `Update()` from the main thread (e.g., in an agent tick loop).
6. Query run status, control runs, retrieve run/job info as needed.
7. Call `Stop()`/`SignalStop()`/`WaitStop()` during shutdown.

---

## Example Usage Snippet

```cpp
WorkflowRuntimeManager runtime;
runtime.SetRegistry(&workflowRegistry);
runtime.Start();

std::string runId = runtime.EnqueueWorkflowRunAndGetRunId("build_and_test_workflow");

// In the main application loop:
while (running) {
    bool changed = runtime.Update(); // call on each agent tick
    // handle events...
}

// During shutdown:
runtime.Stop();
```

---

## Important Internal Data Structures

- **ActiveRun**: Holds WorkflowDefinition, current WorkflowRun state, in-flight task futures, and controlflow tracking.
- **PendingRun**: Holds workflowId, runId, context for pending runs.
- **TaskExecutionResult**: Contains per-task status/result info for worker thread completions.
- **FilterEvalResult**: Contains per-item filter outcomes (for per_item fan-out tasks).
- **SubWorkflowLink**: Records parent-child relationships for propagating subworkflow completions.

---

## Error Handling

- If an error occurs (e.g., missing workflow, no AI provider, task failure), the workflow run is marked as failed.
- Terminal runs are kept visible for a brief period to allow UI/UI snapshot reporting.
- Errors and info are logged via the logging subsystem.

---

## Dependencies

- Requires integration with:
    - `WorkflowRegistry` (for workflow definitions)
    - `AiRequestPool` (for ai_call tasks)
    - Thread pool (`ThreadPool` via Core object)
    - Logging, file I/O, and filter evaluation modules

---

## License

MIT License (see header in source code).

---

## See Also

- `WorkflowRegistry`
- `WorkflowRun`
- `TaskInstanceState`
- `AiRequestPool`
- `FilterEngine`

---

## Summary

`WorkflowRuntimeManager` provides comprehensive lifecycle control, dispatch, monitoring, and artifact management for running, pausing, stopping, and cleaning up complex, multi-task workflows, including support for per-item fan-out and conditional control flow, in a thread-safe, non-blocking, and integration-friendly way. Use this class to run and manage workflows efficiently in an AI assistant or orchestration system.


---

### 101_doc_application_workflow_workflowTriggerBinder_h

<a id="101-doc-application-workflow-workflowtriggerbinder-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/101_doc_application_workflow_workflowTriggerBinder_h/docu.output.md`



# WorkflowTriggerBinder (C++ Class) Documentation

## Overview

The `AIAssistant::WorkflowTriggerBinder` class binds workflow triggers from a registry to a trigger engine. It automates the registration of various trigger types for workflows, enabling automated or manual execution of workflows based on defined trigger conditions.

---

## Class Declaration

```cpp
namespace AIAssistant {
    class WorkflowRegistry;
    class TriggerEngine;

    class WorkflowTriggerBinder {
    public:
        WorkflowTriggerBinder() = default;
        ~WorkflowTriggerBinder() = default;

        void RegisterAll(
            WorkflowRegistry const& workflowRegistry,
            TriggerEngine& triggerEngine,
            bool fireAutoTriggers = true
        ) const;
    };
}
```

---

## Public Methods

### WorkflowTriggerBinder()

- Default constructor.

### ~WorkflowTriggerBinder()

- Default destructor.

### void RegisterAll(const WorkflowRegistry& workflowRegistry, TriggerEngine& triggerEngine, bool fireAutoTriggers = true) const

**Purpose:**  
Registers all triggers for every workflow in the provided registry with the given trigger engine.

**Parameters:**
- `workflowRegistry`: Reference to a `WorkflowRegistry` containing all workflow definitions.
- `triggerEngine`: Reference to a `TriggerEngine` where triggers are registered.
- `fireAutoTriggers` (optional, default: `true`): Whether to immediately fire auto-triggers as they are registered.

**Behavior:**
- Iterates over all workflows in `workflowRegistry`.
- Skips sub-workflows (triggered internally).
- Determines trigger types in each workflow and registers them with `triggerEngine` using the appropriate parameters:
    - **Auto:** Registers auto-run triggers.
    - **Cron:** Parses cron parameters, registers scheduled triggers.
    - **FileWatch:** Parses file event triggers, including path, event types, and debounce period.
    - **Manual:** Registers manual triggers for user invocation.
    - **Webhook:** Registers webhook triggers, optionally with a secret.
    - **S3Watch / AzureBlobWatch / GcsWatch / OneDriveWatch / EmailWatch:** Registers cloud resource triggers with connection, folder/bucket/container, and polling interval.
    - **Structure:** No runtime trigger is registered (for per-item expansions).
    - **Unknown:** Issues a warning.

- Performs per-trigger-type parameter extraction and validation (e.g., parsing cron, file watch, and webhook parameter JSON).
- Errors and warnings are logged as appropriate.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::WorkflowRegistry registry = ...;
AIAssistant::TriggerEngine engine = ...;
AIAssistant::WorkflowTriggerBinder binder;

binder.RegisterAll(registry, engine); // Registers triggers, firing auto-triggers by default
```

---

## Notes

- This class does not maintain any state; it only provides a utility function for batch trigger registration.
- Intended for initializing and synchronizing a trigger engine with all defined workflows and their triggers.
- Error handling and logging are provided for invalid or incomplete trigger definitions.

---

## Dependencies

- Requires `WorkflowRegistry`, `TriggerEngine`, and related trigger/definition types.
- Relies on JSON parsing (via simdjson) for parameter extraction.

---

## License

This class is licensed under GPL-3.0 (see source for full license text).


---

### 102_doc_application_workflow_workflowTypes_h

<a id="102-doc-application-workflow-workflowtypes-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/102_doc_application_workflow_workflowTypes_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant Workflow & Task Structures

## Overview

This header defines core types and structures for workflow/task orchestration in the AIAssistant system. It supports workflows, tasks, context management, IO definitions, triggers, control flow, runtime/task states, and related orchestration features. All core types are thread-safe and suitable for concurrent execution and cloud-based task management.

---

## Key Structures

### 1. `TaskWatchdog`
- Monitors inactivity timeouts for running tasks.
- Methods:
  - `Kick()` — Updates last activity timestamp.
  - `ElapsedSinceLastKickMs()` — Returns ms elapsed since last activity.
- Thread-safe via atomic operations.

---

### 2. Enums

#### Workflow Triggers
- `WorkflowTriggerType`: Trigger kinds (Auto, Cron, FileWatch, etc.).
- `WorkflowRunState`: Overall run state (Pending, Running, Succeeded, etc.).
- `TaskType`: Task implementation type (Python, Shell, AiCall, etc.).
- `TaskMode`: Single vs. per-item task execution.
- `TaskInstanceStateKind`: Per-task instance state (Pending, Running, WaitingExternal, etc.).
- `ControlNodeType`, `ControlflowKind`: Control-flow constructs.

---

### 3. Context & IO

#### Context
- `ContextValue`: Wrapper for string values (may store JSON).
- `ContextMap`: Maps string keys to `ContextValue`.

#### Task IO
- `TaskIOField`: Metadata for a task input/output field.
  - Type, required flag, default value.
- `TaskIOMap`: Maps field names to `TaskIOField`.

---

### 4. Environment, Queue, and File Bindings

#### TaskEnvironment
- Logical environment name, assistant ID, environment variables.

#### QueueFileRef
- Represents a file reference (path or inline content).
- Can be used as a string (path).

#### QueueBinding
- Lists of files for task configuration (settings, instructions, context, provider, problem files).

---

### 5. Workflow Triggers & Dataflow

- `WorkflowTrigger`: Defines a trigger (type, id, enabled flag, params JSON).
- `DataflowDef`: Dataflow edge (source task/output to target task/input, with optional mapping).

---

### 6. Filters

- `FilterSource`: Defines a data source for filtering (csv, text, query, polarion_query).
- `FilterDef`: Filter specification (id, source, binding name, item limit).

---

### 7. Task Definition

- `RetryPolicy`: Max attempts, backoff timing.
- `TaskDef` (alias `TaskDefinition`): Captures full static schema for a task:
  - ID, type, mode, label, doc, dependencies, directories.
  - File inputs/outputs, environment, queue bindings.
  - Input/output models, timeouts, retries, raw params JSON.
  - Filtering, error signaling, subworkflow files, output schema, schema retry limits.

---

### 8. Workflow-Level Defaults

- `WorkflowDefaults`: Default timeout and retry policy for tasks without overrides.

---

### 9. Control-Flow Graph

- `ControlNodeDef`: Orchestration node (branches, etc.).
- `ControlflowEdgeDef`: Edge definition for control flow (from/to node, kind, ports).

---

### 10. WorkflowDefinition

- Static configuration for a workflow:
  - Version, id, label, doc.
  - File paths/directories (runtime context for loading).
  - Triggers, tasks, dataflows, filters, control nodes/edges.
  - Defaults, container-related info (for zipped workflows).
  - Flags and lists for ai_call presence and provider requirements.

---

### 11. Runtime State Structures

#### TaskInstanceState
- Captures current execution state for an individual task instance.
- Tracks attempts, errors, stdout/stderr captured output, timestamps, resolved inputs/outputs, runtime metadata, and watchdog.

#### WorkflowRun
- Represents a live run of a workflow:
  - Run/workflow ID, state.
  - Shared context map.
  - Per-task instance runtime state.
  - Run timings.
  - Completion/failure flags.
  - Shared cancellation token.

---

## Thread Safety

Most structures managing mutability use atomic/shared_ptr or are intended for ephemeral (per-run) or config-time (per-load) access patterns.

---

## Usage

- These types are for internal orchestration and cloud task management in the AIAssistant framework.
- Suitable for complex workflows involving user/AI cooperation, multiple task types, dataflow, branching, and external triggers.

---

## License

GPL-3.0. See header notice for full terms.

---

## Namespace

All symbols are under `AIAssistant`.


---

### 103_doc_application_workflow_workflowValidator_h

<a id="103-doc-application-workflow-workflowvalidator-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/103_doc_application_workflow_workflowValidator_h/docu.output.md`



# Documentation for `AIAssistant::WorkflowValidator`

## Overview

The `WorkflowValidator` class provides static methods for validating workflow definitions and related files, scripts, and dependencies. It collects and reports errors, warnings, and other issues found in workflow files, allowing both humans and AI tools to automatically check and suggest fixes.

Validation includes structure checks, runtime policy compliance, file reachability, script existence and content heuristics, dependency/cycle checks, and common best-practices for JCWF workflows. Issues are attached with severity, explanation, location, and, when possible, auto-fix guidance.

---

## Key Types

- **WorkflowValidationSeverity**:  
  - `Error`, `Warning`, `Info`
  - Indicates the seriousness of a validation issue.
- **WorkflowValidationTier**:  
  - A, B, C, D (A=critical, D=best-practices)
  - Indicates the category/importance of the issue.
- **WorkflowValidationIssue**:  
  - Contains `severity`, `tier`, issue `code`, `message`, location `path`, optional `taskId`, optional `suggestedFix`, and extra `context`.
- **GeneratedScript**:  
  - Represents a script (path, source content, and executability information).

---

## Public Interface

### Static Methods

#### 1. `void Validate(const WorkflowDefinition&, std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>& issues)`
- Validates a `WorkflowDefinition`.
- Collects all structural and policy issues (Tiers A & B) into `issues`.
- To be used for basic validation.

#### 2. 
```cpp
void Validate(
    const WorkflowDefinition&,
    const ScriptRegistry* scriptRegistry,
    const std::vector<GeneratedScript>* pendingScripts,
    std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>& issues,
    const WorkflowFileIndex* workflowFileIndex = nullptr
)
```
- Extends validation with additional runtime and file-aware checks (Tiers B, C, D).
- Checks:
  - Script/module presence and content.
  - Pending, generated, or missing scripts.
  - Python module/policy compliance.
  - Shell/Python script headers, metadata markers.
  - Reachability of files and dataflow.
  - Suggested fixes for issues, where possible.
- Use when you have script registry and/or listed generated scripts for full editor or AI tooling scenarios.

#### 3. `bool HasErrors(const std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>& issues)`
- Returns true if any `Error`-severity validation issues are present.

#### 4. 
```cpp
void ValidateSubWorkflowGraph(
    const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::vector<std::string>>& dependencyGraph,
    std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>& issues,
    uint32_t maxDepth = 10
)
```
- Checks sub-workflow dependency graph for cycles and excessive nesting depth.
- Adds Tier-A error issues for any cycles or depth exceeded.

---

## Validation Tiers & Logic

- **Tier A**: Structural correctness (required fields, id uniqueness, dependency cycles).
- **Tier B**: Runtime policy compliance (script path rules, script existence, module/function, script contents, parameters).
- **Tier C**: Best practices and reachability (working directory conventions, file_outputs placement, file_inputs existence/reachability, cntx_files reachability).
- **Tier D**: Advanced analysis (cross-task file chains, output collisions).

Each tier adds more context-sensitive and project-convention checks.

---

## Issue Reporting

Each issue reported includes:
- **Severity**: Error, Warning, or Info
- **Tier**: Estimated category/importance
- **Code**: Short machine-friendly code
- **Message**: Human-friendly problem description
- **Path**: JSON path or location string for issue source
- **TaskId**: If applicable, which task triggered issue
- **SuggestedFix**: AI-friendly and human-friendly auto-fix suggestion
- **Context**: Extra context for automated tools or UI

---

## Policy and Convention Checks

- **Script paths**: Shell/Python scripts must be under `scripts/` directory.
- **ID validation**: Only alphanumeric, `_`, `-`, and `.` are allowed.
- **File inputs/outputs**: Must resolve inside allowed directories and ideally be produced/consumed by other tasks.
- **Script content**: Checks for standard headers, `@jarvis-script`, `@short:` metadata, and shell/Python conventions (`shebang`, `pipefail`, `Set-StrictMode` for PowerShell).
- **Python modules**: Must start with `scripts.`, must exist as `.py` files, and contain required `function`.
- **AiCall tasks**: Checks around STNG content, file_output placement, and cntx_files reachability.

---

## Usage

- Call `Validate()` on a workflow, collecting issues.
- Use `HasErrors()` to determine if issues prevent execution.
- Use the extended `Validate()` to integrate with script/asset registries and generated code.
- Use `ValidateSubWorkflowGraph()` when working with nested or multi-workflow scenarios.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue> issues;
AIAssistant::WorkflowValidator::Validate(workflowDefinition, issues);
if (AIAssistant::WorkflowValidator::HasErrors(issues)) {
    // Handle errors
}
```

---

## Thread Safety / State

All methods are static and stateless. No global state is modified except access to paths for runtime checks (may depend on workflow engine directory).

---

## Dependencies

- Requires workflow, task, script, and file index types (`WorkflowDefinition`, `ScriptRegistry`, `GeneratedScript`, `WorkflowFileIndex`).
- Uses [simdjson](https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson) for fast JSON parsing.
- Uses `<filesystem>`, `<unordered_map>`, and standard STL.

---

## Licensing

- Copyright (c) 2025 JC Technolabs
- Licensed under GPL-3.0

---

## Summary

`WorkflowValidator` enables rigorous, automated validation of workflow definitions (“JCWF”). It checks for completeness, correctness, runtime safety, best-practice adherence, and gives actionable diagnostics for fixing problems in workflows and scripts. Designed for both human-machine interaction and automated AI validation pipelines.


---

### 104_doc_engine_auxiliary_file_h

<a id="104-doc-engine-auxiliary-file-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/104_doc_engine_auxiliary_file_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::EngineCore File Utility Functions

This namespace provides a collection of utility functions for file and directory operations. These functions support common file system tasks such as checking file existence, directory manipulation, extracting file/path information, and file copying. Cross-platform support is provided (POSIX, Windows).

## Functions

### File Existence and Directory Checks

- **bool FileExists(const char* filename)**
  - Returns true if the file exists at the specified C-string path.

- **bool FileExists(const std::string& filename)**
  - Returns true if the file exists at the specified path.

- **bool FileExists(const std::filesystem::directory_entry& filename)**
  - Returns true if the directory entry exists in the file system.

- **bool IsDirectory(const char* filename)**
  - Returns true if the given C-string path is an existing directory.

- **bool IsDirectory(const std::string& filename)**
  - Returns true if the given string path is an existing directory.

### File/Path Name Manipulation

- **std::string GetFilenameWithoutPath(const std::filesystem::path& path)**
  - Returns the filename without its preceding directory path.

- **std::string GetPathWithoutFilename(const std::filesystem::path& path)**
  - Returns the directory path containing the file, ending with a slash.

- **std::string GetFilenameWithoutExtension(const std::filesystem::path& path)**
  - Returns the filename without its extension.

- **std::string GetFilenameWithoutPathAndExtension(const std::filesystem::path& path)**
  - Returns the filename with both the directory path and extension removed.

- **std::string GetFileExtension(const std::filesystem::path& path)**
  - Returns the file extension, including the leading dot.

### Working Directory

- **std::string GetCurrentWorkingDirectory()**
  - Returns the current working directory as a string.

- **void SetCurrentWorkingDirectory(const std::filesystem::path& path)**
  - Sets the process’ current working directory.

### File and Directory Operations

- **bool CreateDirectory(const std::string& filename)**
  - Creates directories for the specified path. Returns true if successful.

- **bool CopyFile(const std::string& src, const std::string& dest)**
  - Copies a file from `src` to `dest`. Returns true on success.

- **std::ifstream::pos_type FileSize(const std::string& filename)**
  - Returns the size of the specified file in bytes.

- **std::string& AddSlash(std::string& filename)**
  - Appends a path separator (slash or backslash) to `filename` if not already present.

### File Timestamps

- **fs::file_time_type GetNewestTimestamp(const std::vector<fs::path>& files)**
  - Returns the most recent last-write timestamp from a vector of paths.

## Usage

These functions are grouped within:

```
namespace AIAssistant {
    namespace EngineCore {
        /* functions */
    }
}
```

They are suitable for projects needing lightweight, dependency-free file and directory utilities in C++.

## Notes

- Cross-platform support: conditional code exists for Windows vs. POSIX path and directory handling.
- Exceptions during file operations are caught and handled silently, returning default or false values.
- Most functions use `std::filesystem` for path manipulations.
- The file is released under the MIT License.

### 105_doc_engine_auxiliary_threadPool_h

<a id="105-doc-engine-auxiliary-threadpool-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/105_doc_engine_auxiliary_threadPool_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::ThreadPool

AIAssistant::ThreadPool is a thread pool manager that provides an interface to submit, manage, and control concurrent tasks using an underlying `BS::thread_pool` implementation.

## Overview

- Manages a pool of worker threads for parallel task execution.
- Provides lifecycle controls: wait, stop, shutdown, and reset.
- Thread-safe task submission.
- Compatible with C++ futures for result retrieval.

## Public Methods

### Constructor

**ThreadPool()**
- Initializes the thread pool.

### Wait

**void Wait()**
- Blocks until all queued and running tasks are complete.
- Logs thread and queue info before and after waiting.

### RequestStop

**void RequestStop()**
- Signals the thread pool to stop accepting new tasks.
- Does not immediately terminate running tasks.

### Shutdown

**void Shutdown()**
- Marks the pool as stopped and blocks until all running and queued tasks finish.
- No new tasks can be accepted after calling.

### Reset

**void Reset(size_t numThreads)**
- Resets the pool to use the specified number of threads.
- Useful for scaling the pool at runtime.

### Size

**size_t Size() const**
- Returns the number of threads currently assigned to the pool.

### IsStopped

**bool IsStopped() const**
- Returns `true` if `RequestStop()` or `Shutdown()` have been called.

### SubmitTask

**template <typename FunctionType, typename ReturnType = std::invoke_result_t<std::decay_t<FunctionType>>>  
std::future<ReturnType> SubmitTask(FunctionType&& task)**
- Submits a callable to be executed by the thread pool.
- Returns a `std::future<ReturnType>` for result retrieval.
- If called after shutdown, returns an already-resolved default value.
- Thread-safe.

### GetThreadIDs

**std::vector<std::thread::id> GetThreadIDs() const**
- Returns a list of thread IDs currently running in the pool.

## Private Members

- `BS::thread_pool<> m_Pool;` — Underlying thread pool.
- `std::mutex m_Mutex;` — Guards access to internal state, mainly for task submission.
- `std::atomic<bool> m_Stopped{false};` — Indicates if the pool has been stopped.

## Usage

1. Create a `ThreadPool` instance.
2. Submit work using `SubmitTask()`.
3. Use `Wait()`, `RequestStop()`, or `Shutdown()` for lifecycle management.
4. Optionally, resize the pool with `Reset()`.

## Notes

- After calling `Shutdown()` or `RequestStop()`, new tasks are rejected.
- Designed to be thread-safe.
- Requires the [BS::thread_pool](https://github.com/bshoshany/thread-pool) library.

## License

MIT License (see source code).

### 106_doc_engine_core_h
