# Documentation: `AIAssistant::Core` Class

## Overview

The `AIAssistant::Core` class is the central manager for application lifecycle, event processing, configuration, logging, input handling, threading, and cloud integration within the AIAssistant framework. It initializes, runs, and shuts down components required by an AI assistant or agent application.

---

## Key Responsibilities

- Application initialization, running, and orderly shutdown
- Event queue management and event dispatch
- Logging and terminal management (including log file and console redirection)
- Thread pool and background task management
- Keyboard input capture and processing
- Key and OAuth token management
- Cloud connection and integration management
- Configuration loading and runtime access

---

## Main Methods

| Method / API                                                         | Description                                                                                                                                      |
|-----------------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `Core()`                                                             | Constructor. Initializes paths, logging, terminal, and signal handlers.                                                                          |
| `~Core()`                                                            | Destructor. Cleans up and restores original console stream buffers.                                                                               |
| `void Start(const EngineConfig&)`                                     | Initializes core systems and subsystems (thread pool, keyboard input, OAuth manager) using the provided configuration.                            |
| `void Run(std::unique_ptr<Application>& app)`                        | Main execution loop: event dispatch, application update, and terminal rendering. Handles shutdown signals.                                        |
| `void Shutdown()`                                                    | Cleanly shuts down all systems in dependency-safe order (OAuth, input, threads, logging, terminal, log file).                                     |
| `void SignalShutdown()`                                              | Initiates shutdown procedure asynchronously and signals components to stop.                                                                       |
| `bool IsShuttingDown() const`                                        | Returns true if the system is shutting down.                                                                                                     |
| `void RequestQuit()`                                                 | Sets quit requested flag; triggers shutdown on next check.                                                                                       |
| `bool IsQuitRequested() const`                                       | Returns true if a quit has been requested.                                                                                                       |
| `bool Verbose() const`                                               | Returns whether verbose logging is enabled from config.                                                                                          |
| `const EngineConfig& GetConfig() const`                              | Returns current engine configuration (const).                                                                                                    |
| `EngineConfig& GetMutableConfig()`                                   | Returns mutable engine configuration.                                                                                                            |
| `const InterfaceType& GetInterfaceType() const`                      | Returns the selected API interface type (from config).                                                                                           |
| `void SetConfigFilePath(const std::filesystem::path&)`               | Sets the configuration file path.                                                                                                                |
| `const std::filesystem::path& GetConfigFilePath() const`             | Gets the configuration file path.                                                                                                                |
| `ThreadPool& GetThreadPool()`                                        | Access to the thread pool for background tasks.                                                                                                  |
| `TerminalManager* GetTerminalManager()`                              | Access to the terminal UI manager.                                                                                                               |
| `TerminalLogStreamBuf* GetTerminalLogStreamBuf()`                    | Access to the terminal logging stream buffer.                                                                                                    |
| `KeyManager& GetKeyManager()` / `const KeyManager& GetKeyManager()`  | Access to key management for API/service keys.                                                                                                   |
| `CloudConnectionManager& GetCloudConnectionManager()`                | Access to cloud connection management.                                                                                                           |
| `CloudConnectorRegistry& GetCloudConnectorRegistry()`                | Access to registry of cloud service connectors.                                                                                                  |
| `CloudCircuitBreaker& GetCloudCircuitBreaker()`                      | Access to cloud error-handling circuit breaker.                                                                                                  |
| `OAuthTokenManager& GetOAuthTokenManager()` / `const ...`            | Access to OAuth token management for cloud APIs.                                                                                                 |
| `const std::filesystem::path& GetLaunchCWDAbsolute() const`          | Returns absolute path to initial working directory.                                                                                              |
| `void PushEvent(EventQueue::EventPtr)`                               | Add an event to the asynchronous event queue.                                                                                                    |

---

## Static Members

- `static std::unique_ptr<Log> g_Logger;` — Global logger pointer for system-wide logging.
- `static Core* g_Core;` — Global (singleton-style) pointer to running Core instance.

---

## Event Handling

- Uses an internal `EventQueue` for asynchronous event passing.
- Handles both engine-level events (such as errors and shutdown) and dispatches application-specific events.
- Signal handlers integrate with event processing to catch Ctrl+C and initiate clean shutdown or forced exit.

---

## Logging & Terminal Output

- Redirects `std::cout` and `std::cerr` to a custom terminal/log buffer and a log file.
- Log file path: `log/log.txt`. Logging continues until shutdown, then all outputs are flushed and devices restored.
- Supports verbose mode for detailed logging.

---

## Threading

- Internal `ThreadPool` supports offloading background tasks.
- Number of threads determined by configuration + required threads for internal components (file watcher, input, etc.).
- Provides mechanism to request thread pool shutdown and check status.

---

## Cloud and Key Management

- `KeyManager`: Handles storage and retrieval of API keys.
- `OAuthTokenManager`: Manages OAuth tokens for cloud API authentication.
- `CloudConnectionManager`: Manages lifecycle of cloud service connections.
- `CloudConnectorRegistry`: Maintains registry of available cloud service connectors.
- `CloudCircuitBreaker`: Handles error limiting for cloud service calls.

---

## Keyboard Input

- `KeyboardInput` is launched during startup and stopped on shutdown, allowing real-time command or shortcut input during execution.

---

## Configuration

- Holds a parsed `EngineConfig` object (from the config parser).
- Allows mutable or const access to configuration during runtime.
- Stores the path (if set) to the configuration file in use.

---

## Signal Handling

- Handles SIGINT (Ctrl+C) gracefully: first triggers shutdown, second triggers forced exit.
- Ensures terminal echo for signals is controlled/restored.

---

## Usage Example (Summary)

```cpp
AIAssistant::Core core;
core.Start(config);
core.Run(app);
// Signal to request quit or called internally on SIGINT
core.SignalShutdown();
core.Shutdown();
```

---

## Thread Safety

- Uses atomic flags for shutdown and quit requests.
- Signal handler implementation avoids non-signal-safe operations.

---

## Summary Table: Major Members

| Member                         | Type                               | Purpose                                              |
|---------------------------------|------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|
| m_ThreadPool                   | ThreadPool                         | Handles async and background tasks                   |
| m_EventQueue                   | EventQueue                         | Event/message bus for engine and app events          |
| m_EngineConfig                 | EngineConfig                       | Main runtime configuration                           |
| m_TerminalManager              | std::unique_ptr<TerminalManager>   | Terminal UI and interaction                          |
| m_TerminalBuf                  | std::unique_ptr<TerminalLogStreamBuf> | Stream buffer for logging output                     |
| m_KeyboardInput                | std::unique_ptr<KeyboardInput>     | Keyboard event capture                               |
| m_KeyManager                   | KeyManager                         | API/access key management                            |
| m_OAuthTokenManager            | OAuthTokenManager                  | Cloud API OAuth token management                     |
| m_CloudConnectionManager       | CloudConnectionManager             | Cloud session/connection management                  |
| m_CloudConnectorRegistry       | CloudConnectorRegistry             | Registry of cloud connectors                         |
| m_CloudCircuitBreaker          | CloudCircuitBreaker                | Error management for cloud interactions              |
| m_ShuttingDown                 | std::atomic<bool>                  | Async shutdown flag                                  |
| m_QuitRequested                | std::atomic<bool>                  | Quit/exit requested flag                             |
| m_LaunchCWDAbsolute            | std::filesystem::path              | Launch working directory                             |
| m_ConfigFilePath               | std::filesystem::path              | Path to configuration file                           |

---

## Notes

- Designed for cross-platform use (POSIX and Windows terminal/console handling).
- All dependencies (event queue, logging, threading, cloud, config) are initialized and owned by `Core`.
- Not copyable or movable (due to ownership of resources and global state).

---

## License

MIT License – See source for full license terms.

### 107_doc_engine_curlWrapper_authSigner_h

<a id="107-doc-engine-curlwrapper-authsigner-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/107_doc_engine_curlWrapper_authSigner_h/docu.output.md`



# IAuthSigner C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

The `IAuthSigner` class provides an interface for various HTTP request authentication strategies. It is designed to enable different authentication methods (such as Bearer tokens, API keys, AWS SigV4) to be applied polymorphically when making HTTP requests via the `CurlWrapper`. This allows easy extension and consistent usage of new authentication styles across different parts of the application without exposing implementation details.

## Namespace

`AIAssistant`

## Header

```cpp
#include "curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h"
#include <string>
#include <vector>
```

## Usage

`IAuthSigner` is not instantiated directly. Instead, use the static `Get` method to obtain a reference to an auth signer appropriate for a specific `AuthStyle`. Then call its `Apply` method to append the required headers to an outgoing request.

## Interface

```cpp
class IAuthSigner
{
public:
    virtual ~IAuthSigner() = default;
    virtual void Apply(CurlWrapper::QueryData const& queryData, std::vector<std::string>& outHeaders) = 0;
    static IAuthSigner& Get(CurlWrapper::AuthStyle style);
};
```

### Methods

#### `virtual ~IAuthSigner() = default;`

Destructor. Ensures proper cleanup in derived classes.

#### `virtual void Apply(CurlWrapper::QueryData const& queryData, std::vector<std::string>& outHeaders) = 0;`

Pure virtual method. Concrete implementations use this function to append authentication headers to the `outHeaders` vector, based on the data in `queryData`.

- `queryData`: Input structure containing request and credential info.
- `outHeaders`: Output vector to append string headers in the form `"Name: value"`.

#### `static IAuthSigner& Get(CurlWrapper::AuthStyle style);`

Factory-like static method. Returns a reference to a singleton instance of the appropriate concrete `IAuthSigner` based on the specified authentication style.

- `style`: Enum specifying which authentication scheme to use.

  - `CurlWrapper::AuthStyle::Bearer` – Bearer token in `Authorization` header.
  - `CurlWrapper::AuthStyle::XGoogApiKey` – API key in `x-goog-api-key` header.
  - `CurlWrapper::AuthStyle::AnthropicXApiKey` – API key in `x-api-key` header and fixed `anthropic-version` header.
  - `CurlWrapper::AuthStyle::AzureApiKey` – API key in `api-key` header.
  - `CurlWrapper::AuthStyle::AwsSigV4` – AWS SigV4 signature (external class, not fully shown here).

## Concrete Implementations

Defined as internal classes:

- **BearerSigner**: Adds `"Authorization: Bearer {ApiKey}"`
- **XGoogApiKeySigner**: Adds `"x-goog-api-key: {ApiKey}"`
- **AnthropicXApiKeySigner**: Adds `"x-api-key: {ApiKey}"` and `"anthropic-version: 2023-06-01"`
- **AzureApiKeySigner**: Adds `"api-key: {ApiKey}"`
- **SigV4Signer**: For AWS SigV4 signing; details not provided here.

## Example

```cpp
using namespace AIAssistant;

std::vector<std::string> headers;
auto& signer = IAuthSigner::Get(CurlWrapper::AuthStyle::Bearer);
signer.Apply(queryData, headers);
// headers now contains the proper Authorization header
```

## Thread Safety

The returned signers are statically allocated singleton instances.

## Notes

- The system is extendable for new authentication styles by adding new implementations.
- Concrete signers expect the API key and other details to be present in `CurlWrapper::QueryData`.
- This design abstracts credential injection for HTTP requests, simplifying code maintenance and extension.

## License

MIT License (see source code header).


---

### 108_doc_engine_curlWrapper_awsSigV4_h

<a id="108-doc-engine-curlwrapper-awssigv4-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/108_doc_engine_curlWrapper_awsSigV4_h/docu.output.md`



# Documentation: `AIAssistant::SigV4Signer`

## Overview

`SigV4Signer` is a C++ implementation of [AWS Signature Version 4](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/general/latest/gr/sigv4_signing.html) request signing, based on OpenSSL HMAC-SHA256/SHA256. It provides AWS request authentication headers for HTTP(S) requests, primarily POST, supporting use cases such as AWS Bedrock and other AWS services.

The class implements the `IAuthSigner` interface, and can be used as a plug-in for HTTP client frameworks that require AWS SigV4 authentication.

---

## Public Types

### `struct Inputs`
Groups all fields required to produce AWS SigV4 signed headers.
- `std::string m_Method` - HTTP method, e.g., "POST".
- `std::string m_Url` - Full request URL.
- `std::string m_Body` - Request payload (may be empty).
- `std::string m_AccessKey` - AWS access key ID.
- `std::string m_SecretKey` - AWS secret access key.
- `std::string m_SessionToken` - Optional STS session token.
- `std::string m_Region` - AWS region, e.g., "us-east-1".
- `std::string m_Service` - AWS service name, e.g., "bedrock".
- `std::string m_AmzDate` - ISO8601 basic format (`YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ`). If empty, current UTC is used.

### `struct SignedHeaders`
Result of signing a request — ready-to-use headers for an HTTP request.
- `std::string m_Host` - Canonical host header.
- `std::string m_AmzDate` - Amz date header.
- `std::string m_ContentSha256` - SHA256 hash of the request payload.
- `std::string m_Authorization` - Value for the `Authorization` header.
- `std::string m_SecurityToken` - Value for the `X-Amz-Security-Token` header (empty if no session token).

---

## Public Methods

### `void Apply(const CurlWrapper::QueryData& queryData, std::vector<std::string>& outHeaders) override`
Generates and appends SigV4 authentication headers to `outHeaders` for the given request. Required fields (access key, secret key, region) must be provided in `queryData.m_ApiKey` and `queryData.m_Params`. If missing, outputs a placeholder Authorization header and logs an error.

**Used for:** Integrating AWS SigV4 authentication into HTTP request flows.

### `static SignedHeaders Sign(const Inputs& in)`
Pure deterministic function that computes SigV4-signed headers for fixed input values. Used for production signing and for unit testing against AWS test vectors.

**Input:** Values in an `Inputs` struct.  
**Output:** Populated `SignedHeaders` struct.

### `static bool RunSelfTest()`
Runs internal tests to verify hash computation, signing-key derivation, and signing determinism. Logs any failures and returns `true` if all tests pass.

**Intended for:** Validating implementation correctness at build or startup time.

---

## Algorithms and Key Behavior

- Uses canonical request construction, header ordering, payload hash, and derived HMAC signing key as described in AWS documentation.
- Supports session tokens for STS temporary credentials.
- Produces all required signature headers: `Authorization`, `X-Amz-Date`, `X-Amz-Content-Sha256`, `Host`, and `X-Amz-Security-Token` (if needed).
- Handles full URLs with optional query parameters.
- Ensures deterministic output for fixed inputs, enabling known-answer tests and reproducibility.
- If credentials or required parameters are missing, logs a detailed error.

---

## Integration Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::SigV4Signer signer;
std::vector<std::string> headers;
signer.Apply(queryData, headers);
// Use 'headers' in your HTTP request.
```

---

## Error Handling

- Logs errors if any required parameter is missing.
- Outputs minimal headers (with `MISSING-CREDENTIALS`) if signing cannot be performed.
- Self-test logs on failure and aborts early.

---

## Dependencies

- OpenSSL (`libssl`/`libcrypto`) for SHA-256 and HMAC routines.
- Standard C++11 and above.

---

## Testing

- Includes built-in self-test for hash output, AWS-known vector key derivation, and determinism.
- Designed for easy unit testing with deterministic inputs.

---

## Notes

- Only supports AWS Signature Version 4.
- Designed for use with AWS HTTP APIs (REST/JSON/other).
- For questions, refer to AWS documentation or the AWS SigV4 process.

---


---

### 109_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlManager_h

# CurlManager Class Documentation

## Overview

The `CurlManager` class in the `AIAssistant` namespace provides thread-local management of `CurlWrapper` instances. It ensures each thread gets its own `CurlWrapper`, initialized on first access and destroyed when the thread exits.

## Usage

Use `CurlManager::GetThreadCurl()` to get a reference to the `CurlWrapper` instance specific to the current thread.

## Public Static Methods

### `static CurlWrapper& GetThreadCurl()`

- Returns a reference to a thread-local `CurlWrapper` instance.
- Each thread calling this function receives its own unique `CurlWrapper`, which persists for the life of the thread.
- Ensures thread safety for CURL operations by avoiding shared state between threads.

## Thread Safety

- The `CurlWrapper` instance is stored as `thread_local`, guaranteeing isolation between threads.

## Dependencies

- Requires inclusion of `curlWrapper/curlWrapper.h`.
- Depends on a `CurlWrapper` class or structure defined in the included header.

## Example

```cpp
#include "curlManager.h"

void someThreadFunction() {
    auto& curl = AIAssistant::CurlManager::GetThreadCurl();
    // Use 'curl' for CURL operations in this thread
}
```

## License

MIT License. See source file header for full license text.

### 10_doc_application_cloud_azureSharedKeySigner_h

<a id="10-doc-application-cloud-azuresharedkeysigner-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/10_doc_application_cloud_azureSharedKeySigner_h/docu.output.md`



# AzureSharedKeySigner

## Overview

`AzureSharedKeySigner` is a C++ class for creating Azure Storage "Shared Key" signatures, producing the necessary HTTP headers to authenticate requests to the Azure Blob Storage REST API using HMAC-SHA256 with OpenSSL.

## Namespace

```
AIAssistant
```

## Purpose

- Generates compliant Azure Shared Key request signatures.
- Produces `Authorization`, `x-ms-date`, and `x-ms-version` headers required for Azure Blob Storage requests.

## Main Interface

### `SignedRequest` Struct

- `m_Headers` (`std::map<std::string, std::string>`): The headers to add to the HTTP request.

### `static SignedRequest Sign(...)`

Signs an HTTP request for Azure Blob Storage.

#### Parameters

- **method**: `const std::string&` — HTTP method (e.g., `GET`, `PUT`, `DELETE`, `HEAD`)
- **url**: `const std::string&` — Full Azure Blob Storage URL
- **accountName**: `const std::string&` — Azure Storage account name
- **accountKey**: `const std::string&` — Azure Storage account key (Base64-encoded)
- **extraHeaders**: `const std::map<std::string, std::string>&` (*optional*) — Additional headers (e.g., `x-ms-blob-type`, `Content-Type`)
- **contentLength**: `size_t` (*optional*) — Value for `Content-Length` (0 for `GET`, `DELETE`, `HEAD`)

#### Returns

- `SignedRequest`: Holds the `Authorization`, `x-ms-date`, and `x-ms-version` headers for the HTTP request.

#### Usage Example

```cpp
auto signedReq = AzureSharedKeySigner::Sign(
    "PUT",
    "https://myaccount.blob.core.windows.net/container/blob",
    "myaccount",
    "<base64_account_key>",
    { {"x-ms-blob-type", "BlockBlob"}, {"Content-Type", "application/octet-stream"} },
    1024
);
// Use signedReq.m_Headers in your HTTP client
```

## Internal Methods

- **HmacSha256**: HMAC-SHA256 calculation using OpenSSL.
- **Base64Encode/Base64Decode**: Encoding and decoding for signature processing.
- **ParseUrl**: Parses URL into host, path, and query.
- **CanonicalizedHeaders**: Prepares lowercase, sorted `x-ms-*` headers as per spec.
- **CanonicalizedResource**: Builds canonical resource path for signing.
- **GetRfc1123Date**: Returns current UTC time formatted for HTTP headers.

## Header Construction

- `Authorization`: `SharedKey {accountName}:{signature}`
- `x-ms-date`: Current time in RFC 1123 format
- `x-ms-version`: API version (e.g., `2024-11-04`)

## Dependencies

- OpenSSL (for HMAC-SHA256 and Base64)
- C++ standard library (for strings, maps, chrono, etc.)

## License

GPL-3.0 License (see source file header for details)

---

**Note:** This class constructs headers per Azure documentation for Shared Key authentication. It is stateless and thread-safe. Proper error handling for invalid inputs and OpenSSL errors is expected in integration code.


---

### 110_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlMultiDispatcher_h

<a id="110-doc-engine-curlwrapper-curlmultidispatcher-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/110_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlMultiDispatcher_h/docu.output.md`



# CurlMultiDispatcher Documentation

**Namespace**: `AIAssistant`  
**File**: `curlMultiDispatcher.h` / `curlMultiDispatcher.cpp`  
**Dependencies**:  
- `libcurl` (HTTP/2 multi interface)  
- `curlWrapper.h`

## Overview

`CurlMultiDispatcher` is a thread-safe, dedicated I/O-thread dispatcher for HTTP/2 requests using libcurl's multi interface.  
- Manages all AI HTTP(S) requests to concurrent hosts efficiently.
- Shares a single TCP/TLS connection per host via HTTP/2 multiplexing.
- No thread-pool threads are blocked on network I/O.
- Handles retries (including rate limiting) and enforces per-host concurrency caps.

## Thread Safety

- `Submit()` is safe to call from any thread.
- Callbacks are executed on the internal I/O thread.
- Keep callback executions brief; file writes are safe.

## Class Interface

### Constructor & Destructor

- `CurlMultiDispatcher()`
  - Starts the dedicated I/O thread.
- `~CurlMultiDispatcher()`
  - Initiates shutdown and cleans up resources.
- Copy constructor and assignment are deleted; cannot copy or assign.

### Public Methods

#### `void Submit(CurlWrapper::QueryData const& data, Callback callback)`
* Thread-safe.
* Enqueues an HTTP request.  
* `Callback` fires on the I/O thread upon completion.

#### `void SignalStop()`
* Signals the I/O thread to stop accepting new requests.
* Initiates phase-1 shutdown.

#### `void WaitStop()`
* Blocks until the I/O thread exits (phase-2 shutdown).

### Types

#### `using Callback = std::function<void(QueryResult, std::string /*responseBody*/)>`
* Completion callback, called with the result and HTTP response body when a request finishes.

## Internal Mechanics

### Request Queues

- **Inbox**: Thread-safe queue for pending requests.
- **Active**: Map of in-flight (running) requests; managed strictly on the I/O thread.
- **RetryQueue**: For delayed retry of requests (rate-limiting or transient errors).

### Concurrency and Rate Limiting

- Maximum concurrent HTTP/2 streams per host: 48 (customizable via `kMaxActivePerHost`)
- Per-host rate limit state is parsed from provider headers (supports headers such as `x-ratelimit-remaining-requests`, etc.)
- Retries 429 (Too Many Requests) responses with exponential backoff or using provider's reset timers.

### HTTP/2 Connections

- Single TCP/TLS connection per host; streams are multiplexed for efficiency.
- Uses ALPN negotiation where possible; falls back to HTTP/1.1.

### Callbacks

- All callbacks are invoked on the dispatcher’s I/O thread.
- Callback receives a `QueryResult` (success or failure details) and the string response body.

### Shutdown Procedure

- `SignalStop()` and `WaitStop()` control orderly two-phase shutdown.
  - After `SignalStop()`, new requests are rejected.
  - In-flight requests complete, and work is wound down.
  - All remaining queued and retrying requests are cancelled on exit.

## Retry & Error Handling

- **429 (rate limit)**: Up to 5 retries (configurable, exponential backoff or as per response headers).
- **Transient errors (400, 500, 502, 503)**: Up to 2 retries.
- On shutdown, all pending or retrying requests are cancelled and fail with an abort error.

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::CurlMultiDispatcher dispatcher;
CurlWrapper::QueryData data;
// configure `data`
dispatcher.Submit(data, [](QueryResult result, std::string body) {
    if (result.ok()) {
        // Handle success with body
    } else {
        // Handle failure
    }
});
// Shutdown:
dispatcher.SignalStop();
dispatcher.WaitStop();
```

## Notable Internal Functions (for developers)

- `SetupEasyHandle(ActiveRequest&)`: Initializes and configures CURL easy handle for each request.
- `DrainInbox()`: Moves new requests to in-flight, enforcing per-host stream limits.
- `DrainRetryQueue()`: Moves eligible delayed retries back into in-flight queue.
- `ParseRateLimitHeaders()`: Updates per-host rate limit tracking from HTTP headers.
- `ExtractHost()`: Extracts hostname from full URL string.

## Summary Table

| Feature                  | Details                                                      |
|--------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|
| Thread-safe submission   | Yes (via `Submit()`)                                        |
| Background execution     | Dedicated I/O thread, never blocks pool/user threads        |
| HTTP/2 multiplexing      | Yes (1 connection per host, up to 48 concurrent streams)    |
| Rate limit handling      | Yes (429 + provider-specific rate headers, adaptive retry)  |
| Auto retry for transient | Yes (on several error codes)                                |
| Shutdown procedure       | Two-phase, graceful, safe                                   |
| Callback thread          | I/O thread only                                             |

## Limitations

- Not intended for long-running or streaming HTTP requests.
- All callbacks are invoked on the I/O thread—avoid heavy/blocking operations in callbacks.

## See Also

- `curlWrapper.h`, `QueryResult`, `CurlWrapper::QueryData`
- libcurl multi interface documentation

### 111_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlWrapper_h

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/111_doc_engine_curlWrapper_curlWrapper_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::CurlWrapper C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::CurlWrapper` is a utility class that performs authenticated HTTP queries (typically POST with JSON bodies) to AI providers using libcurl. It supports multiple authorization schemes, unified error reporting, timeout controls, and automatic CA bundle detection for cross-platform SSL support.

This class is intended for developers and AIs working with AI APIs that require programmatic, efficient, and robust HTTP access.

---

## Main Features

- **libcurl Integration:** Handles initialization, cleanup, CURL handle management, and error tracking.
- **Multiple AuthStyles:** Supports various AI vendor authorization schemes (Bearer, Google, Anthropic, Azure, AWS SigV4).
- **Unified Error Scheme:** Normalizes errors from libcurl, HTTP, and AI providers into a single error code space with descriptive messages.
- **Configurable Timeouts:** Optionally specifies transfer timeouts per request.
- **Automatic SSL CA Bundle Selection:** Attempts to find and use the appropriate CA certificates for HTTPS, based on system and environment.
- **Thread Safety:** Uses static synchronization for global curl initialization.

---

## Key Types

### `AIAssistant::CurlWrapper::AuthStyle`

Enumeration of authentication schemes:
- `Bearer` (default): `Authorization: Bearer <key>`
- `XGoogApiKey`: `x-goog-api-key: <key>`
- `AnthropicXApiKey`: `x-api-key: <key>`, `anthropic-version: ...`
- `AzureApiKey`: `api-key: <key>`
- `AwsSigV4`: AWS SigV4 signing (`Authorization`, `X-Amz-Date`, `X-Amz-Content-Sha256`)

### `AIAssistant::CurlWrapper::QueryData`

Describes a single query/HTTP API request:
- `m_Url`: Target URL (required)
- `m_Data`: POST JSON payload (required)
- `m_ApiKey`: Provider API Key (required)
- `m_AuthStyle`: Authorization scheme to use (default: Bearer)
- `m_TimeoutMs`: Timeout in milliseconds (0 = no timeout)
- `m_Params`: Key-value pairs for auth/provider specifics
- `IsValid()`: Returns true if all required fields are present

### `AIAssistant::QueryResult`

Represents the outcome of a query:
- `m_Ok`: `true` if successful
- `m_ErrorCode`: 0 for success, or CURLcode, HTTP code, or custom
- `m_ErrorMessage`: Human-readable explanation
- `Ok()`/`Fail(code, msg)`: Helper generators

### `AIAssistant::QueryErrorCode`

Defines integer error codes for:
- CURL transport errors (`1-99`)
- HTTP status codes (`100-599`)
- Internal/pre-flight/AI provider errors (`1000+`)
- `Describe(int code)`: Returns string message for a code

### `AIAssistant::CurlWrapper::CurlSlist`

RAII wrapper for `curl_slist` (header linked list):
- `Append(str)`: Add a header line
- `Get()`: Access the underlying pointer

---

## Main Methods

### CurlWrapper (Constructor/Destructor)

- **CurlWrapper()**: Initializes (globally, once) libcurl. Prepares a per-instance handle. Configures CA certificates if available.
- **~CurlWrapper()**: Cleans up the CURL handle.

### Copy/Move Semantics

- Copy is disabled (deleted).
- Move is permitted (default).

### Query API

- **IsInitialized()**: Returns true if this CurlWrapper is valid and ready for use.
- **Query(QueryData const& queryData)**:  
  Executes an authenticated HTTP query according to `queryData`.  
  Stores result in internal buffer (`m_ReadBuffer`).  
  Returns `QueryResult` indicating success or failure and diagnostic details.

- **GetBuffer()**: Returns (reference to) the internal string buffer containing the latest HTTP response body.
- **Clear()**: Clears the internal response buffer.

### Miscellaneous

- **GlobalCleanup()**: Invokes libcurl global cleanup (should be called before process shutdown if CurlWrapper was used).
- **GetCaBundlePath()**: Returns the path to the SSL CA bundle file chosen for curl setup, or empty string if none found.

---

## Error Handling

All errors are reported via unified error codes:
- **0**: Success
- **1-99**: libcurl errors
- **100-599**: HTTP status codes
- **1000+**: Internal / provider / pre-flight errors
- Use `QueryErrorCode::Describe(code)` for explanation strings.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::CurlWrapper::QueryData qd;
qd.m_Url = "https://api.example.com/v1/chat";
qd.m_Data = R"({"prompt":"Hello?"})";
qd.m_ApiKey = "YOUR_KEY";
qd.m_AuthStyle = AIAssistant::CurlWrapper::AuthStyle::Bearer;
qd.m_TimeoutMs = 5000;

AIAssistant::CurlWrapper curl;
if (!curl.IsInitialized()) { /* handle error */ }
AIAssistant::QueryResult result = curl.Query(qd);

if (result.m_Ok) {
    std::string response = curl.GetBuffer();
} else {
    std::cerr << "Error: " << result.m_ErrorCode << ": " << result.m_ErrorMessage << std::endl;
}
```

---

## Notes

- Requires libcurl, a C++17-compliant standard library, and thread support.
- All requests are sent with `Content-Type: application/json`.
- HTTP/2 is attempted by default when using HTTPS.
- Verbose logging can be enabled on the engine for diagnostics.
- CA bundle setup uses system paths or `CURL_CA_BUNDLE` and `SSL_CERT_FILE` environment variables.

---

## License

MIT License — see header for details. Free for use, modification, and distribution. No warranty.

---

© 2025 JC Technolabs

# JarvisAgent C++ Engine (`engine` function)

## Overview

This file contains the main engine entrypoint for the JarvisAgent application, a C++ backend for running Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)-based AI and automation workflows. The engine sets up core services, initiates logging, parses configuration, loads API keys securely, handles cloud connections, and starts up the main application instance.

---

## Key Features

- **CLI Parsing:** Supports `--help`, `--version`, and validates arguments. Unknown options are rejected with usage information.
- **Configuration:** Requires `config.json` in the working directory. Parses and checks config for correctness before executing any workflow.
- **Logging & Asserts:** Logging and assertion macros provided. Logging is enabled unless `DISTRIBUTION_BUILD` is defined.
- **API Key Management:**
  - Supports encrypted API key storage (`keys.json.enc`)
  - Fallback to plaintext `keys.json` for development
  - Last resort: `OPENAI_API_KEY` environment variable fallback (if no encrypted keys file)
- **Cloud Connections:** Loads cloud connection definitions from `connections.json` if present.
- **Python venv Support:** Handles Python virtual environment activation as needed for workflow tasks.
- **Application Lifecycle:** Orchestrates creation, startup, run, and shutdown of the main AI application.
- **Shutdown Watchdog:** Ensures proper and timely shutdown, terminating the process if the normal shutdown sequence hangs.
- **Diagnostics:** Raw I/O for shutdown diagnostics.

---

## Major Functions and Macros

### `engine(int argc, char* argv[])`
- Main entrypoint.
- Processes command-line arguments.
- Loads and validates configuration files.
- Initializes the core engine, logging, and key managers.
- Loads cloud connection info.
- Starts the JarvisAgent application and monitors shutdown.

### Logging Macros

- `LOG_CORE_TRACE`, `LOG_CORE_INFO`, `LOG_CORE_WARN`, `LOG_CORE_ERROR`, `LOG_CORE_CRITICAL`
- `LOG_APP_TRACE`, etc.
- `LOG_SECURITY_INFO`, `LOG_SECURITY_WARN`

Enable core, application, and security logging to separate channels.

### Assertion Macros

- `CORE_ASSERT(x, str)`
- `APP_ASSERT(x, str)`
- `CORE_HARD_STOP(str)`

Enable runtime assertions. On failure, critical messages are logged and the process may terminate (`CORE_HARD_STOP`).

### Static Helper Functions

- `PrintHelp()`: Displays version and usage information.
- `PrintVersion()`: Shows version.

---

## Usage

Invoke `engine` as your application's main function. Start the process with:

    jarvisAgent [--help|--version]

Requires a `config.json` in the working directory. API keys must be provided (see key management in the code). Optional cloud connection setup in `connections.json`.

---

## File Dependencies

- `log/log.h`, `core.h`: Core logging and engine structures.
- `curlWrapper/awsSigV4.h`: For AWS SigV4 authentication (self-test on debug).
- `json/configParser.h`, `json/configChecker.h`: Configuration parsing and verification.
- `jarvisAgent.h`: Application-specific entrypoint.
- Standard C++ and filesystem libraries.

---

## Typical Lifecycle

1. **Validate and parse command-line arguments.**
2. **Check for config and keys files.**
3. **Initialize core engine and loggers.**
4. **Load configuration with validation.**
5. **Initialize API key management.**
6. **Optionally, parse cloud connections.**
7. **Create and start the main AI application.**
8. **Run the main event loop.**
9. **On shutdown, terminate carefully with diagnostics and a watchdog.**

---

## Exit Codes

- `EXIT_SUCCESS (0)`: Normal completion or help/version shown.
- `EXIT_FAILURE (1)`: Configuration missing/invalid, unknown argument, failed initialization.

---

## Security Notes

- Encrypted API keys are preferred and handled securely in memory.
- No master password is accepted via command line or environment variable, only at runtime via API.
- Fallback to plaintext or environment variable exists for development only and is discouraged for production.

---

## Quick Reference Example

    jarvisAgent           # Starts the agent
    jarvisAgent --help    # Shows usage and config instructions
    jarvisAgent --version # Prints version

---

## See Also

- [API Endpoints Documentation](doc/api-endpoints.md)
- [Cyber Security Guidelines](doc/cyber%20security.md)
- [README.md](README.md) for installation and setup instructions

---

**Note:** This engine is the point of coordination for configuration, key management, application startup, and safe shutdown for the JarvisAgent system.


---

# AppErrorEvent Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::AppErrorEvent` is a C++ class representing error events specific to an application, derived from the base `Event` class. It encapsulates information about application errors via error codes.

## Namespace

`AIAssistant`

## Inheritance

- Inherits from: `Event`

## Public Types

- **enum ErrorCode**
  - `AppErrorBadCurl = 1000`  
    Indicates an error related to cURL operations.

## Constructors

- **AppErrorEvent(ErrorCode errorCode)**
  - Creates an `AppErrorEvent` with the specified `ErrorCode`.

## Public Methods

- **int GetErrorCode() const**
  - Returns the error code associated with the event as an integer.

## Macros

- **EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(AppError)**
  - Declares the event type as `AppError`.
- **EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryApp)**
  - Categorizes the event under application-specific events.

## Member Variables

- **ErrorCode m_ErrorCode** (private)
  - Stores the error code for the event.

## Usage Example

```cpp
#include "app_error_event.h"

using namespace AIAssistant;

AppErrorEvent errorEvent(AppErrorEvent::AppErrorBadCurl);
int code = errorEvent.GetErrorCode(); // code == 1000
```

## Purpose

Use `AppErrorEvent` to signal and propagate application-level error conditions within an event-driven architecture. The error code identifies the specific error type, enabling robust error handling and logging.

# AIAssistant::EngineEvent Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::EngineEvent` is a subclass of `Event` that represents engine-related events in the system, specifically for handling and identifying engine shutdown events.

## Namespace

- `AIAssistant`

## Inheritance

- Inherits from: `Event`

## Members

### Types

- **EngineEventCode**: Enum defining supported engine event codes.
    - `EngineEventShutdown = 2000`: Code for engine shutdown events.

### Constructors

- **EngineEvent(EngineEventCode engineEventCode)**
    - Creates an `EngineEvent` with the specified `EngineEventCode`.

### Methods

- **int GetEngineCode() const**
    - Returns the engine event code as an `int`.

- **EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(EngineEvent)**
    - Declares the class type for event handling (macro).

- **EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryEngine)**
    - Declares the event category as engine-related (macro).

### Private Members

- **EngineEventCode m_EngineEventCode**
    - Stores the event code associated with the engine event.

## Usage

Use `EngineEvent` to represent and handle engine shutdown events or other engine-level events. Query the event code with `GetEngineCode()` to determine the type of engine event.

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::EngineEvent shutdownEvent(AIAssistant::EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown);
if (shutdownEvent.GetEngineCode() == AIAssistant::EngineEvent::EngineEventShutdown) {
    // Handle engine shutdown logic.
}
```

# AIAssistant::Event System Documentation

This module provides a flexible and extensible event handling framework for C++. It is designed to enable quick identification, dispatching, and handling of various application- and AI-related events.

---

## Event Types and Categories

### Enum: `EventType`
Defines possible event types handled in the system:
- `None`
- `KeyPressed`, `KeyReleased`
- `MouseMoved`, `MouseButtonPressed`, `MouseButtonReleased`
- `TimerElapsed`
- `FileAdded`, `FileRemoved`, `FileModified`
- `AppError`
- `EngineEvent`
- `PythonCrashed`
- `AiCallStarted`, `AiCallRetrying`, `AiCallCompleted`, `AiCallFailed`

### Enum: `EventCategory`
Bitmask categories for events:
- `NoneCategory` = 0
- `EventCategoryKeyboard` = 1 << 0
- `EventCategoryMouse` = 1 << 1
- `EventCategoryTimer` = 1 << 2
- `EventCategoryFileSys` = 1 << 3
- `EventCategoryApp` = 1 << 4
- `EventCategoryEngine` = 1 << 5
- `EventCategoryAi` = 1 << 6

---

## Class: `AIAssistant::Event`

Abstract base class for all events. Intended for inheritance.

### Core Methods

- `virtual ~Event() = default;`  
  Destructor.

- `bool IsHandled() const`  
  Returns `true` if the event was handled.

- `void SetHandled(bool handled = true)`  
  Sets whether the event was handled.

- `virtual EventType GetEventType() const = 0`  
  Returns the event's type. Must be implemented by derived classes.

- `virtual const char* GetName() const = 0`  
  Returns the event's name as a string literal.

- `virtual int GetCategoryFlags() const = 0`  
  Returns the event's category flags.

- `bool IsInCategory(EventCategory category) const`  
  Checks if event belongs to given category.

- `virtual std::string ToString() const`  
  Returns string representation. Defaults to event name.

#### Protected Members

- `bool m_Handled = false`  
  Indicates if the event has been handled.

---

## Class: `AIAssistant::EventDispatcher`

Manages targeted dispatch of events to specific handler functions.

### Core Methods

- `EventDispatcher(Event& event)`  
  Constructs with reference to an event object.

- `template <typename T> bool Dispatch(EventFn<T> func)`  
  Dispatches the event to a handler function if the event matches type `T`.
  - If event matches, calls handler and marks as handled based on handler result.
  - Returns `true` if event was dispatched, otherwise `false`.

#### Private Members

- `Event& m_Event`  
  Reference to the event being dispatched.

---

## Macros

- `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(type)`  
  Implements static and virtual methods for event type and name.

- `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(category)`  
  Implements method for returning event category flags.

---

## Intended Usage

- Inherit from `AIAssistant::Event` to define new events.
- Use provided macros within derived event classes for consistency.
- Utilize `AIAssistant::EventDispatcher` to route events to appropriate handlers based on type.

---

## Example

```cpp
class KeyPressedEvent : public AIAssistant::Event {
public:
    KeyPressedEvent(int keyCode) : m_KeyCode(keyCode) {}
    int GetKeyCode() const { return m_KeyCode; }
    EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(KeyPressed)
    EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryKeyboard)
private:
    int m_KeyCode;
};
```

---

## License

MIT License (see code for details).


---

### 116_doc_engine_event_eventQueue_h

# `AIAssistant::EventQueue` Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::EventQueue` is a thread-safe queue for managing event objects (`AIAssistant::Event`). It allows safe insertion and removal of events from multiple threads, intended for main-thread consumption of accumulated events.

## Purpose

- Collect and store `Event` objects produced by any thread.
- Allow the main thread to efficiently and safely retrieve and process events.

## Public Types

- **EventPtr**: `std::shared_ptr<AIAssistant::Event>`  
  Smart pointer to an Event object.

## Public Methods

### `void Push(EventPtr event)`
- Adds an event to the queue.
- Thread-safe.
- Parameter: `event` - The event to enqueue.

### `EventPtr Pop()`
- Removes and returns the oldest event from the queue.
- Returns `nullptr` if the queue is empty.
- Thread-safe.

### `std::vector<EventPtr> PopAll()`
- Removes and returns all events currently in the queue as a vector.
- Intended for periodic use by the main thread.
- Thread-safe.

### `size_t Size()`
- Returns the number of events currently in the queue.
- Thread-safe.

## Thread Safety

- All public methods are thread-safe via an internal `std::mutex` (`m_QueueAccessMutex`).
- Designed for concurrent Push/Pop calls from different threads.

## Typical Usage Pattern

1. Worker/background threads push events using `Push`.
2. The main thread periodically calls `PopAll` to process accumulated events in batch.

## Internal Data Members

- `std::mutex m_QueueAccessMutex`: Synchronizes access to the queue.
- `std::queue<EventPtr> m_Queue`: Stores queued events.

## Namespace

- Declared within the `AIAssistant` namespace.

## Dependencies

- Requires `"event/event.h"` for the `AIAssistant::Event` type.

## License

- MIT License, see source comments for details.

---

**Summary:**  
`EventQueue` provides a simple, efficient, and thread-safe event accumulation and retrieval facility, ideally suited for systems where events are produced on background threads and processed on the main thread.


---

# C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

This C++ class is responsible for managing and handling different types of application events. It includes integration for application events, filesystem events, engine events, timer events, and Python error events.

## Included Headers

- `event/applicationEvent.h`  
- `event/filesystemEvent.h`  
- `event/engineEvent.h`  
- `event/timerEvent.h`  
- `event/pythonErrorEvent.h`  

These headers define various event types the class can process.

## Purpose

The main purpose of this class is to facilitate event-driven programming by providing a unified interface for handling multiple event sources within an application.

## Usage

Include this class in your application to enable handling of:
- Application lifecycle events (startup, shutdown)
- Filesystem changes (file creation, deletion, updates)
- Engine-specific events (rendering, logic updates)
- Timer-based events (scheduling, timeouts)
- Python runtime errors (if embedding or interacting with Python)

This helps decouple event sources and improves code organization by centralizing event management.

## License

MIT License (see source code for full text).

# AIAssistant File System Events Documentation

## Overview

This header defines file system-related event classes for the `AIAssistant` namespace. These classes represent file addition, removal, and modification events, categorizing them for event-driven architectures.

## Class Hierarchy

- **Event**  
  └─ **FileSystemEvent**  
    ├─ FileAddedEvent  
    ├─ FileRemovedEvent  
    └─ FileModifiedEvent  

## Classes

### FileSystemEvent

**Base class** for file system events. Inherits from `AIAssistant::Event`.

#### Constructor
- `FileSystemEvent(std::string path)`  
  Initializes the event with the path to the file.

#### Methods
- `const std::string& GetPath() const`  
  Returns the file path associated with the event.

#### Member Variables
- `std::string m_Path`  
  Holds the file path.

---

### FileAddedEvent

Represents an event for **file addition**. Inherits from `FileSystemEvent`.

- Inherits all constructors from `FileSystemEvent`.
- `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(FileAdded)` macro defines the event's type.
- `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryFileSys)` macro defines the event's category.

---

### FileRemovedEvent

Represents an event for **file removal**. Inherits from `FileSystemEvent`.

- Inherits all constructors from `FileSystemEvent`.
- `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(FileRemoved)` macro defines the event's type.
- `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryFileSys)` macro defines the event's category.

---

### FileModifiedEvent

Represents an event for **file modification**. Inherits from `FileSystemEvent`.

- Inherits all constructors from `FileSystemEvent`.
- `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(FileModified)` macro defines the event's type.
- `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryFileSys)` macro defines the event's category.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
FileAddedEvent fileAdded("/path/to/file.txt");
std::string path = fileAdded.GetPath();
```

## Notes

- These event classes are part of an event system, requiring the macros `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE` and `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY` to be defined elsewhere.
- All events store and provide access to the affected file's path.
- Useful in systems monitoring file changes or dispatching file-related notifications.

### 11_doc_application_cloud_cloudCircuitBreaker_h

<a id="11-doc-application-cloud-cloudcircuitbreaker-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/11_doc_application_cloud_cloudCircuitBreaker_h/docu.output.md`



# CloudCircuitBreaker (C++)

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`

**Purpose:**  
Provides a per-connection circuit breaker to prevent cascading failures during cloud provider outages. Implements the classic Closed–Open–HalfOpen state machine pattern for fault tolerance.

---

## Overview

`CloudCircuitBreaker` manages the availability of cloud service connections. It monitors failures and restricts or allows requests based on the health of each connection. This prevents repeated failures from overwhelming systems dependent on external services.

States:
- **Closed:** Normal operation. Requests are allowed. Tracks consecutive failures.
- **Open:** All requests are rejected immediately. After a cooldown, transitions to HalfOpen.
- **HalfOpen:** Allows limited probe requests. On probe success, transitions to Closed. On probe failure, transitions back to Open.

---

## Key Types

### enum class State
- `Closed`
- `Open`
- `HalfOpen`

### struct Config
- `m_FailureThreshold` – Number of consecutive failures to open circuit (default: 5)
- `m_CooldownSeconds` – Seconds to wait in Open before HalfOpen (default: 60)
- `m_HalfOpenProbeCount` – Probes allowed in HalfOpen (default: 1)

### struct ConnectionHealth
- `m_Name` – Connection name
- `m_State` – Current state (`State`)
- `m_ConsecutiveFailures` – Number of consecutive failures
- `m_EverSucceeded` – True if at least one successful operation occurred

---

## Public API

### Constructors

- **CloudCircuitBreaker()**
- **explicit CloudCircuitBreaker(Config config)**  
  Initialize with custom thresholds and cooldowns.

---

### bool AllowRequest(const std::string& connectionName)

Checks if a request is allowed for the specified connection.
- Returns `true` if allowed, `false` if the circuit is open.

---

### void RecordSuccess(const std::string& connectionName)

Records a successful operation for the connection.
- Resets failure count and closes the circuit if needed.

---

### void RecordFailure(const std::string& connectionName)

Records a failure for the connection.
- Tracks consecutive failures.
- Opens circuit if failure threshold is exceeded.
- In HalfOpen, a failure sends the circuit back to Open.

---

### State GetState(const std::string& connectionName) const

Returns the current state (`State`) of the specified connection.

---

### static std::string StateToString(State state)

Returns a human-readable string for the state: `"closed"`, `"open"`, `"half_open"`.

---

### std::vector<ConnectionHealth> GetHealthSummary() const

Returns a summary of the health for all tracked connections:
- Name
- State
- Consecutive failures
- Ever succeeded status

---

### bool HasEverSucceeded(const std::string& connectionName) const

Checks if the connection has ever recorded a successful operation.

---

## Thread Safety

All public methods are thread-safe via internal mutex locking.

---

## Typical Usage

1. Call `AllowRequest(connectionName)` before each operation.
2. On success, call `RecordSuccess(connectionName)`.
3. On failure, call `RecordFailure(connectionName)`.
4. Use `GetHealthSummary()` and `GetState()` for monitoring and diagnostics.

---

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::CloudCircuitBreaker breaker;
if (breaker.AllowRequest("providerA")) {
    if (doCloudOperation()) {
        breaker.RecordSuccess("providerA");
    } else {
        breaker.RecordFailure("providerA");
    }
}
```

---

## License

- **GPL-3.0**
- See the source header for terms.

---

## Notes

- Each connection is managed separately by name.
- Intended for preventing repeated requests to unhealthy remote services.


---

# PythonCrashedEvent Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `event/event.h`

## Overview

`PythonCrashedEvent` notifies the system about a Python process crash. It encapsulates a message describing the crash and integrates with an event-driven system by inheriting from the `Event` base class.

## Public Methods

### Constructor

#### `PythonCrashedEvent(std::string message)`
Creates a new event instance with the provided message describing the Python crash.

- **Parameters:**
  - `message`: A string detailing the crash context or error.

### GetMessage

Returns the crash message associated with the event.

- **Returns:** Reference to the message string.

#### `EVENT_CLASS_TYPE(PythonCrashed)`
Macro indicating the event's type is `PythonCrashed`.

#### `EVENT_CLASS_CATEGORY(EventCategoryEngine)`
Macro indicating the event belongs to the `EventCategoryEngine` category.

## Private Members

  Holds the crash message.

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::PythonCrashedEvent evt("Interpreter terminated unexpectedly");
std::cout << evt.GetMessage(); // Outputs: Interpreter terminated unexpectedly
```

## Summary

`PythonCrashedEvent` is used to signal Python interpreter crashes within an event-driven architecture, providing error details via a message string. It leverages predefined macros for integration with the event handling system.

### 122_doc_engine_input_keyboardInput_h

<a id="122-doc-engine-input-keyboardinput-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/122_doc_engine_input_keyboardInput_h/docu.output.md`



# KeyboardInput Class Documentation

## Overview

The `KeyboardInput` class in the `AIAssistant` namespace listens for keyboard input in a dedicated thread and dispatches keyboard events to the system. It enables the application to respond to user keyboard actions, including shutdown requests.

## Public Methods

### KeyboardInput()
Default constructor.

### ~KeyboardInput()
Default destructor.

### void Start()
Begins listening for keyboard input in a background thread. If listening is already active, this call has no effect.

### void Stop()
Stops keyboard input listening. Waits for the listener thread to finish. If not running, this call has no effect.

## Private Methods

### void Listen()
Core method executed in a separate thread. Continuously checks for keyboard input while `m_Running` is true.  
- On Linux/macOS: Uses `select` and `getchar` for non-blocking input (requires a terminal).  
- On Windows: Uses `_kbhit` and `_getch`.  
When the 'q' or 'Q' key is pressed, initiates application shutdown by posting a shutdown event.

## Member Variables

- `std::atomic<bool> m_Running`  
  Indicates if the input listener is running.

- `std::future<void> m_ListenerTask`  
  Stores the background task for the listener.

## Key Details

- Platform-independent handling via conditional compilation.
- Notifies the core system on keyboard events, including shutdown (via 'q'/'Q').
- Avoids blocking main thread.
- Logs relevant information for debugging or status.
- Checks for TTY availability on non-Windows systems; disables input in headless environments.

## Usage

1. Call `Start()` to begin keyboard listening.
2. Keyboard presses generate events (`KeyPressedEvent` for regular keys, `EngineEventShutdown` for 'q'/'Q').
3. Call `Stop()` to end keyboard listening and clean up the background thread.

## Dependencies

- Core system (`Core::g_Core`)
- Thread pool (`GetThreadPool().SubmitTask`)
- Event system (`PushEvent`)
- Logging (`LOG_CORE_INFO`)
- Platform headers (`<unistd.h>` for Unix, `<conio.h>` for Windows)
- `keyboardEvent.h`, `engine.h`

## Thread Safety

- The running state is managed atomically.
- Listener runs in a separate thread; main and listener threads are synchronized on shutdown.

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::KeyboardInput keyboardInput;
keyboardInput.Start();
// ... main application logic ...
keyboardInput.Stop();
```

## License

MIT License (see header for full terms).

### 123_doc_engine_json_configChecker_h

<a id="123-doc-engine-json-configchecker-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/123_doc_engine_json_configChecker_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::ConfigChecker

## Overview

`ConfigChecker` is a C++ class designed to validate and correct configuration settings for the AI Assistant engine. It ensures configuration parameters are present, correctly specified, and conform to expected ranges/constraints. It reports errors and automatically fixes some common issues.

## Public Methods

### ConfigChecker()
Default constructor.

### ~ConfigChecker()
Default destructor.

### bool Check(ConfigParser::EngineConfig& engineConfig)
- **Purpose:** Verifies the validity of the provided `EngineConfig` object.
- **Validation Performed:**
  - Checks if queue and workflows folder paths are directories.
  - Checks whether API interface configuration is valid (non-empty, correct URL, model specified, valid type).
- **Automatic Correction:**  
  If config is valid, fixes common misconfigurations:
    - Sets default thread count, engine sleep duration, max file size, max inflight AI calls, and python engines if outside expected ranges.
- **Error Handling:**  
  Prints specific error messages for failures with guidance on log file location.
- **Result:**  
  Sets `engineConfig.m_ConfigValid` to result and returns true if valid, false otherwise.

### bool ConfigIsOk() const
- **Returns:**  
  Status of configuration validity after last check (`true` if ok, `false` otherwise).

## Private Members

### bool m_ConfigIsOk
Holds result of last configuration check.

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::ConfigChecker checker;
ConfigParser::EngineConfig config = ... // fill in config fields
if (!checker.Check(config)) {
    // Handle invalid configuration
}
if (checker.ConfigIsOk()) {
    // Proceed with valid config
}
```

## Behavior Summary

- Ensures required configuration fields are valid.
- Automatically assigns default values to missing or out-of-range settings.
- Provides detailed stdout and log feedback for configuration issues.
- Does not throw exceptions; uses logging and return values for error reporting.

## Dependencies

- `"json/configParser.h"`
- Utilities from `EngineCore` (like `IsDirectory`)
- Macros: `CORE_ASSERT`, `LOG_CORE_ERROR`, and `LOG_APP_ERROR`

## Namespace

- `AIAssistant`

---

**Note:** The class assumes the presence of supporting logging, assertion, and utility code. Errors are logged and reported to both the console and a log file.


---

### 124_doc_engine_json_configParser_h

<a id="124-doc-engine-json-configparser-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/124_doc_engine_json_configParser_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::ConfigParser

**Purpose:**  
`ConfigParser` loads and parses a configuration file (typically JSON) describing AI engine interfaces and system settings for the AIAssistant framework.

## Overview

- Parses JSON config describing AI model endpoints, authentication, and operational parameters.
- Produces a validated `EngineConfig` structure holding all configuration info.
- Reports parse/validation state via `State`.
- Uses [simdjson](https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson) for fast JSON parsing.
- Can auto-resolve AI model context window limits using built-in fallback logic.

---

## Key Types

### `ConfigParser::State`
Represents parser state:
- `Undefined`
- `ConfigOk`
- `ParseFailure`
- `FileNotFound`
- `FileFormatFailure`

### `ConfigParser::EngineConfig`
Holds all parsed configuration options.

#### Nested types
- **`EngineConfig::InterfaceType`:** Enumerates supported API interface categories (API1, API2, ..., Test, InvalidAPI).
- **`EngineConfig::ApiInterface`:** Represents a single AI model endpoint/interface.
  - Fields: name, description, URL, model, API key name, interface type, max context tokens.

#### Notable utility functions
- **`GenerateInterfaceName()`**
  - Creates a unique interface name using URL domain, model, and API type.
- **`ResolveMaxContextTokensFromModel()`**
  - Returns best-guess context window size for a known model (e.g., gpt-4 → 128,000). Uses a fallback value for unknown models.

#### Main EngineConfig fields
- Threading, batching, and rate limit params.
- Paths for queue/workflow folders and key files.
- List of `ApiInterface` entries (AI endpoints).
- Security, TLS, and API-specific settings.
- Determinism control (temperature, seed).

---

## Main Methods

### Constructor
```cpp
ConfigParser(std::string const& configPathAndFilename)
```
- Stores the path to the config file for future parsing.

### Destructor
```cpp
~ConfigParser()
```
- Standard destructor (no special logic).

### GetState
```cpp
State GetState() const
```
- Returns current parser state.

### Parse
```cpp
State Parse(EngineConfig& outConfig)
```
- Loads, parses, and validates the configuration file.
- Populates `outConfig` with results.
- Sets and returns appropriate `State`.

### ConfigParsed
```cpp
bool ConfigParsed() const
```
- Returns true if configuration is successfully parsed.

---

## Internal Parsing Details

- **Checks file existence** and loads content.
- Uses simdjson to iterate and extract recognized config fields.
- Unrecognized JSON fields are logged.
- Some fields (like max context tokens) are auto-derived if omitted.
- Parsing produces detailed info about which fields occurred and their values.

---

## Example JSON Fields Recognized

- `"queue folder"` — Path for job queue storage.
- `"workflows folder"` — Path for workflow definitions.
- `"max threads"` — Thread pool limit.
- `"API interfaces"` — Array, each describing an API endpoint.
- `"API index"` — Default index for the main API to use.
- `"verbose"` — Enables detailed logging.

Each `"API interface"` array entry may specify:
- `"name"`
- `"description"`
- `"url"`
- `"model"`
- `"max_context_tokens"`
- `"API"` ("API1", "API2", etc.)
- `"key_name"`

---

## Fallback Logic

- If a model's context window is omitted, fallback uses case-insensitive substring matching on model names (e.g., "gpt-4", "claude", "llama").
- Unknown models default to 50,000 tokens.

---

## Design Notes

- Validates presence of required fields (`queue folder`, at least one API endpoint).
- Tries to auto-complete/auto-correct missing or malformed fields where possible.
- Structured for straightforward extension and robust error reporting.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::ConfigParser parser("aiassistant_config.json");
AIAssistant::ConfigParser::EngineConfig config;
if (parser.Parse(config) == AIAssistant::ConfigParser::ConfigOk) {
    // Use config...
} else {
    // Handle error...
}
```

---

## License

MIT License - JC Technolabs (2025)  
See header of source for full terms.


---

### 125_doc_engine_json_jsonHelper_h

<a id="125-doc-engine-json-jsonhelper-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/125_doc_engine_json_jsonHelper_h/docu.output.md`



# JsonHelper Class Documentation

## Overview

The `JsonHelper` class, defined in the `AIAssistant` namespace, provides utility functions for working with JSON data in C++. It is designed to assist with preparing strings for safe inclusion in JSON-formatted text.

## Class Declaration

```cpp
namespace AIAssistant
{
    class JsonHelper
    {
    public:
        JsonHelper();
        ~JsonHelper();

        std::string SanitizeForJson(std::string const& input);
    };
}
```

## Member Functions

### JsonHelper()

- **Constructor.**
- Initializes a `JsonHelper` object.
- Defaulted; does not perform any additional operations.

### ~JsonHelper()

- **Destructor.**
- Destroys a `JsonHelper` object.
- Defaulted; no custom cleanup.

### std::string SanitizeForJson(std::string const& input)

- **Purpose:**  
  Escapes special characters in a string so that it can be safely included in JSON text.

- **Parameters:**  
  - `input`: The string to sanitize.

- **Returns:**  
  - A new `std::string` where special characters are replaced by their JSON escape sequences.

- **Behavior:**  
  - The following characters in `input` are replaced:
    - `"` (double quote) → `\"`
    - `\` (backslash) → `\\`
    - Newline (`\n`) → `\n`
    - Carriage return (`\r`) → `\r`
    - Tab (`\t`) → `\t`
  - All other characters are copied unchanged.

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::JsonHelper helper;
std::string raw = "Line 1\t\"quoted\"\nLine 2\\end";
std::string sanitized = helper.SanitizeForJson(raw);
// sanitized: "Line 1\t\\\"quoted\\\"\nLine 2\\\\end"
```

## License

MIT License © 2025 JC Technolabs — See source code for details.
