### 126_doc_engine_keys_credential_h

<a id="126-doc-engine-keys-credential-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/126_doc_engine_keys_credential_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::ICredential and Derived Classes

## Overview

The `ICredential` class and its subclasses provide an abstract, type-safe hierarchy for storing various types of credentials within the `AIAssistant` namespace. This design replaces the use of unstructured API key strings with clear, strongly-typed credential types, simplifying credential management for applications that handle multiple authentication mechanisms.

---

## Class Hierarchy

### ICredential

**Abstract base class for all credential types.**

- **Purpose:** Provides a common interface for different credential forms used in `KeyManager`.
- **Members:**
  - `std::string m_Name;`  
    Unique credential name (usually provider or key name).
- **Methods:**
  - `virtual ~ICredential();`  
    Virtual destructor for safe polymorphic deletion.
  - `virtual std::string GetType() const = 0;`  
    Returns a string that identifies the subclass type (used for serialization).

---

### ApiKeyCredential

**Represents a simple API key or personal access token.**

- **Members:**
  - `std::string m_ApiKey;`  
    The API key or token value.
- **Methods:**
  - `std::string GetType() const override;`  
    Returns `"api_key"`.

---

### OAuthCredential

**Represents OAuth 2.0 credentials with access and refresh tokens.**

- **Members:**
  - `std::string m_AccessToken;`  
    The current OAuth access token.
  - `std::string m_RefreshToken;`  
    The OAuth refresh token.
  - `int64_t m_ExpiresAt;`  
    Unix timestamp (seconds) indicating token expiry.
  - `std::string m_Scopes;`  
    The OAuth scopes associated with the token.
- **Methods:**
  - `std::string GetType() const override;`  
    Returns `"oauth"`.

---

### KeyPairCredential

**Represents RSA private key credentials for JWT or similar authentication.**

- **Members:**
  - `std::string m_PrivateKeyPem;`  
    RSA private key in PEM format.
- **Methods:**
  - `std::string GetType() const override;`  
    Returns `"key_pair"`.

---

### BasicAuthCredential

**Represents username and password credentials (basic authentication).**

- **Members:**
  - `std::string m_Username;`  
    Username string.
  - `std::string m_Password;`  
    Password string.
- **Methods:**
  - `std::string GetType() const override;`  
    Returns `"credentials"`.

---

## Usage

- All credential types are derived from `ICredential` and can be managed polymorphically.
- Use the `GetType()` method to determine the subclass type for serialization or logic branching.
- Each subclass contains the necessary fields for its specific authentication mechanism.

---

## License

This code is released under the MIT License. See the source file for details.


---

### 127_doc_engine_keys_jwtGenerator_h

<a id="127-doc-engine-keys-jwtgenerator-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/127_doc_engine_keys_jwtGenerator_h/docu.output.md`



# JwtGenerator Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::JwtGenerator` is a static utility class for creating JSON Web Tokens (JWT) signed with RS256 (RSA + SHA-256). It leverages OpenSSL for cryptography and supports general JWT creation, as well as Snowflake-specific JWTs. It enforces a minimum RSA key size of 2048 bits.

---

## Public Interface

### static std::string Generate(
    std::string const& headerJson, 
    std::string const& payloadJson,
    std::string const& privateKeyPem, 
    std::string& errorMessage)

Creates an RS256-signed JWT.

- **Parameters:**
  - `headerJson`: JSON string for the JWT header
  - `payloadJson`: JSON string for the JWT payload
  - `privateKeyPem`: RSA private key in PEM format (min 2048 bits)
  - `errorMessage`: Outputs error descriptions on failure

- **Returns:**  
  Signed JWT (`header.payload.signature`) as a string, or an empty string on error.

---

### static std::string GenerateSnowflakeJwt(
    std::string const& account, 
    std::string const& user,
    std::string const& privateKeyPem, 
    std::string& errorMessage)

Convenience function for creating a JWT tailored for Snowflake database authentication. Handles uppercase conversion and required claims.

- **Parameters:**
  - `account`: Snowflake account name (will be uppercased)
  - `user`: Snowflake user name (will be uppercased)
  - `privateKeyPem`: RSA private key in PEM format (min 2048 bits)
  - `errorMessage`: Outputs error descriptions on failure

- **Returns:**  
  Signed Snowflake JWT (`header.payload.signature`) as a string, or an empty string on error.

---

## Private Methods

### static std::string Base64UrlEncode(std::vector<uint8_t> const& data)
### static std::string Base64UrlEncode(std::string const& data)

Performs Base64URL encoding (RFC 7515 / JWT-safe, no padding, `+` → `-`, `/` → `_`).

---

### static std::string ComputePublicKeyFingerprint(
    std::string const& privateKeyPem, 
    std::string& errorMessage)

Computes a SHA-256 fingerprint (`SHA256:<base64>`) of the public key derived from the supplied private key (DER-encoded, standard Base64 with padding). Used for the Snowflake JWT `sub` claim.

- **Parameters:**
  - `privateKeyPem`: RSA private key in PEM format
  - `errorMessage`: Outputs error descriptions on failure

- **Returns:**  
  Public key fingerprint string, or empty string on error.

---

## Usage Notes

- Always provide an RSA private key of at least 2048 bits.
- Fills `errorMessage` with details on failure (e.g., key parsing errors, signing errors).
- `GenerateSnowflakeJwt` automatically uppercases account and user names, strips region from the account if present, builds JWT claims as required by Snowflake, and creates a 1-hour expiry token.
- The class does not store keys or generated JWTs beyond what is needed for computation.

---

## Dependencies

- OpenSSL (BIO, EVP, PEM, RSA, SHA)
- C++ Standard Library (strings, vectors, chrono, algorithms)
- JC Technolabs internal headers: `engine.h`, `keys/jwtGenerator.h`, `log/secretRedactor.h`

---

## Example

```cpp
std::string error;
std::string jwt = AIAssistant::JwtGenerator::GenerateSnowflakeJwt(
    "my_account", "my_user", privatePemKey, error);
if (jwt.empty()) {
    std::cerr << "Failed to generate JWT: " << error << std::endl;
}
```

---

## License

This class is provided under the MIT License (see source file for full license text).


---

### 128_doc_engine_keys_keyEncryption_h

<a id="128-doc-engine-keys-keyencryption-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/128_doc_engine_keys_keyEncryption_h/docu.output.md`



# KeyEncryption C++ Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**File:** `keys/keyEncryption.h`  
**Purpose:** Encrypt and decrypt plaintext JSON using AES-256-GCM and password-derived key.

---

## Overview

`KeyEncryption` provides static methods for encrypting and decrypting JSON strings with a master password. It uses AES-256-GCM for authenticated encryption. The key is derived from the password using PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256. The encrypted format includes a header with magic, version, salt, and IV, followed by the ciphertext and authentication tag.

---

## File Format

Encrypted blob layout:

- `[4 bytes]` Magic: `"JKEY"`
- `[1 byte ]` Version: `0x01`
- `[16 bytes]` Salt (for PBKDF2)
- `[12 bytes]` IV (GCM nonce)
- `[ N  bytes]` Ciphertext
- `[16 bytes]` GCM Authentication Tag

---

## Constants

- `MAGIC`: `'J','K','E','Y'`
- `VERSION`: `1`
- `SALT_SIZE`: `16` bytes
- `IV_SIZE`: `12` bytes
- `TAG_SIZE`: `16` bytes
- `HEADER_SIZE`: `33` bytes (4+1+16+12)
- `PBKDF2_ITERATIONS`: `100,000`
- `KEY_SIZE`: `32` bytes (AES-256)

---

## Public Static Methods

### Encrypt

```cpp
static std::vector<uint8_t> Encrypt(const std::string& plaintext, const std::string& masterPassword);
```

#### Parameters

- `plaintext`: Plaintext JSON as a string.
- `masterPassword`: Password used to derive the encryption key.

#### Returns

- Encrypted blob as a vector of bytes (header + ciphertext + tag).
- Empty vector on failure.

#### Description

1. Generates a random 16-byte PBKDF2 salt and a random 12-byte IV (nonce).
2. Derives a 256-bit key using PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with the `masterPassword` and salt.
3. Encrypts the `plaintext` using AES-256-GCM with IV.
4. Prepends header (magic, version, salt, IV).
5. Appends 16-byte GCM authentication tag.

#### Errors

- Returns empty vector if random generation, key derivation, or encryption fails.
- Errors are logged (LOG_CORE_ERROR).

---

### Decrypt

```cpp
static std::string Decrypt(const std::vector<uint8_t>& encryptedBlob, const std::string& masterPassword);
```

#### Parameters

- `encryptedBlob`: The encrypted blob as produced by `Encrypt`.
- `masterPassword`: Password for key derivation.

#### Returns

- Decrypted plaintext JSON as a string.
- Returns empty string if decryption or authentication fails.

#### Description

1. Validates blob size, magic, and version.
2. Extracts salt, IV, ciphertext, and tag from header.
3. Derives the decryption key using PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 and salt.
4. Decrypts ciphertext with AES-256-GCM and validates the tag.
5. Returns plaintext if authentication succeeds; empty string otherwise.

#### Errors

- Returns empty string on invalid header, key derivation error, or authentication failure.
- Errors are logged (LOG_CORE_ERROR).

---

## Security Notes

- Key material is securely cleansed from memory after use.
- GCM mode ensures both confidentiality and integrity (authentication).
- Only AES-256-GCM with PBKDF2-SHA256 is used; versioning allows for future upgrades.
- Brute-force resistance is determined by PBKDF2 iteration count and password strength.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
#include "keys/keyEncryption.h"

using namespace AIAssistant;

std::string json = R"({"mykey":"mysecret"})";
std::string password = "StrongPassword!";

// Encryption
std::vector<uint8_t> ciphertext = KeyEncryption::Encrypt(json, password);

// Decryption
std::string decrypted = KeyEncryption::Decrypt(ciphertext, password);
```

---

## Dependencies

- OpenSSL (`<openssl/evp.h>`, `<openssl/rand.h>`)
- C++ STL (`<string>`, `<vector>`, `<cstdint>`, `<cstring>`)

---

## License

MIT License (see code for full terms).


---

### 129_doc_engine_keys_keyManager_h

<a id="129-doc-engine-keys-keymanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/129_doc_engine_keys_keyManager_h/docu.output.md`



# KeyManager Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `keys/keyManager.h`

## Purpose

The `KeyManager` class securely manages API credential sets ("providers") for various AI services. It supports storage, encryption, loading, updating, and retrieval of credentials, including legacy environment variable support and multiple credential formats (API keys, OAuth, key pairs, basic auth).

---

## Types

### Enum: `KeyLoadStatus`
Indicates the status after an attempted key file load.

- `Ok` – Loaded successfully.
- `NoKeysFile` – No encrypted key file found.
- `NoPassword` – Encrypted file exists, but no password provided.
- `WrongPassword` – Encrypted file exists, but decryption failed.

### Struct: `ProviderConfig`
Represents the configuration for a provider.

- `m_DisplayName` (`std::string`)
- `m_Endpoint` (`std::string`)
- `m_ApiKey` (`std::string`)
- `m_DefaultModel` (`std::string`)
- `m_ApiType` (`std::string`): For example, "API1" or "API2".
- `m_CredentialType` (`std::string`): "api_key" (default), "oauth", "key_pair", "credentials", or "aws".
- OAuth fields: `m_RefreshToken`, `m_ExpiresAt` (int64), `m_Scopes`, `m_TokenEndpoint`, `m_ClientId`, `m_ClientSecret`
- Key pair: `m_PrivateKeyPem`
- Basic auth: `m_Username`, `m_Password`
- Extra params: `m_Params` (`unordered_map<string, string>`)

---

## Core Methods

### File Operations

- `bool Load(std::filesystem::path const& keysFilePath, std::string const& masterPassword)`
  - Loads and decrypts providers from a file with the provided password.

- `bool Save(std::filesystem::path const& keysFilePath, std::string const& masterPassword)`
  - Serializes and encrypts current providers to a file.

- `bool LoadPlaintext(std::filesystem::path const& keysFilePath)`
  - Loads providers from a plaintext JSON file (for development use).

- `bool SavePlaintext(std::filesystem::path const& keysFilePath)`
  - Saves providers to a plaintext JSON file (for development use).

- `bool LoadFromEnvironment(std::string const& endpoint, std::string const& model, std::string const& apiType)`
  - Creates a single "openai" provider from the `OPENAI_API_KEY` environment variable.

- `bool Unlock(std::string const& masterPassword)`
  - Decrypts an already-set keys file path with the provided password at runtime.

---

### Provider Registry Access (Thread-Safe)

- `ProviderConfig const* GetProvider(std::string const& name) const`
  - Returns a pointer to the provider config by name, or nullptr if not found.

- `ProviderConfig const* GetDefaultProvider() const`
  - Returns a pointer to the default provider config, or nullptr if unset.

- `std::string GetDefaultProviderName() const`
  - Returns the name of the default provider.

- `std::vector<std::string> GetProviderNames() const`
  - Returns a list of all provider names.

- `bool HasProviders() const`
  - Returns true if any provider is present.

---

### Provider Registry Management (Thread-Safe, Write-Locked)

- `bool AddProvider(std::string const& name, ProviderConfig config)`
  - Adds a new provider; fails if the name already exists.

- `bool UpdateProvider(std::string const& name, ProviderConfig config)`
  - Updates an existing provider; fails if not found.

- `bool RemoveProvider(std::string const& name)`
  - Removes a provider by name; adjusts default if removed.

- `void SetDefaultProvider(std::string const& name)`
  - Sets the default provider (no-op if `name` not present).

---

### Other Utilities

- `std::string GetCachedMasterPassword() const`
  - Returns the master password cached on successful load/unlock; empty if unset.

- `KeyLoadStatus GetKeyLoadStatus() const`
  - Returns the current status of key loading.

- `void SetKeyLoadStatus(KeyLoadStatus status)`
  - Sets the key load status.

- `void SetKeysFilePath(std::filesystem::path const& path)`
  - Sets the file path used by `Unlock()` and future loads.

- `std::filesystem::path const& GetKeysFilePath() const`
  - Gets the current keys file path.

- `bool IsDirty() const`
  - Returns true if in-memory state is not synchronized with on-disk state.

---

## Implementation Details

- Providers are stored in an unordered map (`m_Providers`).
- All public methods are thread-safe (use a shared mutex).
- Supports secure password caching using `SecureString`.
- Uses simdjson for JSON parsing.
- Honors credential redaction for logging, notably AWS secret material.
- Maintains backward compatibility with plaintext dev files and legacy OpenAI API key via environment variable.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::KeyManager mgr;
if (mgr.Load("/path/to/keys.enc", "mypassword")) {
    auto provider = mgr.GetProvider("openai");
    if (provider) {
        // Use provider->m_ApiKey, etc.
    }
}
```

---

## Notes

- Plaintext file methods are for development only; use encrypted methods in production.
- Proper error handling/logging is integral; errors report the cause of common failures.
- Modifying providers marks the state as dirty; call `Save()` to persist changes.


---

### 12_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnectionManager_h

<a id="12-doc-application-cloud-cloudconnectionmanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/12_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnectionManager_h/docu.output.md`



# CloudConnectionManager Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::CloudConnectionManager` manages an in-memory collection of `CloudConnection` configurations. It supports thread-safe CRUD operations, JSON serialization/deserialization, and tracks whether in-memory state differs from on-disk state via a dirty flag. Connections are persisted externally via the REST API to `config.json`.

## Thread Safety

All operations are thread-safe using `std::shared_mutex`:  
- Writers (add, update, remove, parse JSON) use unique locks  
- Readers (get, list, serialize) use shared locks

## Public Interface

### Constructors

- `CloudConnectionManager()`
  - Default constructor.

### CRUD Methods

- `bool AddConnection(CloudConnection connection)`
  - Adds a new connection.  
  - Returns `false` if the name is empty or already exists.

- `bool UpdateConnection(std::string const& name, CloudConnection connection)`
  - Updates an existing connection identified by `name`.  
  - Returns `false` if not found.

- `bool RemoveConnection(std::string const& name)`
  - Removes the named connection.  
  - Returns `false` if not found.

### Read Methods

- `CloudConnection const* GetConnection(std::string const& name) const`
  - Returns pointer to the specified connection, or `nullptr` if not found.

- `std::vector<std::string> GetConnectionNames() const`
  - Returns names of all current connections.

- `std::vector<CloudConnection> GetAllConnections() const`
  - Returns copies of all current connections.

- `bool HasConnections() const`
  - Returns `true` if at least one connection exists.

### Dirty State

- `bool IsDirty() const`
  - Returns `true` if in-memory data has changed since last serialization.

- `void ClearDirty()`
  - Marks the manager as not dirty.

### Serialization

- `bool ParseConnectionsJson(std::string const& json)`
  - Replaces all connections by parsing the JSON structure.
  - Expects array at `connections` key.
  - Ignores malformed or missing entries.

- `std::string SerializeToJson() const`
  - Serializes all connections to a JSON string.

## Internal Representation

- Stores connections in `std::unordered_map<std::string, CloudConnection> m_Connections`;
- Access protected by `mutable std::shared_mutex m_Mutex`.
- Dirty flag in `bool m_Dirty`.

## JSON Schema

Connections are stored as an array of objects in a `connections` array. Each object contains:
- `name` (string)
- `type` (string)
- `endpoint` (string)
- `key_name` (string)
- `auth_type` (string or enum)
- `params` (object, optional, type-specific string-to-string map)

Example:
```json
{
    "connections": [
        {
            "name": "mycloud",
            "type": "aws",
            "endpoint": "https://api.aws.com",
            "key_name": "example-key",
            "auth_type": "api_key",
            "params": {
                "region": "us-west-2"
            }
        }
    ]
}
```

## Dependencies

- [simdjson](https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson) for fast JSON parsing.
- `cloudConnector.h` (defines `CloudConnection`)
- Logging macros such as `LOG_CORE_WARN`, `LOG_CORE_ERROR`

## Notes

- Names must be unique and non-empty.
- Changes via add/update/remove will set the dirty flag.
- Serialization escapes special characters for JSON safety.

---

© 2026 JC Technolabs. Licensed under GPL-3.0.


---

### 130_doc_engine_keys_oauthTokenManager_h

<a id="130-doc-engine-keys-oauthtokenmanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/130_doc_engine_keys_oauthTokenManager_h/docu.output.md`



# OAuthTokenManager Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`

Manages the lifecycle, storage, and refreshing of OAuth 2.0 access and refresh tokens for different credential providers. Supports thread-safe concurrent access and automatic background refresh.

---

## Overview

- Stores and manages OAuth 2.0 access tokens and associated refresh tokens.
- Automatically refreshes tokens when they are close to expiry.
- Thread-safe; suitable for use in multi-threaded applications.
- Integrates with a `KeyManager` that holds provider configurations and credentials.

---

## Public Methods

### Constructor / Destructor

- **OAuthTokenManager(KeyManager& keyManager)**  
  Initializes the manager with a given `KeyManager` reference.

- **~OAuthTokenManager()**  
  Stops the background refresh thread on destruction.

---

### Start / Stop

- **void Start()**  
  Starts the background thread for periodic token refresh. Also loads any pre-existing tokens from the `KeyManager`.

- **void Stop()**  
  Stops the background refresh thread and waits for its completion.

---

### Token Handling

- **void HydrateFromKeyManager()**  
  Loads OAuth credentials (with valid refresh tokens) from the `KeyManager` and prepares them for use, marking them as requiring an initial access token refresh.

- **std::string GetAccessToken(const std::string& keyName, std::string& errorMessage)**  
  Returns a valid access token for the named credential. If the token is missing or expired, blocks briefly to synchronously refresh it using the stored refresh token. Returns an empty string on failure and sets `errorMessage`.

- **void StoreTokens(const std::string& keyName, const std::string& accessToken, const std::string& refreshToken, int64_t expiresInSeconds, const std::string& tokenEndpoint = {}, const std::string& clientId = {}, const std::string& clientSecret = {})**  
  Stores and tracks initial token data after a completed OAuth consent flow, including endpoint and client information.

- **bool HasValidToken(const std::string& keyName) const**  
  Checks whether the named provider currently has a non-expired access token.

---

## Behavior

- **Automatic Refresh**:  
  - Runs a background thread that checks all tracked OAuth credentials periodically.
  - Refreshes any token that is expiring within 5 minutes.
  - Handles both public-client and confidential-client OAuth flows (supports client secret).
- **On-demand Refresh**:  
  - If a token is not available or has expired and an access request arrives, immediately refreshes as part of `GetAccessToken`.
- **Thread Safety**:  
  - All public methods are thread-safe.
  - Internal state is guarded by a mutex.

---

## Internal Types and Structures

- **struct TokenEntry**  
  Represents stored tokens and metadata for each credential provider:
    - `m_AccessToken`: The active access token.
    - `m_RefreshToken`: The refresh token.
    - `m_TokenEndpoint`: OAuth provider token endpoint.
    - `m_ClientId`: Client ID used for refresh requests.
    - `m_ClientSecret`: OAuth client secret.
    - `m_ExpiresAt`: Token expiry (Unix timestamp).
    - `m_Refreshing`: Whether the token is currently being refreshed.

---

## Dependencies

- **KeyManager**: Used to hydrate, lookup, and associate configuration for credential providers.
- **SecretRedactor**: Blocks sensitive tokens from logs and diagnostics.
- **cURL**: Used to perform HTTP requests for token refresh.
- **simdjson**: Used to parse refresh responses.

---

## Threading & Synchronization

- Uses `std::mutex` and `std::condition_variable` to synchronize token refresh and concurrent accesses.
- The background refresh thread can be started and stopped via `Start()` and `Stop()`.

---

## Usage Patterns

1. Instantiate `OAuthTokenManager` with a `KeyManager`.
2. Call `Start()` to enable background refresh and hydrate tokens from `KeyManager`.
3. Retrieve tokens for use with `GetAccessToken()`.
4. Store new or updated tokens as needed with `StoreTokens()`.
5. Stop the background thread with `Stop()` before shutdown or on destruction.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::KeyManager keyManager;
AIAssistant::OAuthTokenManager tokenManager(keyManager);

tokenManager.Start();

std::string error;
std::string accessToken = tokenManager.GetAccessToken("myProvider", error);
if (accessToken.empty()) {
    // Handle error
}

tokenManager.Stop();
```

---

## Error Handling

- Methods that fail (e.g., token refresh, missing token) will return default/empty results and provide error descriptions in the `errorMessage` argument (where applicable).

---

## Logging

- Logs lifecycle events, errors, and refresh outcomes using logging macros (e.g., `LOG_CORE_INFO`, `LOG_CORE_WARN`, `LOG_CORE_ERROR`).

---

## Security

- All tokens and secrets are registered with `SecretRedactor` to prevent accidental leakage via logs.

---

## License

MIT License. See file header for details.

---

**Note:**  
This class does not handle OAuth consent flows; it assumes refresh tokens and client credentials are already available (typically seeded via `KeyManager` or `StoreTokens`).


---

### 131_doc_engine_keys_secureString_h

<a id="131-doc-engine-keys-securestring-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/131_doc_engine_keys_secureString_h/docu.output.md`



# SecureString Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `keys/secureString.h`

## Overview

`SecureString` is a RAII (Resource Acquisition Is Initialization) buffer for handling sensitive strings (e.g., master passwords). It protects its contents by:

- Locking the backing memory (`mlock()`/`VirtualLock()`) to prevent swapping to disk.
- Zeroing out the memory (`explicit_bzero()`/`SecureZeroMemory()`) upon destruction, reassignment, or clearing.

**Primary goals:**  
- Prevent secrets from being written to swap or left in heap after use.  
- Defend against cold-boot/swap forensics and heap reuse leaks.  
- Does not defend against attackers with `ptrace`/kernel access.

## Class Definition

```cpp
class SecureString;
```

## Member Functions

### Constructor / Destructor

- `SecureString()`
  - Default constructor.
- `~SecureString()`
  - Destructor. Zeroes and releases any allocated memory.

### Copy/Move Semantics

- `SecureString(SecureString const&) = delete`
- `SecureString& operator=(SecureString const&) = delete`
  - Copying is disabled.
- `SecureString(SecureString&& other) noexcept`
  - Move-constructs from another `SecureString`.
- `SecureString& operator=(SecureString&& other) noexcept`
  - Move-assigns from another `SecureString`.

### Methods

- `void Set(std::string_view value)`
  - Copies the contents of `value` into a locked buffer. Old contents are securely erased.
  - If reallocation is needed, securely frees previous memory.
- `std::string_view Get() const`
  - Returns a view of the buffer contents. View is valid until the next `Set()`, `Clear()`, or destruction.
  - Returns an empty view if the string is empty.
- `bool IsEmpty() const`
  - Returns true if the buffer is empty.
- `size_t Size() const`
  - Returns the size (number of bytes) of the string.
- `void Clear()`
  - Securely zeroes the buffer and marks it as empty.

## Internal Functions (Private)

- `void Release()`
  - Helper to zero, unlock, and release memory.

## Data Members (Private)

- `char* m_Buffer`
  - Pointer to locked memory holding string data.
- `size_t m_Size`
  - Length of used buffer.
- `size_t m_Capacity`
  - Allocated buffer size.

## Platform-Specific Details

- Uses `VirtualLock`/`VirtualUnlock` on Windows, `mlock`/`munlock` on POSIX.
- Uses appropriate secure zeroing functions per platform.

## Limitations

- Protection does not extend to compromise via kernel/debugger access.
- If memory locking fails, the buffer still works but is not locked (warning is logged).

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::SecureString secret;
secret.Set("my password");
auto view = secret.Get();
// Use 'view' while needed...
secret.Clear();
```

## License

GPL-3.0, Copyright (c) 2026 JC Technolabs

---

**Summary:**  
Use `SecureString` to reduce risk of leaking sensitive strings in swap or freed heap memory. It ensures secure handling and destruction of secret data in memory.  
Not a substitute for full operating system security controls.


---

### 132_doc_engine_log_log_h

<a id="132-doc-engine-log-log-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/132_doc_engine_log_log_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::Log Class Documentation

## Overview

The `AIAssistant::Log` class manages application logging using the [spdlog](https://github.com/gabime/spdlog) library.  
It provides three separate logger instances: Engine, Application, and Security.  
Loggers write to the terminal (std::cout) and, for security logs, to a rotating file.

## Key Features

- **Centralized logging utility** for engine, application, and security logs.
- **Terminal logging** via ostream sink, suitable for integration with TUI systems.
- **Dedicated rotating file logging** for security-related events, up to 5 files of 10MB each.
- **Customizable log message patterns** (timestamp, logger name, level, message).
- **Trace-level logging and immediate flushing** for all loggers.

## Class Members

### Public Methods

- **Log()**
  - Constructor. Initializes and configures the three loggers and their sinks.

- **spdlog::logger& GetLogger()**
  - Returns a reference to the Engine logger.

- **spdlog::logger& GetAppLogger()**
  - Returns a reference to the Application logger.

- **spdlog::logger& GetSecurityLogger()**
  - Returns a reference to the Security logger.

### Private Members

- **std::shared_ptr<spdlog::logger> m_Logger**
  - Engine logger instance.

- **std::shared_ptr<spdlog::logger> m_AppLogger**
  - Application logger instance.

- **std::shared_ptr<spdlog::logger> m_SecurityLogger**
  - Security logger instance.

## Logging Behavior

- **Engine and Application logs:** Output to terminal (std::cout) via ostream sink, formatted as `[date time] [logger] [level] message`.
- **Security logs:** Output to both terminal and a rotating file at `log/security.txt` (retaining 5 files, 10MB each), formatted as `[date time] [Security] [level] message`.

## Usage Example

```cpp
#include "log/log.h"

AIAssistant::Log log;

// Engine log
log.GetLogger().info("Engine started");

// Application log
log.GetAppLogger().warn("Potential issue detected");

// Security log
log.GetSecurityLogger().error("Unauthorized access attempt");
```

## Notes

- Make sure `spdlog` is available and included in your project.
- The class creates the `log` directory automatically for security log files.
- Patterns avoid color codes for compatibility with TUI/ncurses environments.

## License

MIT License. See source file header for full terms.


---

### 133_doc_engine_log_secretRedactor_h

<a id="133-doc-engine-log-secretredactor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/133_doc_engine_log_secretRedactor_h/docu.output.md`



# SecretRedactor Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `log/secretRedactor.h`  

## Purpose

The `SecretRedactor` class provides thread-safe redaction of sensitive information (secrets) from log output. Registered secrets are removed from any log message, replaced by the string `[REDACTED]`. It is designed for environments where confidential values (like API keys, tokens, passwords) must not appear in logs.

## Features

- Singleton access via `SecretRedactor::Get()`
- Thread-safe: supports concurrent addition, removal, and redaction of secrets
- Ignores secrets shorter than 4 characters to reduce false positives
- Only exact matches of secrets are redacted
- Redacted text is replaced with `[REDACTED]`

## Class Members

### Static Methods

#### `static SecretRedactor& Get();`
Returns the singleton instance of `SecretRedactor`.

### Public Methods

#### `void AddSecret(std::string const& secret);`
Registers a secret value to be redacted.  
- If the secret is shorter than 4 characters, it is ignored.
- Duplicate secrets are not added.

#### `void RemoveSecret(std::string const& secret);`
Removes a previously registered secret.  
- Useful for cases like token rotation.

#### `std::string Redact(std::string const& message) const;`
Redacts all registered secrets from the input message, replacing them with `[REDACTED]`.  
- Secrets are replaced everywhere they appear in the message.

#### `bool HasSecrets() const;`
Returns true if at least one secret is registered.

### Private Members

- `mutable std::mutex m_Mutex;`  
  Ensures thread-safe operations on secrets.
- `std::vector<std::string> m_Secrets;`  
  Stores the list of registered secrets.

## Example Usage

```cpp
using AIAssistant::SecretRedactor;

// Register secrets
SecretRedactor::Get().AddSecret("super_secret_token");

// Redact secrets in a message
std::string safeMsg = SecretRedactor::Get().Redact("This is my super_secret_token!");

// Remove secrets as needed
SecretRedactor::Get().RemoveSecret("super_secret_token");
```

## Thread Safety

All methods that access or modify secrets acquire a mutex lock, making the class safe for use from multiple threads simultaneously.

## Notes

- Registered secrets are matched exactly; partial, case-insensitive, or pattern-based redaction is not supported.
- Redaction is performed sequentially for each secret in the registration order.
- Secrets must be at least 4 characters long to avoid accidental redaction of common substrings.

## License

MIT License (see code for details).


---

### 134_doc_engine_log_terminalLogStreamBuf_h

<a id="134-doc-engine-log-terminallogstreambuf-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/134_doc_engine_log_terminalLogStreamBuf_h/docu.output.md`



# TerminalLogStreamBuf Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** Included via `#include "log/terminalManager.h"`

## Overview

`TerminalLogStreamBuf` is a custom stream buffer derived from `std::streambuf`. It efficiently routes log output simultaneously to a terminal manager, a file logger, and an optional callback function. It removes ANSI escape sequences before writing. Thread safety is ensured using mutexes.

## Constructor

```cpp
TerminalLogStreamBuf(TerminalManager* terminalManager, std::shared_ptr<std::ofstream> fileLogger)
```
- `terminalManager`: Pointer to a `TerminalManager` for log display handling.
- `fileLogger`: Shared pointer to an `std::ofstream` for writing logs to a file.

## Public Methods

### void SetLogBroadcastCallback(std::function<void(std::string const&)> callback)

Sets a callback function to receive broadcasted log lines (after ANSI stripping). Thread-safe.

---

## Overridden Protected Methods

### int sync() override

Flushes the buffer, writes the content (with ANSI escapes removed) to:
- The terminal manager (via `EnqueueLogLine`)
- The file logger (`std::ofstream`)
- The broadcast callback (if set)

Thread-safe.

### int overflow(int character) override

Handles inserting a character into the buffer. On newline (`\n`), calls `syncLocked()` to flush. Otherwise, accumulates characters.

### std::streamsize xsputn(char const* data, std::streamsize count) override

Writes a range of characters. Handles newlines by splitting and flushing buffered lines as needed.

---

## Private Methods

### static std::string StripAnsi(std::string const& input)

Removes ANSI escape sequences from a string for cleaner log output.

### int syncLocked()

Internal buffer flush; writes the cleaned buffer to the terminal manager, file logger, and callback function. Resets buffer after writing. Ensures thread-safe operation.

---

## Members

- `TerminalManager* m_TerminalManager`: For terminal log display.
- `std::string m_Buffer`: Internal log line buffer.
- `std::mutex m_BufferMutex`: Guards access to the buffer.
- `std::shared_ptr<std::ofstream> m_FileLogger`: File output log stream.
- `std::mutex m_FileMutex`: Guards access to the file logger.
- `std::function<void(std::string const&)> m_LogBroadcastCallback`: Optional log broadcast callback.
- `std::mutex m_CallbackMutex`: Guards access to the callback.

---

## Usage

`TerminalLogStreamBuf` is best used where log streams should be displayed to users, persisted to disk, and optionally broadcast to other consumer functions (e.g., UIs, network relays), all in a thread-safe way and free of terminal formatting escapes.

---

## Thread Safety

All mutable members are protected by their respective mutexes. Log flushing operations and callback invocations are safe across threads.

---

## License

MIT License, 2025 JC Technolabs


---

### 135_doc_engine_log_terminalManager_h

<a id="135-doc-engine-log-terminalmanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/135_doc_engine_log_terminalManager_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::TerminalManager

`TerminalManager` is a C++ class for managing structured log and status output in a terminal UI using PDCursesMod. It provides a thread-safe interface for queuing log lines and displaying dynamic status sections, supporting both human users and AI systems in real-time terminal applications.

## Purpose

- Display log and status messages in a terminal in a structured, visually separated format.
- Safely render log lines from multiple threads.
- Support dynamic status lines via user-supplied callbacks.
- Ensure robust UTF-8 handling for AI-generated and user text.
- Abstract terminal management and resizing complexities.

## Usage Summary

1. **Instantiate** `TerminalManager`.
2. **Call** `Initialize()` before any rendering.
3. **Setup** status callbacks via `SetStatusCallbacks()`.
4. **Feed** logs with `EnqueueLogLine()`.
5. **Call** `Render()` on each refresh/tick.
6. **Call** `Shutdown()` before exit.

---

## Public API

### Constructors/Destructors

- **TerminalManager()**  
  Creates and initializes the manager object.

- **~TerminalManager()**  
  Cleans up resources, calls `Shutdown()` if not already called.

### Lifecycle

- **void Initialize()**  
  Initializes the terminal, sets up windows/layouts, and prepares for rendering. Safe to call multiple times (will only initialize once).

- **void Shutdown()**  
  Destroys windows, restores terminal state, and releases resources. Safe to call multiple times.

### Rendering

- **void Render()**  
  Updates terminal UI: drains queued logs, refreshes log and status windows, and handles resizing.

- **void RenderPaused(int counter)**  
  Displays a "paused" message in the log window, showing the provided counter value; also updates the status window.

### Logging

- **void EnqueueLogLine(const std::string& line)**  
  Thread-safe. Queues a new log line for display. Actual rendering occurs on the next `Render()` call.

### Dynamic Status

- **void SetStatusCallbacks(StatusLinesCallback, StatusHeightCallback)**  
  - `StatusLinesCallback`: receives a vector of reference to fill with status lines and the display width.
  - `StatusHeightCallback`: computes the number of rows the status window should occupy, given the total available rows.
  Used to provide current status text and dynamic sizing logic for the status area. Can be called at any time; will trigger window recreation if already initialized.

---

## Implementation Details

- Log and status windows are rendered with headers, sized according to terminal dimensions.
- Log lines are sanitized for Unicode and incompatible control characters before printing.
- Log lines can be enqueued from any thread; rendering and dequeueing are synchronized.
- Terminal resizing is automatically handled on each `Render()`.
- Flexible status area: user supplies callbacks for both contents and desired visible height.
- Uses PDCursesMod for cross-platform terminal window management.
- On shutdown, restores terminal modes (POSIX only), and clears all created windows.

---

## Key Types

- **StatusLinesCallback:**  
  `std::function<void(std::vector<std::string>& lines, int maxWidth)>`  
  Fill `lines` with text to be displayed, respecting provided max width.

- **StatusHeightCallback:**  
  `std::function<int(int totalRows)>`  
  Return desired status window content height (in rows), given the current total terminal rows.

---

## Thread Safety

- Log queue (`EnqueueLogLine`/internal rendering) is guarded by a mutex.
- All public API functions are safe to call from the main thread; `EnqueueLogLine` can be called from any thread.

---

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::TerminalManager tm;
tm.Initialize();
tm.SetStatusCallbacks(
    [](std::vector<std::string>& lines, int width) {
        lines.push_back("Status: Running...");
        // Add additional status lines
    },
    [](int totalRows) { return 2; } // Show 2 status lines
);
tm.EnqueueLogLine("Log message 1");
tm.Render();
// ... repeat Render() in a loop
tm.Shutdown();
```

---

## Notes

- Only effective in a terminal (TTY) environment.
- Requires PDCursesMod library.
- Unicode support: replaces invalid or oversized codepoints with `?` to avoid rendering crashes.

---

## License

MIT License – see file header for full terms.


---

### 136_doc_engine_auxiliary_TracyClient_cpp

<a id="136-doc-engine-auxiliary-tracyclient-cpp"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/136_doc_engine_auxiliary_TracyClient_cpp/docu.output.md`



# Tracy Profiler Integration Documentation

## Overview

This integration file enables the Tracy profiler in a C++ project. The Tracy profiler is a real-time, nanosecond resolution frame profiler for applications. This file serves as the main entry point for embedding Tracy by compiling and linking only this source file with the application.

## Usage

1. **Enabling Profiler**:  
   Define `TRACY_ENABLE` in your build system or in your code to activate the profiler integration.

2. **Integration**:  
   - Add this source file to your build.
   - Do not compile or link any other Tracy source files directly.
   - For multi-DLL/shared object projects, link this file only in the main DLL/shared object.

3. **Supported Platforms**:  
   Supports major desktop platforms, with additional handling for symbol resolution and stack traces depending on the platform and compiler.

## Source Files Included

When `TRACY_ENABLE` is defined, the following source files are included for profiling functionality:
- `TracySystem.cpp`: System abstraction.
- `tracy_lz4.cpp`: Compression.
- `TracyProfiler.cpp`: Core profiler logic.
- `TracyCallstack.cpp`: Call stack capture.
- `TracySysPower.cpp`: System power status.
- `TracySysTime.cpp`: Timing functions.
- `TracySysTrace.cpp`: System tracing.
- `TracySocket.cpp`: Network communication.
- `tracy_rpmalloc.cpp`: Custom memory allocator.
- `TracyDxt1.cpp`: Texture compression (for UI).
- `TracyAlloc.cpp`: Memory allocation tracking.
- `TracyOverride.cpp`: Override global operators.
- `TracyKCore.cpp`: Kernel statistics.

If callstack support is enabled (`TRACY_HAS_CALLSTACK`), additional files for stack trace resolution are included, such as libbacktrace modules and stack frames support.

## Windows-Specific details

When building on MSVC:
- Suppresses some compiler warnings.
- Links with required system libraries: `ws2_32`, `dbghelp`, `advapi32`, `user32`.

## How to Use in Your Project

1. Define `TRACY_ENABLE` (for example, via a compiler flag: `-DTRACY_ENABLE`).
2. Add this integration source file to your project.
3. Rebuild your application.
4. Run the Tracy server to connect to your application for profiling.

## Notes

- Only include and compile/link this integration file in your project; do not include individual Tracy source files elsewhere.
- For projects with multiple shared libraries, ensure only one main executable or shared object incorporates this file.

## Related Resources

- [Tracy documentation](https://github.com/wolfpld/tracy)
- [Tracy releases and server](https://github.com/wolfpld/tracy/releases)

## License

Refer to the Tracy project for license details.

# Documentation: Main Program Entry Point

## Description

This file defines the entry point (`main`) of a C++ program. Its purpose is to initialize and run an application engine.

## Components

### main(int argc, char* argv[])

- **Parameters:**
  - `argc`: Number of command-line arguments.
  - `argv`: Array of command-line argument strings.
- **Returns:**  
  - Returns the integer result of `engine(argc, argv)`.

### engine

- The function `engine(argc, argv)` is called (definition is expected in `"engine.h"`).
- Handles initialization and execution of the application's core logic, passing command-line arguments.

## Usage

- Run the program with command-line arguments as needed.
- The `main` function delegates execution to the engine and returns its result.

### 13_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnectionPool_h

<a id="13-doc-application-cloud-cloudconnectionpool-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/13_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnectionPool_h/docu.output.md`



# CloudConnectionPool Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `cloud/cloudConnectionPool.h`  
**License:** GPL-3.0  
**Author:** JC Technolabs (2026)  

## Purpose

`CloudConnectionPool` is a thread-safe, generic connection pool for persistent-connection protocols (such as PostgreSQL via `libpq`). It manages connection reuse, health checking, automatic eviction, and lifecycle callbacks. It is intended for non-HTTP protocols, as HTTP is usually handled by libraries like `libcurl`.

---

## Key Features

- **Pooling by Name:** Separate connection pools by connection name (e.g., "my-postgres").
- **Custom Lifecycle:** Per-type callbacks allow for custom creation, destruction, and health-check logic.
- **Timeouts & Limits:** Per-pool connection and wait timeouts prevent resource exhaustion.
- **Eviction:** Idle or unhealthy connections are removed automatically or explicitly.
- **Thread-safe:** Uses mutex and condition variable for multithreaded environments.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
auto handle = pool.Acquire("my-postgres", "postgres", createFn, errorMsg);
// use handle.m_Connection (e.g., PGconn*)
pool.Release("my-postgres", "postgres", std::move(handle));
```

---

## Structs & Types

### Config

Configures pool behavior:

- `m_MaxConnectionsPerName` (int): Max idle + active connections per connection name. Default 4.
- `m_MaxIdleTime` (seconds): Idle connection eviction timeout. Default 300s.
- `m_AcquireTimeout` (seconds): Max wait to acquire a connection when pool is full. Default 10s.

### PooledHandle

Represents a checked-out or idle connection:

- `void* m_Connection`: Opaque pointer to actual connection object.
- `std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point m_LastUsed`: Last usage time.
- `bool m_Valid`: Whether the handle is valid.
- Explicit bool operator: True if valid and has a connection.

### Callbacks

- `CreateFn`: `void* (std::string&)` — Creates a connection, returns pointer or null.
- `DestroyFn`: `void(void*)` — Destroys a given connection.
- `HealthCheckFn`: `bool(void*)` — Returns true if connection is healthy.

---

## Main API

### CloudConnectionPool(Config config = {})

Constructor. Pass custom settings via `Config`.

### ~CloudConnectionPool()

Destructor. Drains and destroys all pooled connections.

### void RegisterType(const std::string& type, DestroyFn destroyFn, HealthCheckFn healthCheckFn)

Registers destroy and health-check callbacks for a connection type (e.g., "postgres").

### PooledHandle Acquire(const std::string& connectionName, const std::string& type, const CreateFn& createFn, std::string& errorMessage)

Acquires a pooled connection:
- Returns a healthy, idle connection if available.
- Otherwise, creates a new one if under the limit.
- Else, waits for up to `m_AcquireTimeout`.
- On error, sets `errorMessage` and returns invalid handle.

### void Release(const std::string& connectionName, const std::string& type, PooledHandle handle)

Returns a handle to the pool for re-use. Notifies waiting acquirers.

### void EvictStale()

Evicts all idle connections exceeding `m_MaxIdleTime`.

### void Drain(const std::string& connectionName)

Destroys all idle connections for a given connection name.

### void DrainAll()

Destroys all idle connections in all pools.

---

## Internals

- Connection handles are moved between idle and active lists.
- Destruction and health checks are enforced per type.
- Pooling, eviction, and draining are synchronized using locks.
- The pool does **not** own or manage the lifetime of the underlying connection type except as indicated by callbacks.

---

## Notes

- Only for persistent-connection, non-HTTP protocols.
- Not intended for stateless or short-lived HTTP connections (use `libcurl` pooling instead).
- For custom connection types, users must provide creation, destruction, and optional health-check logic.

---

## Example Registration

```cpp
pool.RegisterType(
  "postgres",
  [](void* conn) { PQfinish(static_cast<PGconn*>(conn)); },
  [](void* conn) { return PQstatus(static_cast<PGconn*>(conn)) == CONNECTION_OK; }
);
```

---

## Thread Safety

All public methods are thread-safe; internal data is guarded by mutex and condition variable.

---

## Limitations

- Pool only tracks idle and in-use handles. Lost or leaked `PooledHandle` instances are not reclaimed until explicitly drained.
- Does not perform reconnection or handle server-side disconnects outside of health checks.
- User must supply robust create/destroy/health logic per connection type.

---


---

### 14_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnector_h

<a id="14-doc-application-cloud-cloudconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/14_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnector_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant Cloud Connector API

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**License:** GPL-3.0 (see source header)  
**Purpose:** Unified abstraction for representing and connecting to various cloud services with authentication and configuration management.

---

## Key Types

### `enum class CloudAuthType`
Defines authentication methods:
- **BearerToken:** API key/PAT using Authorization: Bearer header
- **OAuth2:** OAuth 2.0 token (auto-refreshable)
- **JwtRsa:** JWT signed with RSA (e.g., Snowflake)
- **BasicAuth:** Username/password
- **SigV4:** AWS Signature Version 4 (S3-compatible)
- **AzureSharedKey:** Azure Storage Shared Key

Serialization helpers:
- `AuthTypeToString(authType)` – Converts enum to string.
- `StringToAuthType(str)` – Converts string to enum.

---

### `struct CloudCredentials`
Runtime-resolved credentials for making HTTP requests.
- `CloudAuthType m_AuthType`
- `std::string m_Token` – For Bearer, OAuth, JWT
- `std::string m_AccessKeyId` – For SigV4
- `std::string m_SecretKey` – For SigV4
- `std::string m_Username` – For BasicAuth
- `std::string m_Password` – For BasicAuth

_Not designed for persistent storage. Used after resolving and refreshing credentials._

---

### `struct CloudConnection`
Configuration for a named cloud connection. Persistable (e.g., config file).
- `std::string m_Name` – User-visible name
- `std::string m_Type` – Connector class/type (e.g., "s3", "snowflake")
- `std::string m_Endpoint` – Base URL or identifier
- `std::string m_KeyName` – Reference to stored credential
- `CloudAuthType m_AuthType`
- `std::map<std::string, std::string> m_Params` – Additional config

---

### `struct OAuth2ProviderInfo`
Provider-specific metadata for OAuth2 flow.
- `std::string m_AuthorizeUrl`
- `std::string m_TokenUrl`
- `std::string m_DefaultScopes` – Space-separated scopes
- `std::map<std::string, std::string> m_ExtraAuthorizeParams`
- `bool m_RequiresClientSecret` – If client_secret required in token exchange

Used by generic OAuth2 handling functions for dynamic provider support.

---

## Abstract Interface: `ICloudConnector`

Used to implement connectors for specific cloud providers.

### Methods

- **`virtual std::string GetType() const = 0`**  
  Returns connector type string (e.g., "s3", "polarion").

- **`virtual bool TestConnection(CloudConnection const&, std::string& errorMessage) = 0`**  
  Checks connectivity. Returns `true` on success, `false` on failure with `errorMessage` populated.

- **`virtual bool ResolveCredentials(CloudConnection const&, CloudCredentials&, std::string& errorMessage) = 0`**  
  Resolves/refreshes credentials, handles provider-specific logic. Returns `true` on success, or `false` with failure reason.

- **`virtual bool GetOAuth2ProviderInfo(CloudConnection const&, OAuth2ProviderInfo&) const`**  
  Optional. Returns provider-specific OAuth2 info. Default implementation returns `false` (not supported).

---

## Usage Overview

1. **Define a `CloudConnection`.**
2. **Implement/Instantiate a subclass of `ICloudConnector`.**
3. **Call `TestConnection()`** to validate the connection.
4. **Call `ResolveCredentials()`** to obtain a `CloudCredentials` bundle for use in request headers.
5. If OAuth2 is used, call `GetOAuth2ProviderInfo()` for dynamic OAuth interactions.

---

## Design Notes

- Credential resolution is runtime-only; persisted credentials are managed separately.
- Supports multiple authentication paradigms.
- Easily extends for new providers (implement `ICloudConnector`).
- Suitable for both human use and automation/AIs.

---

**See Source Code for Full Licence and Implementation Details.**


---

### 15_doc_application_cloud_cloudConnectorRegistry_h

<a id="15-doc-application-cloud-cloudconnectorregistry-h"></a>
