### 34_doc_application_cloud_providerRateLimitPolicy_h

<a id="34-doc-application-cloud-providerratelimitpolicy-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/34_doc_application_cloud_providerRateLimitPolicy_h/docu.output.md`



# ProviderRateLimitPolicy (C++ Documentation)

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `cloud/providerRateLimitPolicy.h`

## Overview

`ProviderRateLimitPolicy` extends `CloudRetryPolicy` to manage provider-specific rate-limiting rules and semantics for cloud service APIs.  
It tracks request intervals, burst windows, and supports custom retry logic per provider (e.g., Slack, GitHub, Microsoft Graph, Snowflake).

This policy allows dynamic registration and enforcement of multiple providers' rate-limiting requirements.

## Core Concepts

- **Per-provider rate-limiting:** Each provider type can have separately configured min-intervals and burst limits.
- **Thread-safe:** Internal synchronization via mutex.
- **Provider extensibility:** Specific rate-limit and retry settings per API provider.

## Key Structures

### `ProviderConfig`

Configuration for a provider's rate limits and base retry policy.

| Field                     | Type                        | Description                                            |
|---------------------------|-----------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------|
| `m_MinRequestIntervalMs`  | `int`                       | Min milliseconds between two requests. Default: 0      |
| `m_BurstLimit`            | `int`                       | Max requests in a burst window; 0 = unlimited          |
| `m_BurstWindowMs`         | `int`                       | Burst window length in milliseconds. Default: 60000    |
| `m_RetryConfig`           | `CloudRetryPolicy::Config`  | Retry configuration for this provider                  |

## Public Methods

### `void RegisterProvider(const std::string& providerType, ProviderConfig config)`

Registers or updates rate-limit and retry configuration for a given provider type.

**Parameters:**
- `providerType`: Name/key of the provider (e.g., "Slack").
- `config`: Configuration object for rate limits and retry logic.

---

### `CloudRetryPolicy::Config GetRetryConfig(const std::string& providerType) const`

Returns the retry configuration for the specified provider.

**Parameters:**
- `providerType`: Name/key of the provider.

**Returns:**
- The configured `CloudRetryPolicy::Config`, or a default if not registered.

---

### `int GetThrottleDelayMs(const std::string& providerType)`

Checks if a request to the given provider should be throttled according to its rate-limiting settings.

**Parameters:**
- `providerType`: Name/key of the provider.

**Returns:**
- `0` if request can be sent immediately.
- Number of milliseconds to wait before the next allowed request if current rate limit/burst window is exceeded.

---

### `void RecordRequest(const std::string& providerType)`

Records that a request has been made to the specified provider.  
Updates internal state for interval and burst tracking.

**Parameters:**
- `providerType`: Name/key of the provider.

---

## Thread Safety

All methods are thread-safe and synchronize access via an internal mutex.

## Use Case Flow Example

1. Register provider settings with `RegisterProvider`.
2. Before making a provider API call, check `GetThrottleDelayMs`.
3. If `0` is returned, proceed and call `RecordRequest`.
4. Otherwise, wait for the specified delay.

## Typical Providers Supported

- Slack (handles Retry-After, burst, and secondary limits)
- GitHub (primary and secondary rate-limits)
- Microsoft Graph API (per-tenant throttling windows)
- Snowflake (async polling intervals)

## License

GPL-3.0 (See copyright notice)

---

© 2026 JC Technolabs

### 35_doc_application_cloud_redmineCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="35-doc-application-cloud-redminecloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/35_doc_application_cloud_redmineCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# RedmineCloudTaskExecutor Documentation

## Overview

**RedmineCloudTaskExecutor** is a C++ class for automating operations on [Redmine](https://www.redmine.org/) projects and issues over the Redmine REST API. It is designed to be plugged into workflows, supporting two primary operations:

- **Listing issues** in a Redmine project
- **Updating a Redmine issue** (add notes, assign users)

It inherits from `ICloudTaskExecutor` and is integrated into workflow frameworks (JCWF) as the `"redmine_issue"` task type.

---

## Supported Operations

### 1. list_issues

Performs a `GET /issues.json` request to Redmine, fetching issues for a single project.

- **Required Params**
    - `connection`: Named `CloudConnection` object with Redmine endpoint and API key

- **Optional Params**
    - `project_identifier`: Redmine project identifier (e.g., `"j9t-demo"`)  
      If omitted, uses the identifier in the `connection` params.
    - `status`: `"open"` (default), `"closed"`, `"*"`  
      Defaults to "open".
    - `limit`: Maximum number of issues to return. Default is 25.

- **Output**
    - Raw Redmine JSON response is written to `response.json` in the task’s working directory.

### 2. update_issue

Performs a `PUT /issues/{id}.json` request to Redmine to add notes or assign an issue.

- **Required Params**
    - `connection`: Named `CloudConnection` object with Redmine endpoint and API key
    - `issue_id`: Integer Redmine issue ID

- **Optional Params** (any combination, at least one required)
    - `notes`: Inline comment text to append to the issue
    - `notes_file`: Path to a text file containing comment text (trailing whitespace trimmed)
    - `assigned_to_id`: Numeric user ID to assign the issue to
    - `assigned_to_id_file`: Path to a file containing the user ID (one integer per line)

- Both notes and assignee can be provided in a single request. The JSON payload includes only fields present.

- **Output**
    - Raw Redmine response (or synthetic success JSON if no content returned) is written to `response.json` in the task’s working directory.

---

## Implementation Details

- Uses `curl` for HTTP requests.
- Parses and constructs JSON using `simdjson`.
- Utilities are provided for:
    - JSON string escaping
    - File reading and whitespace trimming
- Returns error messages and HTTP status codes on failure.
- Successful operation sets `taskState.m_CapturedStdout` to the first 1024 characters of the response for workflow integration.

---

## Error Handling

- Validates required parameters for each operation.
- If parsing or HTTP requests fail, populates `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` and sets task state to `Failed`.
- Writes detailed error messages in case of missing params, JSON parse errors, failed requests, or HTTP errors (with body when under 500 bytes).

---

## Usage Context

Intended for use in automated workflow platforms where tasks interact with cloud services (here, Redmine). It is **not** a general-purpose Redmine library but a workflow task executor.

---

## Example Usage

See the workflow task documentation for integrating `"redmine_issue"` tasks, supplying the relevant parameters as described above.

---

## License

GPL-3.0 (c) 2026 JC Technolabs

---

## Related Files

- `cloud/cloudTaskExecutor.h` – Parent class and task infrastructure
- `cloud/redmineConnector.h` – Base URL utility
- `simdjson/simdjson.h`, `curl/curl.h` – Dependencies

---


---

### 36_doc_application_cloud_redmineConnector_h

<a id="36-doc-application-cloud-redmineconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/36_doc_application_cloud_redmineConnector_h/docu.output.md`



# RedmineConnector Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::RedmineConnector` is a C++ class implementing the `ICloudConnector` interface to connect with a [Redmine](https://www.redmine.org/) project management server via its REST API. It supports authentication using Redmine API keys and provides helper methods for integration.

---

## Namespace

`AIAssistant`

---

## Inheritance

- Inherits from: `ICloudConnector`

---

## Usage

- Connects to a Redmine instance using the API key.
- Requires configuration of endpoint URL, key name, and authentication type.
- Used to verify connectivity and resolve credentials for Redmine integrations.

### Expected CloudConnection Members

- `m_Endpoint`: Redmine server URL (e.g., `"http://localhost:3000"`)
- `m_KeyName`: Key identifier registered in the KeyManager, containing the Redmine API key
- `m_AuthType`: Should be set to `ApiKey`
- `m_Params["project_identifier"]`: (Optional) Default project identifier, used by list operations

---

## Public Methods

### std::string GetType() const

- Returns the connector type identifier `"redmine"`.

---

### bool TestConnection(const CloudConnection& connection, std::string& errorMessage) override

- Verifies connection to Redmine by performing a GET to `/users/current.json`.
- Checks API key validity and endpoint accessibility.
- On failure, sets a descriptive reason in `errorMessage`.
- Returns `true` on success, `false` on failure.

---

### bool ResolveCredentials(const CloudConnection& connection, CloudCredentials& credentials, std::string& errorMessage) override

- Resolves and verifies credentials for connecting to Redmine.
- Fetches the API key from KeyManager using `connection.m_KeyName`.
- Populates `credentials.m_AuthType` and `credentials.m_Token` (contains the API key).
- On error, sets `errorMessage` and returns `false`.

---

### static std::string GetBaseUrl(const CloudConnection& connection)

- Returns the base URL for the Redmine instance, trimming any trailing slash.

---

## Authentication Details

- Redmine API key is sent in the `X-Redmine-API-Key` HTTP header.
- The API key must be stored in KeyManager and referenced by `m_KeyName`.
- Accept header is set to `application/json`.

---

## Error Handling

- All methods set a human-readable `errorMessage` on failure.
- Common errors: missing endpoint/key, invalid/missing API key, HTTP 401 Unauthorized, network errors.

---

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::RedmineConnector connector;
CloudConnection conn;
// set conn.m_Endpoint, conn.m_KeyName, etc.
std::string error;
if (!connector.TestConnection(conn, error)) {
    // handle error
}
```

---

## License

- GPL-3.0 License (see source header for full terms)

### 37_doc_application_cloud_s3CloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="37-doc-application-cloud-s3cloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/37_doc_application_cloud_s3CloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# S3CloudTaskExecutor C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`S3CloudTaskExecutor` is a class for executing S3 file operations (upload, download, list, delete) as cloud tasks within a workflow system. It inherits from `ICloudTaskExecutor`.

- **Namespace:** `AIAssistant`
- **Task type:** `"s3"`

## Supported Operations

Each operation is determined by the `operation` parameter. Supported values:

- **upload**: Upload a file to S3.
- **download**: Download a file from S3.
- **list**: List objects in a bucket/prefix.
- **delete**: Delete an object from S3.

## Task Parameter JSON Format

Parameters are provided as JSON in `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`:

| Key         | Required  | Description                                      |
|-------------|-----------|--------------------------------------------------|
| connection  | Yes       | Named CloudConnection to use                     |
| operation   | Yes       | `"upload"`, `"download"`, `"list"`, `"delete"`   |
| bucket      | No        | S3 bucket name (defaults to connection's bucket) |
| key         | Varies*   | S3 object key (for upload/download/delete)       |
| file_path   | Varies*   | Local file path (for upload/download)            |
| prefix      | No        | Key prefix for list operation                    |
| max_keys    | No        | Max items for list (default: 1000)               |

\* `key` and `file_path` are required for `upload` and `download`; `key` is required for `delete`.

## Class Declaration

```cpp
class S3CloudTaskExecutor : public ICloudTaskExecutor
{
public:
    using ICloudTaskExecutor::ICloudTaskExecutor;

protected:
    bool ExecuteCloud(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
                      TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState& taskState,
                      CloudConnection const& connection, CloudCredentials const& credentials,
                      TaskCancellationToken const& cancellationToken) override;
};
```

## Key Method: `ExecuteCloud`

Handles dispatch of S3 operations based on task parameters.

### Parameters

- `workflowDefinition`: Workflow metadata.
- `workflowRun`: Workflow runtime state.
- `taskDefinition`: Task definition and parameters.
- `taskState`: Task instance state mutated during execution.
- `connection`: Cloud connection details.
- `credentials`: Cloud access credentials.
- `cancellationToken`: Cancellation support.

### Behaviour

Operation is determined by the `operation` parameter:

#### Upload

- Requires `key` and `file_path`.
- Uploads the local file to S3 as the given key.

#### Download

- Requires `key` and `file_path`.
- Downloads the S3 object to the specified local path.

#### List

- Optionally accepts `prefix` and `max_keys`.
- Lists objects in the bucket or under the given prefix.

#### Delete

- Requires `key`.
- Deletes the specified object from S3.

### Errors

- Sets `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` and marks task as failed on error.
- Responses and errors are captured in `taskState`.

## Helpers

- **S3Request** – General helper for signed S3 HTTP requests using SigV4 and CURL.
- **S3Download** – Downloads an S3 object to a local file with size and error handling.

## Limitations

- Max download size: 256 MB.
- Timeout for requests: 5 minutes per operation.
- Only supports basic S3 operations: no multipart or advanced S3 features.

## Example Usage (Task JSON)

```json
{
  "connection": "myS3Conn",
  "operation": "upload",
  "bucket": "my-bucket",
  "key": "dir/object.txt",
  "file_path": "/local/path/object.txt"
}
```

## Logging and Outputs

- Logs success and failure of S3 operations.
- Writes response JSON to task working directory for workflow tracking.

## License

- GNU GPL-3.0

---

No embellishments. For details, see code and comments inline.


---

### 38_doc_application_cloud_s3Connector_h

<a id="38-doc-application-cloud-s3connector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/38_doc_application_cloud_s3Connector_h/docu.output.md`



# S3Connector Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Inherits from:** `ICloudConnector`  
**Source:** `cloud/s3Connector.h`, `cloud/s3Connector.cpp`  
**License:** GPL-3.0 (see source header for details)  

## Overview

`S3Connector` provides an interface to S3-compatible object storage services such as AWS S3, MinIO, Wasabi, and Cloudflare R2. It supports AWS Signature Version 4 (SigV4) authentication. The class enables connecting, authenticating, and basic communication (connectivity testing) with an S3 endpoint.

## Parameters (used via `CloudConnection.m_Params`):

- **"region"**: AWS region (e.g., `"us-east-1"`); required.
- **"bucket"**: Default bucket name; required for a connection test.

Other fields in `CloudConnection` relevant for S3:

- **m_Endpoint**: Custom S3 endpoint URL (for non-AWS providers). Leave empty to use AWS defaults.
- **m_KeyName**: Credential key name as recognized by KeyManager. Must resolve to access key and secret.
- **m_AuthType**: Set to `SigV4`.

## Member Functions

### std::string GetType() const
- **Returns:** `"s3"`
- **Purpose:** Returns the connector type string identifier.

---

### bool ResolveCredentials(const CloudConnection& connection, CloudCredentials& credentials, std::string& errorMessage)
- **Inputs:**
  - `connection`: Source of credential key name and connection parameters.
  - `credentials`: Output structure—populated with access key, secret, and SigV4 as auth type.
  - `errorMessage`: Output string—set if there is an error.
- **Behavior:**  
  - Retrieves key provider from KeyManager based on `m_KeyName`.
  - Supports credentials in two formats:
    1. **BasicAuthCredential:** `username = access key ID`, `password = secret key`.
    2. **ApiKeyCredential:** `api_key = "ACCESS_KEY_ID:SECRET_KEY"`.
  - On success, fills `credentials` and returns `true`.
  - On error, populates `errorMessage` and returns `false`.

---

### bool TestConnection(const CloudConnection& connection, std::string& errorMessage)
- **Inputs:**
  - `connection`: S3 connection to test.
  - `errorMessage`: Output string—set on error.
- **Behavior:**
  - Validates that required parameters (`region`, `bucket`) are set.
  - Resolves credentials using `ResolveCredentials`.
  - Builds the appropriate endpoint URL.
  - Sends a signed `GET` request to list maximum 1 object in the given bucket.
  - Uses libcurl for HTTP interaction.
  - On 4xx/5xx HTTP or curl error, sets `errorMessage` and returns `false`.
  - Returns `true` on successful test.

---

### static std::string BuildEndpointUrl(const CloudConnection& connection, const std::string& bucket)
- **Inputs:**
  - `connection`: Contains endpoint info and region parameter.
  - `bucket`: The bucket for which to build the URL.
- **Behavior:**
  - If `connection.m_Endpoint` is provided, returns that as the base URL with the bucket appended.
  - Otherwise, constructs AWS style URL: `https://{bucket}.s3.{region}.amazonaws.com`.
- **Returns:** Endpoint URL as string.

---

## Usage Notes

- Requires credentials to be managed via `KeyManager` in one of two supported formats (see **ResolveCredentials**).
- Only AWS SigV4 authentication is supported.
- Test functionality is achieved by sending a signed HTTP GET for object listing.
- Designed to be compatible with both AWS S3 and alternative S3-compatible services (MinIO, Wasabi, R2, etc.).
- CURL dependency for HTTP requests.

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::CloudConnection conn;
conn.m_Params["region"] = "us-east-1";
conn.m_Params["bucket"] = "mybucket";
conn.m_KeyName = "my-s3-creds";
// conn.m_Endpoint (optional for custom S3 provider)

AIAssistant::S3Connector s3;
std::string error;
if (s3.TestConnection(conn, error)) {
    // Connected successfully
} else {
    // Handle error
}
```

---

## Related Classes

- `ICloudConnector`: Interface class implemented.
- `CloudConnection`: Data structure for connection parameters.
- `CloudCredentials`: Structure populated with access key/secret and auth type.
- `KeyManager`: Manages secrets and credentials.
- `SigV4Signer`: Handles AWS SigV4 request signing.

---

## License

GPL-3.0. See source code header for full license text.


---

### 39_doc_application_cloud_sigV4Signer_h

<a id="39-doc-application-cloud-sigv4signer-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/39_doc_application_cloud_sigV4Signer_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::SigV4Signer

## Overview

`AIAssistant::SigV4Signer` is a C++ utility for generating AWS Signature Version 4 (SigV4) request signatures, supporting authentication for S3-compatible APIs. It produces the `Authorization` HTTP header and required `X-Amz-*` headers for signed requests, using OpenSSL for cryptographic primitives (SHA-256, HMAC-SHA256).

---

## Main Features

- Generates SigV4 signed headers for S3.
- Provides methods for SHA-256 digest and url encoding.
- Can handle custom HTTP headers and payload hashing.
- All logic encapsulated in a single static class.

---

## Public Interface

### Types

#### `struct SignedRequest`

- **m_Headers**: `std::map<std::string, std::string>`
    - Headers to add to the HTTP request (including `Authorization`, `X-Amz-Date`, `X-Amz-Content-Sha256`, and `Host`).

---

### Methods

#### `static SignedRequest Sign(const std::string& method, const std::string& url, const std::string& region, const std::string& service, const std::string& accessKeyId, const std::string& secretKey, const std::string& payloadHash, const std::map<std::string, std::string>& extraHeaders = {});`

Generates SigV4 signed headers for an HTTPS S3 (or compatible) API request.

**Parameters:**
- `method`: HTTP method (`GET`, `PUT`, `DELETE`, `HEAD`)
- `url`: Complete resource URL (e.g., `https://bucket.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/key`)
- `region`: AWS region (e.g., `us-east-1`)
- `service`: AWS service name (for S3, use `"s3"`)
- `accessKeyId`: AWS access key ID
- `secretKey`: AWS secret access key
- `payloadHash`: SHA-256 hex digest of the request body (use `"UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD"` for streaming)
- `extraHeaders`: Additional headers to include in the signature (optional)

**Returns:**
- `SignedRequest`: Struct containing HTTP headers for authentication.

---

#### `static std::string Sha256Hex(const std::string& data);`

Returns the SHA-256 hash of the input data, as a lowercase hex string.

---

#### `static const std::string& EmptyPayloadHash();`

Returns the SHA-256 hex digest of an empty string. Used for unsigned or empty payloads.

---

## Private/Internal Methods

- **HmacSha256**: Computes HMAC-SHA256, returning raw bytes.
- **HmacSha256Hex**: Computes HMAC-SHA256, returns result as hex string.
- **ParseUrl**: Parses a URL into host, path, and query components.
- **UriEncode**: URL-encodes a string as per AWS rules.
- **CanonicalQueryString**: Produces a sorted, percent-encoded query string.
- **GetAmzDate**: Returns current UTC timestamp in `YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ` format.
- **GetDateStamp**: Returns current UTC date in `YYYYMMDD` format.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
using namespace AIAssistant;

auto headers = SigV4Signer::Sign(
    "GET",
    "https://bucket.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/key",
    "us-east-1",
    "s3",
    "<access-key-id>",
    "<secret-access-key>",
    SigV4Signer::EmptyPayloadHash()
);
// Add headers.m_Headers to your HTTP request
```

---

## License

GPL-3.0 (JC Technolabs, 2026)

### 40_doc_application_cloud_slackCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="40-doc-application-cloud-slackcloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/40_doc_application_cloud_slackCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# SlackCloudTaskExecutor

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Base class:** `ICloudTaskExecutor`

## Overview

`SlackCloudTaskExecutor` executes workflow tasks that interact with the Slack Web API. It supports two task types:

1. **slack_message** (POST `/api/chat.postMessage`)  
   Sends a message to a Slack channel.

2. **slack_read** (GET `/api/conversations.history`)  
   Reads recent messages from a Slack channel.

This class allows integration of Slack messaging into automated workflows.

---

## Supported Task Types

### 1. `slack_message`

**Description:**  
Sends a message to a specified Slack channel using the Slack API.

**Parameters:**
- `connection` (required): Named CloudConnection object.
- `channel` (required): Slack channel name (`#alerts`) or ID (`C01ABCDEF`).
- `text` (required unless `text_file` is set): Message text.
- `text_file` (optional): Read message text from file.
- `thread_ts` (optional): If set, posts in thread as a reply to this message timestamp.
- `thread_ts_file` (optional): Read `thread_ts` from file.

**Behavior:**
- Reads parameters from JSON.
- Loads text and/or thread timestamp from file if requested.
- Sends a POST request to `/api/chat.postMessage`.
- On success, writes response JSON to the task's working directory.

### 2. `slack_read`

**Description:**  
Reads recent messages from a Slack channel history.

**Parameters:**
- `connection` (required): Named CloudConnection.
- `channel` (required): Slack channel ID (must start with 'C', not `#name`).
- `limit` (optional, default 10): Maximum number of messages to fetch.
- `oldest` (optional): Only return messages newer than this timestamp.
- `exclude_bots` (optional, default `true`): If true, filters out bot messages.

**Outputs (in the task's working directory):**
- `messages_summary.json`: Array of `{ts, user, text}` for each fetched message.
- `latest_message.txt`: Text content of the most recent message returned.
- `latest_ts.txt`: Timestamp of the most recent message.
- `response.json`: Contains `{ok, count, channel, latest_ts}`.

---

## Main Methods

### ExecuteCloud

**Signature:**
```cpp
bool ExecuteCloud(WorkflowDefinition const&, WorkflowRun&, TaskDef const&, TaskInstanceState&, CloudConnection const&, CloudCredentials const&, TaskCancellationToken const&) override;
```

**Description:** Dispatches task execution to `ExecuteSlackPost` or `ExecuteSlackRead` based on task type.

---

### ExecuteSlackPost

**Description:**  
Handles sending messages to Slack (`slack_message` type).  
- Reads parameters and loads files as needed.
- Sends POST requests using the given Slack API token.
- Reports errors via `taskState`.
- Writes API response to task directory.

---

### ExecuteSlackRead

**Description:**  
Handles reading Slack channel history (`slack_read` type).  
- Reads parameters.
- Fetches messages via the Slack API with token.
- Filters messages (e.g., excluding bots).
- Collects data, writes JSON and text files to the task directory.

---

## Internal Utilities

- **JsonEscape:** Safely escapes strings for embedding in JSON.
- **ReadFileToString:** Reads the entire contents of a file.
- **TrimTrailing:** Removes trailing whitespace and newlines.
- **PerformSlackRequest:** Makes authenticated HTTP requests to Slack, handles errors.
- **CheckSlackOk:** Parses Slack's response JSON to check operation success.

---

## Error Handling

- On error, `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage` is set and `taskState.m_State` is set to `Failed`.
- Validates required parameters and proper channel format.
- Catches HTTP and Slack API errors, including invalid JSON responses.

---

## Usage Notes

- Requires a valid Slack OAuth token in the supplied `CloudCredentials`.
- For reading channels, use Slack channel IDs (e.g., `C01ABCDEF`), not names like `#alerts`.
- Output and response files are written to each task's working directory for downstream workflow steps.

---

## License

- GPL-3.0 (see source code header for full license grant and warranty disclaimer).

---

## Example

```json
{
  "type": "slack_message",
  "params": {
    "connection": "my_slack_connection",
    "channel": "#alerts",
    "text": "Alert: process completed"
  }
}
```

```json
{
  "type": "slack_read",
  "params": {
    "connection": "my_slack_connection",
    "channel": "C01ABCDEF",
    "limit": 5,
    "exclude_bots": true
  }
}
```

---


---

### 41_doc_application_cloud_slackConnector_h

<a id="41-doc-application-cloud-slackconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/41_doc_application_cloud_slackConnector_h/docu.output.md`



# SlackConnector Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`

**Header:** `cloud/slackConnector.h`

## Purpose

`SlackConnector` is a connector for integrating with the Slack Web API. It enables authentication, credential resolution, and connection testing for Slack bots using the Slack API.

## Usage

Create and configure a `CloudConnection` object specifying the endpoint (optional), API key name (required, must be a Slack Bot token), and authentication type (must be BearerToken). Instantiate and use `SlackConnector` to interface with Slack.

## Class Members

### Static Constants

- **DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL:**  
  `static constexpr char const* DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL = "https://slack.com/api";`  
  Default base URL for Slack API endpoints.

### Methods

#### `GetType() const`

- **Returns:** `std::string`
- **Purpose:** Identifies the type of connector.  
- **Value:** Always returns `"slack"`.

#### `TestConnection(const CloudConnection& connection, std::string& errorMessage)`

- **Returns:** `bool`
- **Purpose:** Tests whether the supplied `CloudConnection` configuration (and its API key) is valid by performing an API call to `auth.test`.
- **On Success:** Returns `true`.
- **On Failure:** Returns `false`, sets `errorMessage` with details (credential errors, HTTP/cURL failures, or Slack auth errors).

#### `ResolveCredentials(const CloudConnection& connection, CloudCredentials& credentials, std::string& errorMessage)`

- **Returns:** `bool`
- **Purpose:** Resolves and validates API credentials for Slack. Extracts the API key (bot token) from the `KeyManager` corresponding to the connection's key name.
- **On Success:** Sets `credentials` fields appropriately (AuthType = BearerToken, Token = Slack bot token), returns `true`.
- **On Failure:** Returns `false`, sets `errorMessage` (e.g., missing key, no API key, or key not found).

#### `GetApiBaseUrl(const CloudConnection& connection)`

- **Returns:** `std::string` (static)
- **Purpose:** Returns the Slack API base URL for the provided connection. Uses connection's endpoint if set; otherwise defaults to `DEFAULT_API_BASE_URL`.

## CloudConnection Configuration

- `m_Endpoint`: (optional) Slack API base URL (default is `"https://slack.com/api"`).
- `m_KeyName`: (required) Name of the credential in the `KeyManager` (must reference a valid Slack Bot token).
- `m_AuthType`: Must be `BearerToken`.

## Dependencies

- Requires cURL for HTTP requests.
- Uses [simdjson](https://simdjson.org/) for JSON parsing.
- Depends on `KeyManager` for credential resolution.
- Depends on a proper CA bundle for SSL validation (configured in `CurlWrapper`).

## Error Handling

- All error conditions result in a `false` return value and a descriptive `errorMessage`.
- Errors may include missing credentials, HTTP or network failures, invalid tokens, or Slack API error responses.

## Example (Pseudocode)

```
AIAssistant::CloudConnection conn;
conn.m_KeyName = "my_slack_bot";
conn.m_AuthType = CloudAuthType::BearerToken;

AIAssistant::SlackConnector connector;
std::string error;
if (connector.TestConnection(conn, error)) {
    // Connection to Slack is valid.
} else {
    // Handle error.
}
```

## License

GPL-3.0 (see class header for full license text).

### 42_doc_application_cloud_snowflakeCloudTaskExecutor_h

<a id="42-doc-application-cloud-snowflakecloudtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/42_doc_application_cloud_snowflakeCloudTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# SnowflakeCloudTaskExecutor C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`SnowflakeCloudTaskExecutor` is a class that executes Snowflake SQL queries as tasks, interacting with the Snowflake SQL REST API. It implements the `ICloudTaskExecutor` interface, enabling integration into workflow engines that use JSON-specified task parameters.

## Purpose

- Allows automated or programmatic execution of Snowflake SQL statements.
- Supports exporting query results as CSV or JSON files.
- Handles workflow task state, error reporting, and task cancellation.
- Integrates with named cloud connections and supports credential handling.

## Class Declaration

```cpp
class SnowflakeCloudTaskExecutor : public ICloudTaskExecutor
```

## Related Task Type

- **JCWF task type:** `"snowflake_query"`

## Task Parameter JSON Schema

Parameters are provided as a JSON object in the task definition.  
**Keys:**

| Key            | Type   | Required | Description                                                    |
|----------------|--------|----------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
| connection     | string | yes      | Named CloudConnection (from config or system)                  |
| query          | string | yes      | SQL statement to execute                                       |
| warehouse      | string | no       | Override default warehouse from connection                     |
| database       | string | no       | Override default database from connection                      |
| schema         | string | no       | Override default schema from connection                        |
| output_format  | string | no       | Output format: `"csv"` (default) or `"json"`                   |
| output_file    | string | no       | Output file name (default: `result.csv` or `result.json`)      |
| timeout        | int    | no       | Statement timeout in seconds (default: 3600)                   |
| poll_interval  | int    | no       | Polling interval in seconds (default: 2)                       |

## Main Functionality

### Execution Steps

1. **Parse Task Parameters:** Reads and validates task parameters from JSON.
2. **Prepare Context:** Determines warehouse, database, and schema (task params override connection defaults).
3. **Build Query Request:** Escapes SQL for JSON, constructs request body.
4. **Submit Query:** Issues POST request to Snowflake API to execute the SQL statement.
5. **Poll for Result:** If result is not immediately ready, polls the API until statement execution completes or a timeout/cancellation occurs.
6. **Process Results:** Extracts columns and rows from the response.
7. **Write Output:** Exports results to the specified file in CSV or JSON format.
8. **Set Task State:** Updates task state and summary (e.g., success, failure, cancellation).
9. **Error Handling:** Provides detailed messages on failures, timeouts, or API errors.
10. **Cancellation:** Supports mid-execution cancellation requests via a cancellation token.

### Output

- The query result is written to an output file in the specified format (`csv` or `json`).
- Task state includes a summary JSON: 
  - `{"ok":true,"rows":<row_count>,"columns":<column_count>,"output":"<output_file>"}`
- The raw Snowflake response is also saved to `response.json` in the working directory.

## Output Files

- Main data file: as specified by `output_file` (e.g., `result.csv` or `result.json`)
- Raw API response: `response.json` for debugging/tracing.

## Key Internal Methods

- `ExecuteCloud(...)`: Orchestrates the full task lifecycle, from parameter parsing to result saving.
- `SnowflakeRequest(...)`: Helper method for authenticated Snowflake API HTTP requests.
- `CsvEscape(...)`: Escapes fields according to RFC 4180 for CSV output.

## Error Handling

- Invalid, missing, or bad parameters cause immediate task failure with informative messages.
- API errors, connectivity faults, or file I/O errors are reported in the task state.
- Statement timeouts and cancellation are handled gracefully, including Snowflake-side cancellation.

## Integration

- Designed for use within AI Assistant workflow infrastructures or similar workflow/task engines.
- Requires valid Snowflake CloudConnection and credentials.
- Depends on external libraries: `curl` (HTTP), `simdjson` (JSON parsing), and file I/O facilities.

## Example Task JSON

```json
{
  "type": "snowflake_query",
  "params": {
    "connection": "snowflake_prod",
    "query": "SELECT * FROM users",
    "output_format": "csv",
    "output_file": "users.csv"
  }
}
```

## See Also

- `ICloudTaskExecutor` interface
- `SnowflakeConnector` helper class
- [Snowflake SQL REST API documentation](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/developer-guide/sql-api)

### 43_doc_application_cloud_snowflakeConnector_h

<a id="43-doc-application-cloud-snowflakeconnector-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/43_doc_application_cloud_snowflakeConnector_h/docu.output.md`



# SnowflakeConnector C++ Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `cloud/snowflakeConnector.h`  
**Implements:** `ICloudConnector`  
**Purpose:**  
Connector for Snowflake data warehouses using the Snowflake SQL REST API with RSA JWT authentication. Enables connection validation and Snowflake credentials resolution for automated workflows and assistants.

---

## CloudConnection Parameter Reference

- **Required Parameters (`m_Params`):**
  - `account` - Snowflake account identifier (e.g. `"xy12345"`)
  - `user` - Snowflake user name (e.g. `"SVC_JARVIS"`)

- **Optional Parameters (`m_Params`):**
  - `warehouse` - Default warehouse name (e.g. `"COMPUTE_WH"`)
  - `database` - Default database name (e.g. `"ANALYTICS"`)
  - `schema` - Default schema name (e.g. `"PUBLIC"`)

- **Other Required Fields:**
  - `m_Endpoint` - Snowflake account locator with region (e.g. `"xy12345.us-east-1"`)
  - `m_KeyName` - Name of credential in KeyManager (must store RSA private key PEM)
  - `m_AuthType` - Set to `JwtRsa`

---

## Class Members and Functions

### `std::string GetType() const override;`
Returns the string identifier for this connector, `"snowflake"`.

---

### `static std::string BuildApiBaseUrl(const std::string& endpoint);`
Builds the base URL for the Snowflake REST API from the account endpoint/locator.

- **Input:**  
  `endpoint` - Snowflake account locator, with or without protocol

- **Output:**  
  API base URL in the format:  
  `https://{endpoint}.snowflakecomputing.com`

---

### `bool ResolveCredentials(const CloudConnection& connection, CloudCredentials& credentials, std::string& errorMessage) override;`
Resolves and prepares JWT RSA credentials for Snowflake authentication.

- **Checks performed:**
  - Validates presence of credential key (`m_KeyName`)
  - Validates that the credential exists in KeyManager and has an RSA private key
  - Ensures `account` and `user` parameters are provided
  - Generates a JWT for Snowflake with 1-hour expiry

- **On success:**  
  - Sets `credentials.m_AuthType` to `JwtRsa`
  - Sets `credentials.m_Token` to the generated JWT

- **On failure:**  
  - Returns `false` and sets `errorMessage` with diagnostic text

---

### `bool TestConnection(const CloudConnection& connection, std::string& errorMessage) override;`
Tests connectivity to Snowflake using the provided connection and credentials.

- **Procedure:**
  1. Validates the endpoint parameter.
  2. Resolves credentials via `ResolveCredentials`.
  3. Builds Snowflake API URL.
  4. Sends an HTTP POST to `/api/v2/statements` with a `SELECT 1` SQL statement in the request body.
  5. Optionally includes warehouse, database, and schema if specified.
  6. Interprets HTTP response code and returns success/failure.

- **On success:**  
  - Returns `true`

- **On failure:**  
  - Returns `false` and sets `errorMessage` with details (e.g., authentication error, HTTP code, connection failure)

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::CloudConnection conn;
conn.m_Params["account"] = "xy12345";
conn.m_Params["user"] = "SVC_JARVIS";
conn.m_Endpoint = "xy12345.us-east-1";
conn.m_KeyName = "my_snowflake_rsa";
conn.m_AuthType = CloudAuthType::JwtRsa;

AIAssistant::SnowflakeConnector connector;

std::string error;
if (connector.TestConnection(conn, error)) {
    // Connection successful
} else {
    // Handle error: error
}
```

---

## Error Handling

- Functions return `false` on error and populate `errorMessage` with a human-readable description.
- Common failure causes include missing parameters, credential misconfiguration, or failed HTTP authentication.

---

## Dependencies

- Uses `curl` for HTTP requests.
- Uses `simdjson` for JSON handling.
- Requires a KeyManager providing RSA private keys.
- Uses `JwtGenerator` to create Snowflake-compatible JWT tokens.

---

## License

GPL-3.0. See file header for details.

---

## Summary

`SnowflakeConnector` streamlines authenticated connections to Snowflake using industry-standard JWT-based RSA credentials. It validates configuration, generates tokens, and verifies connection health, supporting warehouse/database/schema context when available.


---

### 44_doc_application_cloud_taskCancellationToken_h

<a id="44-doc-application-cloud-taskcancellationtoken-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/44_doc_application_cloud_taskCancellationToken_h/docu.output.md`



# TaskCancellationToken Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::TaskCancellationToken` provides a cooperative cancellation mechanism for long-running operations, such as cloud tasks. It enables operations to check for cancellation requests and exit early if cancellation has been signaled.

## Usage

- Instantiate a `TaskCancellationToken` and pass it to long-running operations.
- The operation periodically checks `IsCancelled()`.
- Any thread may signal cancellation by calling `Cancel()`.

## Class Reference

### Constructors

- **TaskCancellationToken()**
  - Initializes a new, non-cancelled token.

### Public Methods

- **bool IsCancelled() const**
  - Returns `true` if cancellation has been requested; otherwise, `false`.
- **void Cancel()**
  - Signals cancellation. All future calls to `IsCancelled()` return `true`.

### Private Members

- **std::atomic<bool> m_Cancelled{false}**
  - Stores the cancellation state in a thread-safe manner.

## Thread Safety

All operations on `TaskCancellationToken` are thread-safe due to the use of `std::atomic`.

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::TaskCancellationToken token;

// In the task
while (!token.IsCancelled()) {
    // Perform work
}

// From another thread
token.Cancel();
```

## License

GPL-3.0 (see source header for terms)

### 45_doc_application_content_chunkPlanner_h

<a id="45-doc-application-content-chunkplanner-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/45_doc_application_content_chunkPlanner_h/docu.output.md`



# ChunkPlanner C++ Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `content/chunkPlanner.h`  
**License:** GPL-3.0  
**Copyright:** (c) 2026 JC Technolabs  

## Overview

The `ChunkPlanner` class provides utilities for splitting a large text body into smaller, structure-aware chunks that fit within a specified token budget. The main purpose is to divide markdown content into optimal sections for use with AI models or algorithms that require input length limits.

## Features

- Splits text while preserving markdown section boundaries, prioritizing clean breaks at `#`, `##`, and `###` headers.
- Ensures each chunk does not exceed the allowed context token budget (minus prompt overhead).
- If a section alone is too large, further subdivides content by paragraphs and sentences.
- Uses a standard heuristic to estimate token usage: `tokens ≈ chars / 4`.
- Returns a single chunk if the input fits within the budget.

## Public API

### static `uint64_t EstimateTokens(const std::string& text);`

- **Purpose:** Estimates the number of tokens in a given string, using the heuristic: number of characters divided by 4 (rounded up).
- **Parameters:**  
  - `text`: Input string to be analyzed.
- **Returns:** Estimated number of tokens as `uint64_t`.

### static `std::vector<std::string> Plan(const std::string& body, uint64_t maxContextTokens, uint64_t promptOverheadTokens);`

- **Purpose:** Splits a markdown-formatted string into chunks, each fitting within the available token budget after accounting for prompt overhead.
- **Parameters:**  
  - `body`: The full input text (markdown).
  - `maxContextTokens`: Total available token budget.
  - `promptOverheadTokens`: Number of tokens reserved for prompt/context outside the body content.
- **Returns:** Vector of chunk strings (as `std::string`), each within budget limits. If the body fits, returns a single-element vector.

## Splitting Strategy

1. **Estimate total token usage.**
2. **If entire body fits budget:** Return as a single chunk.
3. **Else:**
   - Split body at markdown sections, respecting header hierarchy.
   - For oversized sections, recursively subdivide by:
     - Paragraphs (blank lines)
     - Sentences (`.`, `!`, `?`, or line breaks)
   - Pack as many pieces as possible into chunks without exceeding the token budget.

## Usage Example

```cpp
#include "content/chunkPlanner.h"
using namespace AIAssistant;

std::string markdownBody = "# Title\n\n## Part 1\nContent...";
uint64_t maxTokens = 2048, promptOverhead = 200;

auto chunks = ChunkPlanner::Plan(markdownBody, maxTokens, promptOverhead);
for (const auto& chunk : chunks) {
    // process each chunk
}
```

## Dependencies

- `content/markdownSectionSplitter.h` for markdown-aware splitting.
- C++ STL: `<cstdint>`, `<string>`, `<vector>`, `<sstream>`, `<algorithm>`

## Notes

- Empty or zero-budget input returns the whole input as a single chunk.
- A minimum chunk size of 128 tokens is enforced for practical splitting.
- Splitting is structure-aware and maintains as much semantic integrity as possible.

## License

GPL-3.0. See copyright.

---


---

### 46_doc_application_content_markdownSectionSplitter_h

<a id="46-doc-application-content-markdownsectionsplitter-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/46_doc_application_content_markdownSectionSplitter_h/docu.output.md`



# MarkdownSectionSplitter Documentation

## Overview

`MarkdownSectionSplitter` is a C++ class in the `AIAssistant` namespace that splits a Markdown document into logical sections based on ATX-style headings (`#`, `##`, `###`, etc). The splitter is aware of fenced code blocks and ignores headings within them.

## Data Structures

### MarkdownSection

Represents a segment of a Markdown document.

- `int m_HeadingLevel` — Heading level (0 for preamble, 1–6 for `#` through `######`)
- `std::string m_HeadingText` — Text of the heading (empty for preamble)
- `std::string m_Body` — Section content, including its heading line for levels > 0

## Class: MarkdownSectionSplitter

### Methods

#### static std::vector<MarkdownSection> Split(const std::string& markdown, int maxHeadingLevel = 6)

Splits a Markdown document into sections using headings up to `maxHeadingLevel` (1 to 6).

- **Parameters:**
  - `markdown`: Source Markdown text.
  - `maxHeadingLevel`: Maximum heading level to consider for splitting (defaults to 6).
- **Returns:**  
  - A vector of `MarkdownSection` objects. Each represents a section.  
  - Headings within fenced code blocks (```` ``` ```` or `~~~`) are ignored.
  - Heading line is included at the start of each section's body for heading levels > 0.
  - If no splittable headings are found up to `maxHeadingLevel`, returns the entire document as a single section.

#### Splitting Logic

- Scans Markdown line by line.
- Recognizes ATX-style headings outside of code fences as section splits.
- Ignores headings inside code fences.
- Chooses the lowest heading level ≤ `maxHeadingLevel` that yields more than one section.

## Usage Example

```cpp
#include "content/markdownSectionSplitter.h"

using namespace AIAssistant;

std::string markdown = "# Title\nContent\n## Section\nMore content";
auto sections = MarkdownSectionSplitter::Split(markdown, 2);

// sections will contain two MarkdownSection objects: one for "# Title", one for "## Section".
```

## License

Copyright (c) 2026 JC Technolabs  
License: GPL-3.0

See source code for details.

### 47_doc_application_file_fileWatcher_h

<a id="47-doc-application-file-filewatcher-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/47_doc_application_file_fileWatcher_h/docu.output.md`



# FileWatcher Class Documentation

**Namespace**: `AIAssistant`

**Header**: `fileWatcher.h`

## Overview

The `FileWatcher` class monitors one or more filesystem directories for file changes. It can report when files are created, modified, or deleted in real time by pushing corresponding events to an event queue. Its design supports both a fixed "primary root" directory and dynamic addition/removal of watched directories.

---

## Constructor

```cpp
FileWatcher(const fs::path& pathToWatch, std::chrono::milliseconds interval = 100ms);
```

- `pathToWatch`: The primary root directory to watch. If empty, only paths added via `AddPath()` are monitored.
- `interval`: Scan interval in milliseconds (default: 100ms).

---

## Destructor

```cpp
~FileWatcher();
```

- Stops the watcher and cleans up resources.

---

## Public Methods

### Start

```cpp
void Start();
```

Starts monitoring the registered directories for file changes.

---

### Stop

```cpp
void Stop();
```

Stops the watcher, blocking until the background monitor thread has terminated.

---

### SignalStop

```cpp
void SignalStop();
```

Signals the watcher to stop (non-blocking).

---

### WaitStop

```cpp
void WaitStop();
```

Waits until the watcher thread has stopped.

---

### AddPath

```cpp
bool AddPath(fs::path const& root);
```

Registers an additional directory to be watched.

- Thread-safe and idempotent (adding duplicate paths has no effect).
- Returns `false` if the path is empty, does not exist, or is a duplicate.
- The new root will be watched starting from the next scan interval.
- The primary root (given at construction) cannot be added via this method.

---

### RemovePath

```cpp
bool RemovePath(fs::path const& root);
```

Unregisters a directory previously added by `AddPath`.

- Thread-safe.
- Does not remove the primary root.
- Does not fire file-removed events for files under the removed root; the caller is responsible for cleanup signaling.
- Returns `false` if the path is empty or not currently registered.

---

## Event Generation

- **FileAddedEvent**: When a new regular file appears.
- **FileModifiedEvent**: When a watched file's modification timestamp changes.
- **FileRemovedEvent**: When a previously-watched file is deleted.
- **EngineEvent(Shutdown)**: If the primary root directory is deleted, triggers an engine shutdown event.

Events are pushed to an event queue managed by the core application.

---

## Private Logic

- Maintains a set of watched directories (primary + additional).
- Uses atomic and mutex synchronization for thread safety.
- Keeps a map of filenames to their last modification times to detect changes.
- Ignores hidden files (filenames starting with a dot).
- Gracefully handles removal of roots that may have been deleted from disk.

---

## Usage Notes

- A watcher with an empty primary root never triggers engine shutdown if the root disappears and relies solely on `AddPath`/`RemovePath` for managing directories.
- Removing a path only stops watching it; to handle cleanup of associated files, higher-level code must handle events as appropriate.
- The class is designed for integration in event-driven engines.

---

## Threading

All public methods are thread-safe. File watching runs in a background task.

---

## Typical Use Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::FileWatcher watcher("/path/to/folder");
watcher.Start();
watcher.AddPath("/another/folder");
// ...
watcher.Stop();
```

---

## Dependencies

- C++17 (`<filesystem>`, `<atomic>`, `<condition_variable>`, etc.)
- Project-specific event system and core infrastructure.

---


---

### 48_doc_application_file_scriptRegistry_h

<a id="48-doc-application-file-scriptregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/48_doc_application_file_scriptRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::ScriptRegistry Documentation

## Overview

`ScriptRegistry` manages a dynamic registry of script files (Python `.py`, Shell `.sh`, PowerShell `.ps1`) by scanning directories, parsing script metadata from header comments, and providing lookup and serialization features. It is intended for environments where scripts are organized, described, and discoverable, such as AI assistants or automation platforms.

---

## ScriptRegistryEntry Struct

Holds extracted metadata for a single script.

| Field                | Type                        | Description                                           |
|----------------------|-----------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------|
| `m_FilePath`         | `std::string`               | Script file path                                      |
| `m_Short`            | `std::string`               | Short description (from `@short` tag)                 |
| `m_Description`      | `std::string`               | Full description (from `@description` tag)            |
| `m_Params`           | `std::vector<std::string>`  | Parameters (from `@params` tags)                      |
| `m_Outputs`          | `std::vector<std::string>`  | Outputs (from `@outputs` tags)                        |
| `m_ExportedFunctions`| `std::vector<std::string>`  | Names of top-level functions exported by the script   |

---

## Script File Metadata Parsing

A script is recognized and registered if it has a header containing the `@jarvis-script` marker and a `@short` field within the first 20 lines (up to line 50 is parsed). Supported fields are:

- `@short`         – short description/title (**required**)
- `@params`        – parameters (multiple allowed)
- `@description`   – extended description
- `@outputs`       – outputs (multiple allowed)

In Python/Shell scripts, top-level function definitions are also listed.

---

## Class API

### void ScanDirectory(const std::filesystem::path& scriptsDir)
- Scans all `.sh`, `.py`, and `.ps1` scripts in the specified directory recursively.
- Extracts header metadata and registers valid scripts.
- Nonexistent or invalid directories are handled gracefully.

### void AddOrUpdate(const std::filesystem::path& filePath)
- Re-parses a specified script file, updating or adding it in the registry.
- If the marker or required fields are missing, unregisters the script.

### void Remove(const std::filesystem::path& filePath)
- Removes the specified script from the registry.

### std::string SerializeMarkdownTable() const
- Returns a Markdown-formatted table listing all registered scripts with file path, short description, and parameters.
- For documentation, UI, or AI context injection.

### std::vector<ScriptRegistryEntry> GetEntries() const
- Returns a vector snapshot of all registry entries.
- Useful for REST APIs or programmatic access.

### size_t Size() const
- Returns the number of registered scripts.

### const ScriptRegistryEntry* FindByModulePath(const std::string& modulePath) const
- Looks up a script by Python-style module path (e.g., `scripts.parseLog` maps to `scripts/parseLog.py`).

### const ScriptRegistryEntry* FindByFilePath(const std::string& filePath) const
- Looks up a script by (relative) file path (e.g., `scripts/parseLog.py`).

---

## Thread Safety

All registry operations are protected by a mutex. Read and write accesses are safe for concurrent use.

---

## Script Header Example

```sh
# @jarvis-script
# @short: Parse log file for errors
# @params: file_path
# @description: Reads a log file and extracts error entries.
# @outputs: error_list
```

---

## Supported Script Types

- Python (`.py`)
- Shell (`.sh`)
- PowerShell (`.ps1`) *(extension only, parsing focuses on Python/Shell)*

---

## Limitations

- Only the first 50 lines are checked for metadata.
- `@short` is required; scripts without it are ignored.
- Currently, function scanning for exported functions is only detailed for Python and Shell scripts.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::ScriptRegistry registry;
registry.ScanDirectory("scripts/");
auto entries = registry.GetEntries();
std::string markdown = registry.SerializeMarkdownTable();
auto entry = registry.FindByModulePath("tools.cleanup");
```

---

## License

MIT License (see source code for details).


---

### 49_doc_application_jarvisAgent_h

<a id="49-doc-application-jarvisagent-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/49_doc_application_jarvisAgent_h/docu.output.md`



# JarvisAgent Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Inherits:** `Application`

---

## Overview

`JarvisAgent` is the main application class for the AIAssistant platform. It manages initialization, updates, events, and shutdown of all subsystems critical for AI-powered workflow automation. It integrates workflow management, scripting, status reporting, web UI, trigger handling, and cloud connectors.

---

## Key Responsibilities

- Application lifecycle: start, update, event handling, shutdown
- Registers and manages internal task executors and workflow engines
- Scans and watches script and workflow directories
- Manages web server and API/WebSocket dashboard
- Coordinates cloud service connectors and Python engine pool
- Orchestrates trigger handling (file events, cron, etc.)
- Tracks status and runtime metrics for UI

---

## Main Methods

### Constructors & Destructor

- `JarvisAgent()`
- `virtual ~JarvisAgent()`

### Lifecycle (Inherited from Application)

- `void OnStart() override`  
  Initializes all subsystems, sets up directory watchers, task executors, cloud connectors, workflow registry, runtime manager, triggers, and the web server.

- `void OnUpdate() override`  
  Periodically updates submodules (AI request pool, triggers, workflow runtime) and checks for application termination.

- `void OnEvent(std::shared_ptr<Event>&) override`  
  Handles application-level events (engine shutdown, file events, Python crash, AI call events). Routes file events to triggers and script registry; forwards events to Python engine pool as needed.

- `void OnShutdown() override`  
  Signals all subsystems to stop, waits for their termination, and performs orderly cleanup. Provides diagnostic log messages during shutdown.

- `bool IsFinished() const override`  
  Returns true if the application is finished/shutting down.

### Static

- `static std::unique_ptr<Application> Create()`  
  Factory method, returns new `JarvisAgent` instance.

---

## Accessors

- `WebServer* GetWebServer() const`  
  Returns web server instance.

- `std::chrono::system_clock::time_point GetStartupTime() const`  
  Returns startup time.

- `int64_t GetStartupTimestamp() const`  
  Returns startup timestamp (nanos since epoch).

- `StatusRenderer& GetStatusRenderer()`  
  Returns status renderer for dashboard/TUI.

- `PythonEnginePool* GetPythonEnginePool()`  
  Returns Python engine pool instance.

- `WorkflowRegistry* GetWorkflowRegistry()`  
  Returns workflow registry.

- `ScriptRegistry* GetScriptRegistry()`  
  Returns script registry.

- `WorkflowFileIndex* GetWorkflowFileIndex()`  
  Returns workflow file index.

- `AiRequestPool* GetAiRequestPool() const`  
  Returns AI request pool.

- `CurlMultiDispatcher* GetCurlMultiDispatcher() const`  
  Returns Curl dispatcher for external HTTP tasks.

- `WorkflowRuntimeManager* GetWorkflowRuntimeManager() const`  
  Returns manager for workflow runs.

- `IInternalTaskRegistry* GetInternalTaskRegistry()`  
  Returns internal task registry (for registering custom tasks).

---

## Important Private Members

- `StatusRenderer m_StatusRenderer`  
  Tracks metrics and status for UI.

- `std::chrono::system_clock::time_point m_StartupTime`  
  Startup time point.

- Submodules (all as `std::unique_ptr` unless noted):
  - FileWatcher (`m_ScriptFileWatcher`)
  - ScriptRegistry (`m_ScriptRegistry`)
  - WebServer (`m_WebServer`)
  - PythonEnginePool (`m_PythonEnginePool`)
  - WorkflowRegistry (`m_WorkflowRegistry`)
  - TriggerEngine (`m_TriggerEngine`)
  - InternalTaskRegistry (`m_InternalTaskRegistry`)
  - AiRequestPool (`m_AiRequestPool`)
  - CurlMultiDispatcher (`m_CurlMultiDispatcher`)
  - WorkflowRuntimeManager (`m_WorkflowRuntimeManager`)
  - WorkflowFileIndex (`m_WorkflowFileIndex`)
  - SubWorkflowTaskExecutor (`m_SubWorkflowExecutor`, shared_ptr)

- `bool m_IsFinished`  
  Indicates if the application is finished.

---

## Key Behavior

- **OnStart:**  
  - Captures startup time.
  - Registers internal tasks.
  - Hooks status renderer to terminal/status UI.
  - Starts submodules: script file watcher, script registry, workflow index, web server, Python pool, AI request pool, Curl dispatcher.
  - Loads workflows, bind triggers, registers task executors and cloud connectors.
  - Connects all modules for dashboard reporting and event communication.

- **OnEvent:**  
  - Handles shutdown and subsystem events.
  - Processes file changes for scripts/triggers.
  - Routes events to Python engine pool.

- **OnShutdown:**  
  - Signals all subsystems to terminate.
  - Waits for orderly shutdown, cleans up resources.

---

## Associated Class

**App**  
- Singleton-like static: `JarvisAgent* g_App` — global pointer to the current application instance.

---

## Usage Notes

- Designed to be the main entrypoint for the assistant application.
- Manages all critical services required for interactive and web-driven workflow automation.
- Integrates extensibility for new cloud connectors, task types, and triggers.
- All configuration and setup is performed on `OnStart`; component-safe shutdown in `OnShutdown`.

---

## See Also

- `Application` (base class)
- `StatusRenderer`
- `WebServer`
- `PythonEnginePool`
- `WorkflowRegistry`
- `TriggerEngine`
- `ScriptRegistry`
- Registered task executors and cloud connectors

---

Licensed under the MIT License.

### 50_doc_application_json_jcwfGenerationGuide_generated_h

# Documentation: `AIAssistant::kJcwfGenerationGuide`

## Summary

`AIAssistant::kJcwfGenerationGuide` is a compile-time constant string containing the full "JCWF Generation Guide". This guide is a detailed, structured reference for authoring JC Workflow (JCWF) JSON files, covering task types, dependencies, data and control flow, filters, triggers, best practices, and common pitfalls.

The guide is intended for both human users and AI agents involved in generating or validating workflow files for the JC Workflow engine. It provides everything needed to create, understand, or troubleshoot modern `.jcwf` workflow containers.

## Declaration

```cpp
namespace AIAssistant {
    inline constexpr char const* kJcwfGenerationGuide = R"JCWF(...JCWF markdown...)JCWF";
}
```

- **Namespace:** `AIAssistant`
- **Type:** `inline constexpr char const*`
- **Value:** Large Markdown document (specification/instructions for workflow files)

## Intended Use

- As an embedded reference/knowledge base for developer tools, code assistants, LLM agents, or workflow IDEs.
- For fast, local, in-memory access to the authoritative JC Workflow file format documentation (no parsing external files at runtime).
- For immediate lookup, generation, and validation of workflow tasks, structures, and idioms.

## Content Highlights

- **Comprehensive schema for JCWF JSON root objects and tasks**
- **Supported task types:** shell, ai_call, python, internal, sub_workflow, cloud task types (db, storage, slack, email, etc)
- **Workflow mechanics:** dependencies, dataflow, manual and automatic triggers
- **Error handling:** branch nodes, error signaling, control flow edges
- **Per-item (array-driven) tasks using filters**
- **Best practices and common mistakes to avoid**
- **Multiple rich, real-world annotated examples**

## Technical Notes

- The guide is stored using a raw string (`R"JCWF(...)"`) for correct preservation of formatting (especially tables and code blocks).
- The docstring is auto-generated; do not edit by hand.
- You can access or embed the guide directly via the symbol `AIAssistant::kJcwfGenerationGuide`.

## Example Usage

```cpp
#include "AIAssistant/JcwfGenerationGuide.h"
std::cout << AIAssistant::kJcwfGenerationGuide << std::endl;
```

- Use in any context requiring programmatic access to the full JCWF specification.

## Limitations

- This variable only holds documentation (informational Markdown). No parsing, validation, or logic is provided.
- File contents are static—updates require regeneration via the build system.

## License

- Copyright (c) 2026 JC Technolabs
- License: GPL-3.0

---

**Generated by:** `premake5.lua prebuild`
