### 62_doc_application_python_pythonEngine_h

<a id="62-doc-application-python-pythonengine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/62_doc_application_python_pythonEngine_h/docu.output.md`



# PythonEngine Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::PythonEngine` provides a dedicated Python sub-interpreter with its own Global Interpreter Lock (GIL) and worker thread for running Python tasks in parallel. It is designed for reliable, concurrent execution of Python code (including workflow tasks and event hooks) within a C++/Python integration context.

Multiple instances can be managed (see: PythonEnginePool) to support load balancing and full parallelism.

---

## Key Responsibilities

- Manages a Python sub-interpreter and its thread.
- Queues and executes workflow tasks and event hooks (`OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, `OnEvent`, `OnShutdown`).
- Ensures safe interaction with Python via thread-safe queues and GIL handling.
- Supports hot-reloading of Python modules/functions.
- Redirects Python stdout/stderr to the C++ logging system and captures outputs.
- Provides synchronous and asynchronous API for submitting tasks.

---

## Main Types

### `WorkflowTaskRequest`

Struct carrying all inputs/outputs for a workflow task, including:
- Task definition (`TaskDef`), working directory, input/context values.
- Output values (after execution), captured stdout/stderr, error message, and success flag.
- Synchronization via `std::promise`.

### `PythonTask`

Struct encapsulating a queued item, either an event or a workflow task.

---

## Main API

### Construction / Lifecycle

- `explicit PythonEngine(size_t engineIndex)`
- `~PythonEngine()`

### Interpreter / Thread Management

- `bool SetupSubInterpreter(const std::string& scriptDir, const std::string& moduleName, bool loadHooks)`  
  Initializes the Python sub-interpreter, sys.path, stdout/stderr, and optionally loads a hook module.
- `void SetInterpreterState(PyInterpreterState* interpState)`  
  Set interpreter state (called by PythonEnginePool).
- `void StartWorkerThread()`  
  Launches the dedicated worker thread for this interpreter.
- `void SignalStop()`, `void WaitStop()`  
  Initiate and wait for a clean shutdown.

### Event Hooks

- `void OnStart()`, `void OnUpdate()`, `void OnEvent(std::shared_ptr<Event> eventPtr)`  
  Enqueue the call of `OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, or `OnEvent` hook (if defined in Python).

### Workflow Task Execution

- `bool ExecuteWorkflowTask(const TaskDef& taskDefinition, const std::string& taskWorkingDirectory, const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& inputValues, const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& contextValues, std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& outputValuesOut, std::string& errorMessage, std::string& capturedStdout, std::string& capturedStderr)`  
  Submits a workflow task to the worker thread and blocks until complete.  
  Returns true on success. Handles all GIL and error propagation, captures outputs, and injects context.

### Monitoring

- `bool IsRunning() const`
- `size_t GetQueueDepth() const`
- `size_t GetEngineIndex() const`
- `size_t GetTasksCompleted() const`

---

## Internal Methods

- `void WorkerLoop()`  
  The main loop run by the worker thread; pulls from the task queue and executes Python or event hooks under proper GIL management.
- `void EnqueueTask(const PythonTask& task)`  
  Thread-safe enqueue of a task.
- `void CallHook(PyObject* function, const char* hookName)`  
  Call a Python event hook (no args).
- `void CallHookWithEvent(PyObject* function, const char* hookName, const Event& event)`  
  Call an event hook, passing an event as a dict.
- `PyObject* BuildEventDict(const Event& event)`  
  Convert a C++ Event to a Python dictionary.
- `void ExecuteWorkflowTaskOnWorker(std::shared_ptr<WorkflowTaskRequest> const& request)`  
  Worker-side execution of workflow tasks, including input/output marshalling, error capture, hot-reloading, stdout/stderr capture, and context injection.

---

## Threading and Concurrency

- All communication between the main thread and the Python interpreter occurs via a `std::queue<PythonTask>` guarded by a mutex and condition variable.
- Each PythonEngine executes only one task at a time, but parallelism is achieved with multiple engines.
- Uses `std::promise`/`std::future` to synchronize workflow task completion.
- Queue is drained at shutdown to avoid deadlocks.

---

## Error Handling

- All exceptions, both at the C++ and Python levels, are captured and reported via error messages.
- Python-side errors are caught and made available in `m_ErrorMessage`, and detailed logs are produced.
- If the engine stops/aborts, queued tasks are notified of failure.

---

## Python Integration Details

- Interpreter setup includes:
  - Redirection of stdout/stderr so Python print/logging integrates with C++ logging.
  - Manipulation of `sys.path` for module discovery and package import support.
  - Optional import and management of special event hooks (`OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, etc).
- Module/function hot-reload is supported by removing modules from `sys.modules` before import.
- Task execution marshals all input values as keyword arguments; context values are always available via a `context` dict.

---

## Key Constraints & Requirements

- All tasks and hooks must be Python-callable and obey expected signatures.
- Returned values must be a dict (string keys), or None.
- Engine must be running (`IsRunning()` returns `true`) to accept tasks.
- Requires correct dependency setup (`Python.h`, proper linkage).

---

## Usage Pattern

1. Create and initialize the PythonEngine via `SetupSubInterpreter`.
2. Start the worker thread with `StartWorkerThread`.
3. Submit hooks or workflow tasks (`OnStart`, `OnUpdate`, `ExecuteWorkflowTask`, etc).
4. Call `SignalStop` and `WaitStop` for clean shutdown.

---

## Thread Safety & Ownership

- `PythonEngine` methods are safe to call from user threads unless noted (all public API methods are either thread-safe or use internal queueing).
- Ownership of task and event pointers passed to the queue is via `std::shared_ptr`.

---

## Example: Executing a Workflow Task

```
std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string> input;
std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string> context;
std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string> output;
std::string error, out, err;

TaskDef taskDef = ...; // Must include valid m_ParamsJson with "module" and "function" fields

bool ok = engine.ExecuteWorkflowTask(
    taskDef,
    "/path/to/workingdir",
    input,
    context,
    output,
    error,
    out,
    err
);
if (!ok) {
    // handle error, see 'error' message
}
```

---

## Summary Table

| API                     | Function                                                                                     |
|-------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| SetupSubInterpreter     | Setup GIL, sys.path, redirect I/O, (optionally) load hooks                                  |
| StartWorkerThread       | Launch worker for queue processing                                                           |
| EnqueueTask             | Thread-safe add to task queue                                                                |
| OnStart                 | Queue call to Python `OnStart()` hook                                                       |
| OnUpdate                | Queue call to Python `OnUpdate()` hook                                                      |
| OnEvent                 | Queue call to Python `OnEvent(event)` hook                                                  |
| SignalStop/WaitStop     | Clean shutdown of worker/thread                                                              |
| ExecuteWorkflowTask     | Submit a workflow task, block for result, get output/err/stdout/err                         |
| GetQueueDepth           | Returns number of unprocessed tasks                                                          |
| GetTasksCompleted       | Returns number of processed tasks since start                                                |
| IsRunning               | Returns engine running state                                                                 |

---

## See Also

- `AIAssistant::PythonEnginePool` - Manages a pool of `PythonEngine` instances.
- Python/C API (`Python.h`) for low-level interpreter integration.
- Logging and error reporting infrastructure.

---

## License

MIT License (see file headers for details).


---

### 63_doc_application_python_pythonEnginePool_h

<a id="63-doc-application-python-pythonenginepool-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/63_doc_application_python_pythonEnginePool_h/docu.output.md`



# PythonEnginePool Class Documentation

**Namespace**: `AIAssistant`  
**Header**: `pythonEnginePool.h`  
**Purpose**: Manages a pool of Python sub-interpreters for concurrent workflow task execution.

---

## Overview

`PythonEnginePool` manages multiple instances of `PythonEngine`, each backed by a distinct CPython sub-interpreter. Supports load-balanced dispatch of workflow tasks and routes event hooks to a primary engine. On Python 3.12+, each engine can have a separate GIL (PEP 684); on older versions, falls back to a single engine with a shared GIL.

---

## Key Features

- **Parallel Python Execution:** Creates N sub-interpreters (subject to Python version capabilities).
- **Load-Balanced Task Dispatch:** Tasks are assigned to the engine with the smallest queue.
- **Event Hook Routing:** Lifecycle hooks (OnStart, OnUpdate, OnEvent, OnShutdown) are handled by the primary engine only.
- **Graceful Shutdown:** Provides mechanisms to signal and wait for shutdown of all engines.

---

## Python Version Compatibility

- **Python ≥ 3.12:** Uses `Py_NewInterpreterFromConfig` (PEP 684) to create multiple sub-interpreters with shared or isolated GIL based on config.
- **Python < 3.12:** Automatically restricts to a single engine using legacy `Py_NewInterpreter`. No true concurrency due to shared GIL.

---

## Public API

### Constructor / Destructor

- **PythonEnginePool()**: Default constructor.
- **~PythonEnginePool()**: Destructor. Calls `Stop()` if engines are running.

### Engine Pool Management

- **bool Initialize(const std::string& scriptPath, size_t engineCount)**  
  Initializes the engine pool. Creates `engineCount` sub-interpreters using the specified Python script as the module entry point. Returns `true` on success.

- **void Stop()**  
  Gracefully stops all engines (calls `SignalStop` and `WaitStop`).

- **void SignalStop()**  
  Signals all engines to stop.

- **void WaitStop()**  
  Waits for all engines to finish stopping.

### Lifecycle Hooks (Primary Engine Only)

- **void OnStart()**  
  Calls the primary engine's `OnStart()`.

- **void OnUpdate()**  
  Calls the primary engine's `OnUpdate()`.

- **void OnEvent(std::shared_ptr<Event> eventPtr)**  
  Passes an event to the primary engine only.

### Task Execution

- **bool ExecuteWorkflowTask(const TaskDef& taskDefinition,  
  const std::string& taskWorkingDirectory,  
  const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& inputValues,  
  const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& contextValues,  
  std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& outputValuesOut,  
  std::string& errorMessage,  
  std::string& capturedStdout,  
  std::string& capturedStderr)**  

  Dispatches a workflow task to the least busy engine. Executes the given Python workflow. Populates output and error streams on failure. Returns `true` on success.

### Status and Information

- **bool IsRunning() const**  
  Returns `true` if the engine pool is active.
- **size_t GetEngineCount() const**  
  Returns the number of active Python engines.
- **size_t GetTasksCompleted(size_t engineIndex) const**  
  Returns the number of tasks completed by the engine at the given index.

---

## Internal Logic

- **Load Balancing:** `SelectEngine()` chooses the engine with the lowest queue depth.
- **Sub-interpreter Setup:** Each PythonEngine instance is set up with isolated state (sys.modules, globals, etc.).
- **Thread Safety:** Each worker thread has its own thread state for its interpreter.
- **Event Routing:** Only index 0 (primary engine) receives lifecycle event hooks.

---

## Shutdown Procedure

1. Signals each engine to stop (`SignalStop`).
2. Waits for each engine to cleanly shut down (`WaitStop`).
3. Clears engine vector and resets running state.

---

## Error Handling

- Initialization and interpreter creation errors are logged and halt further setup.
- On misconfiguration (e.g., `engineCount == 0`), logs an error and returns `false`.
- All API methods are designed to be safe to call even when the pool is not running.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::PythonEnginePool pool;
if (pool.Initialize("user_script.py", 4)) {
    // Execute tasks...
    pool.Stop();
}
```

---

## Related Classes / Structures

- **PythonEngine:** Represents a single sub-interpreter instance and worker thread.
- **Event:** Encapsulates events for the OnEvent lifecycle hook.
- **TaskDef:** Structure defining a workflow task.

---

## License

MIT License (see code header for details).

---

## Summary Table

| Method                                   | Purpose                                         |
|:------------------------------------------|:------------------------------------------------|
| `Initialize`                             | Initialize engine pool and sub-interpreters     |
| `Stop` / `SignalStop` / `WaitStop`       | Stop all engines cleanly                        |
| `ExecuteWorkflowTask`                    | Dispatch a workflow task                        |
| `OnStart` / `OnUpdate` / `OnEvent`       | Lifecycle hooks (primary engine)                |
| `IsRunning` / `GetEngineCount`           | Pool status/query                               |
| `GetTasksCompleted(size_t)`              | Per-engine completed task count                 |

---

**Contact**: JC Technolabs (copyright)


---

### 64_doc_application_session_fileWriter_h

<a id="64-doc-application-session-filewriter-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/64_doc_application_session_fileWriter_h/docu.output.md`



# FileWriter Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`

## Overview

`FileWriter` is a singleton class for writing text content to files with thread safety. It handles directory creation, logging, and error checking. The class provides two main methods: writing plain content and writing content with a header.

---

## Class Interface

### Singleton Access

- **static FileWriter& Get()**  
  Returns the singleton instance of `FileWriter`.

---

### Writing Methods

- **void Write(const std::filesystem::path& filePath, const std::string& content)**  
  Writes `content` to the file at `filePath`.  
  - Thread-safe.
  - Automatically creates the parent directory if it does not exist.
  - Logs events and errors.

- **void WriteWithHeader(const std::filesystem::path& filePath, const std::string& content, const std::string& model)**  
  Writes `content` to `filePath`, intended for cases where a header needs to be included (implementation writes only the content as provided).  
  - Thread-safe.
  - Automatically creates the parent directory.
  - Logs events and errors.

---

## Thread Safety

- All write operations are protected by a mutex, ensuring thread-safe access.

---

## Construction & Copy Control

- **Private constructor and destructor.**
- **Copy constructor and assignment operator are deleted.**  
  Ensures only one instance exists (singleton pattern).

---

## Logging & Error Handling

- Logs folder creation attempts, outcomes, file writing, and any errors encountered during file operations.
- Uses exceptions to handle errors that may occur during write operations.

---

## Dependencies

- C++ Standard Library: `<string>`, `<mutex>`, `<filesystem>`, `<fstream>`, `<chrono>`, `<iomanip>`
- External logging functions/macros: `LOG_APP_INFO`, `LOG_APP_ERROR` (must be defined elsewhere)

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
#include "session/fileWriter.h"

AIAssistant::FileWriter::Get().Write("output.txt", "Hello, world!");
AIAssistant::FileWriter::Get().WriteWithHeader("output_with_header.txt", "Content", "ModelName");
```

---

## License

MIT License (see header in source code).

---

## Notes

- Parent directories will be created as needed.
- Failed file operations are logged; check logs for diagnostics.
- `WriteWithHeader` currently does not format or add a header, only writes the provided content.


---

### 65_doc_application_task_carMaintenanceTask_h

<a id="65-doc-application-task-carmaintenancetask-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/65_doc_application_task_carMaintenanceTask_h/docu.output.md`



# CarMaintenanceTask C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::CarMaintenanceTask` is a concrete task class that processes car maintenance requests based on structured JSON input. It determines whether the request is related to the engine, tires, or is unclear, and writes an appropriate response to an output file.

## Inheritance

- Inherits from `ITask`.

## Public Methods

### CarMaintenanceTask()
Default constructor.

### ~CarMaintenanceTask()
Default destructor.

### bool Execute(const std::vector<std::filesystem::path>& inputFilePaths,  
                 const std::vector<std::filesystem::path>& outputFilePaths,  
                 std::string& errorMessageOut) override

**Purpose:**  
Executes the car maintenance task.

**Parameters:**
- `inputFilePaths` – Vector of input file paths. The first file is required and expected to be a structured JSON classification.
- `outputFilePaths` – Vector of output file paths. The first file is where the result will be written.
- `errorMessageOut` – Populated with error details if execution fails.

**Returns:**  
- `true` on success.
- `false` if there are invalid files, I/O errors, parsing errors, or an unexpected category.

## Private Static Methods

### bool TryReadAllText(const std::filesystem::path& filePath,  
                       std::string& fileContentsOut,  
                       std::string& errorMessageOut)

Reads entire content of a file into `fileContentsOut`.  
Returns `true` on success, `false` and fills `errorMessageOut` on failure.

### bool TryWriteAllText(const std::filesystem::path& filePath,  
                        const std::string& fileContents,  
                        std::string& errorMessageOut)

Writes `fileContents` to the specified file.  
Creates parent directories if needed.  
Returns `true` on success, `false` and fills `errorMessageOut` on failure.

### bool ExtractCategoryFromStructuredJson(const std::string& fileContents,  
                                          std::string& categoryOut,  
                                          std::string& errorMessageOut)

Parses the input JSON and extracts the `category` string.  
Category must be `"engine"`, `"tires"`, or `"rephrase"`.  
Returns `true` on success, `false` if parsing fails or the schema is invalid.

### std::string MakeEngineManualText()

Returns a predefined string with engine maintenance quick notes.

### std::string MakeTireMaintenanceText()

Returns a predefined string with tire maintenance quick notes.

### std::string MakeRephraseRequestText()

Returns a predefined string requesting clarification for ambiguous queries.

## Workflow

1. **Input Validation**: Ensures both `inputFilePaths` and `outputFilePaths` are non-empty.
2. **Read Input**: Loads structured JSON from the first input file.
3. **Category Extraction**: Parses the JSON and extracts the `category` field.
4. **Output Generation**:
   - `"engine"`: Writes engine manual notes.
   - `"tires"`: Writes tire maintenance notes.
   - `"rephrase"`: Writes a clarification request.
   - Any other value: Fails with an error.
5. **Write Output**: Writes the selected response text to the first output file.

## Error Handling

- All methods return `false` and provide details in `errorMessageOut` if they encounter any error (file not found, read/write error, invalid JSON, missing/invalid category).

## Dependencies

- C++17 or later.
- `<string>`, `<filesystem>`, `<fstream>`, `<system_error>`
- SIMDJSON library for fast JSON parsing.
- Inherits `ITask` interface (from `"task/taskBase.h"`).

## Usage Example

- Input: JSON file with structure `{ "category": "<engine|tires|rephrase>" }`
- Output: Text file containing predefined maintenance response.

---

**License:** MIT License (see file header for full terms).


---

### 66_doc_application_task_internalTaskRegistry_h

<a id="66-doc-application-task-internaltaskregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/66_doc_application_task_internalTaskRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



# InternalTaskRegistry Class Documentation

## Overview

`InternalTaskRegistry` is a class in the `AIAssistant` namespace that manages the registration and creation of internal task objects based on string action identifiers. It enables dynamic association between action names and task factory functions, supporting thread-safety for concurrent access.

## Key Types

- **TaskFactory:**  
  A type alias for `std::function<std::unique_ptr<ITask>()>`. It defines a factory function that creates instances of objects implementing `ITask`.

## Public Methods

### Constructor

- **InternalTaskRegistry()**  
  Default constructor. Initializes an empty registry.

### Destructor

- **~InternalTaskRegistry() override**  
  Default destructor. Cleans up resources.

### RegisterFactory

- **void RegisterFactory(const std::string& action, const TaskFactory& factory)**  
  Registers a factory function for the specified action name.  
  - **Parameters:**  
    - `action`: The unique string identifier for a task type.  
    - `factory`: The factory function that creates a new `ITask` instance.  
  Thread-safe; can be called from multiple threads.

### CreateTask

- **std::unique_ptr<ITask> CreateTask(const std::string& action) const override**  
  Creates and returns a new `ITask` instance tied to the provided action name.  
  - **Parameters:**  
    - `action`: The string identifier of the desired task type.  
  - **Returns:**  
    - A unique pointer to a new `ITask` object, or `nullptr` if the action is not registered.  
  Thread-safe; can be called from multiple threads.

## Private Members

- **mutable std::mutex m_Mutex**  
  Ensures thread-safe access to internal data.

- **std::unordered_map<std::string, TaskFactory> m_Factories**  
  Maps action names to their corresponding task factory functions.

## Inheritance

- Implements the `IInternalTaskRegistry` interface, providing task creation via `CreateTask`.

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::InternalTaskRegistry registry;

// Register a task factory
registry.RegisterFactory("analyze", []() { return std::make_unique<AnalyzeTask>(); });

// Create a task
auto task = registry.CreateTask("analyze");
if (task) {
    task->Execute();
}
```

## Thread Safety

All public methods are thread-safe.

## License

GPL-3.0 (see source header for details).


---

### 67_doc_application_task_taskBase_h

<a id="67-doc-application-task-taskbase-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/67_doc_application_task_taskBase_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::ITask Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::ITask` is an abstract base class defining an interface for task execution within the AIAssistant namespace. It facilitates creating tasks that process input files and produce output files, with error reporting.

## Public Member Functions

### Destructor

```cpp
virtual ~ITask() = default;
```
Ensures proper cleanup in derived classes.

### Execute

```cpp
virtual bool Execute(
    std::vector<std::filesystem::path> const& inputFilePaths,
    std::vector<std::filesystem::path> const& outputFilePaths,
    std::string& errorMessageOut
) = 0;
```

**Description**:  
Executes the task with specified input and output file paths.

**Parameters**:
- `inputFilePaths`: Vector of input file paths required for the task.
- `outputFilePaths`: Vector of output file paths to be generated by the task.
- `errorMessageOut`: Reference to a string that receives a human-readable error message if execution fails.

**Returns**:
- `true` if the task executes successfully.
- `false` if execution fails; in that case, `errorMessageOut` contains the error message.

**Note**:  
`Execute` is a pure virtual function and must be implemented by derived classes.

## Usage

Inherit from `AIAssistant::ITask` and implement the `Execute` method to define custom task behavior that reads from input files, processes data, and writes to output files, while handling errors by populating `errorMessageOut`.

## Namespace

`AIAssistant`

## Dependencies

- `<filesystem>`
- `<string>`
- `<vector>`


---

### 68_doc_application_web_aiJcwfService_h

<a id="68-doc-application-web-aijcwfservice-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/68_doc_application_web_aiJcwfService_h/docu.output.md`



# AiJcwfService Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Location:** `web/aiJcwfService.h` and `web/aiJcwfService.cpp`  
**Thread Safety:** All public methods are thread-safe.

---

## Overview

`AiJcwfService` is a service class for AI-powered explanation and generation of JCWF (JSON Control Workflow) data. The class provides:

- Asynchronous conversion of JCWF JSON to human-readable summaries.
- Asynchronous generation of JCWF JSON from natural language prompts.
- Automatic validation and repair of generated workflows/scripts.
- Fixing failed scripts with AI assistance.
- Testing AI interfaces for reachability and correctness.
- Progress and results communicated via WebSocket broadcast callback.
- All operations run on background threads.

---

## Public APIs

### Constructor & Destructor

```cpp
AiJcwfService();
~AiJcwfService();
```
- Default constructor and destructor. Destructor calls `Shutdown()` for resource cleanup.

---

### Deleted Copy/Move Operations

```cpp
AiJcwfService(AiJcwfService const&) = delete;
AiJcwfService& operator=(AiJcwfService const&) = delete;
```

Copy and move are disabled.

---

### Broadcast Callback

```cpp
void SetBroadcastFn(BroadcastFn broadcastFn);
```
_Set the callback function used to broadcast JSON messages to clients (e.g., WebSocket)._

---

### Explaining JCWF

```cpp
void ExplainAsync(std::string const& jcwfJsonText);
```
_Asynchronously explain JCWF JSON in natural language. Progress and result are sent via broadcast callback as `ai-explain-progress` and `ai-explain-result` events._

---

### Generating JCWF

```cpp
void GenerateAsync(std::string const& prompt, std::string const& currentJcwfJson);
```
_Asynchronously generate a JCWF JSON file from a natural language prompt. If `currentJcwfJson` is non-empty, modifies the existing workflow. Progress and result are sent via broadcast callback as `ai-generate-progress` and `ai-generate-result` events._

---

### Fix Failed Script

```cpp
void FixFailedScriptAsync(std::string const& scriptPath, std::string const& stderrContent, std::string const& taskType);
```
_Asynchronously repair a failed script, using the script path and stderr content. Broadcasts progress and result via `ai-fix-script-progress` and `ai-fix-script-result`._

---

### Test AI Interface

```cpp
bool TestAiInterface(size_t interfaceIndex,
                     std::string& outResponsePreview,
                     std::string& outError,
                     int64_t& outLatencyMs);
```
_Blocking call. Sends a basic test prompt to a configured AI interface (by index). Returns success status, preview of the response (first ~200 chars), error message (if any), and request latency (ms)._

---

### Shutdown

```cpp
void Shutdown();
```
_Signals all background threads to terminate and waits for them to join. Called automatically in destructor._

---

### JCWF Validation

```cpp
static bool ValidateJcwf(std::string const& jcwfJsonText,
                         std::string& outValidationSummary,
                         ScriptRegistry const* scriptRegistry = nullptr,
                         std::vector<GeneratedScript> const* pendingScripts = nullptr,
                         std::vector<WorkflowValidationIssue>* outIssues = nullptr);
```
_Validates JCWF JSON. Returns `true` if valid (no errors). Writes a formatted summary of errors/warnings to `outValidationSummary`. Optionally accepts a `ScriptRegistry` for script checks, a scripts list, and returns detailed issues._

---

## Key Behaviors

### AI Request Handling

- All AI-powered operations are dispatched to a background thread.
- Each operation creates a subfolder in the AI request pool’s queue directory for replay/debug.
- Operations build an AI invocation envelope (with input files, schema, and context) and submit via `AiRequestPool`.
- Results and progress messages are broadcast to connected clients via the registered callback.

---

### Workflow Generation (GenerateAsync)

- Decomposes natural language prompt into tasks.
- Produces JCWF in a single AI call, or batch/fan-out if there are many tasks.
- Merges batch results.
- Early validation: catches and attempts to auto-fix path/structural issues before script generation.
- Generates scripts referenced in the JCWF if missing.
- Scripts are generated, validated, and auto-fixed if possible.
- Final JCWF is validated; errors/warnings auto-fixed (single or targeted patch).
- Re-validation ensures all critical issues are addressed before returning results.

---

### Script Fixing (FixFailedScriptAsync)

- Reads failed script from disk and gathers its stderr output.
- Invokes the AI with precise instructions and runtime error context to repair the script.
- Returns the fixed script for review (does not overwrite without user approval).

---

### Explanation (ExplainAsync)

- Two-stage explanation: base summary, then review/refinement with JCWF specification awareness.
- Ensures concise, structure-preserving, accurate, and reproducible description.

---

## Thread Safety

- All public methods are safe to call concurrently from any thread.
- Internal mutexes protect broadcast and thread management.

---

## Internal Helpers & Concepts

- **BroadcastFn**: `std::function<void(const std::string&)>` used to send JSON event/data to clients.
- **ScriptRegistry**: Used for metadata and script signature validation.
- **GeneratedScript/WorkflowValidationIssue**: Models script artifacts and validation errors.
- **RunSingleAiCall**: Core method for assembling input, building the AI envelope, submitting, and collecting the AI's response.
- **Validation/Fixing Cycle**: If errors/warnings are detected, the class uses targeted AI fix passes before returning results.

---

## Error Handling

- All AI operations check shutdown state before and after major steps.
- Errors in any async call are caught, logged, and broadcast with the appropriate error/event type.
- All issues and fixes are tracked and reported in detail for transparency.

---

## Events and Result Communication

All result and progress messages are delivered as JSON to all registered WebSocket clients via the `BroadcastFn`. Event types include:

- `ai-explain-progress` / `ai-explain-result`
- `ai-generate-progress` / `ai-generate-result`
- `ai-fix-script-progress` / `ai-fix-script-result`

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::AiJcwfService svc;
svc.SetBroadcastFn([](const std::string& msg) {
    // Send to WebSocket
});
svc.GenerateAsync("Write a workflow that downloads a file and parses it", "");
// Progress and results delivered asynchronously via callback.
```

---

## Extensibility

- Pluggable AI backends/interfaces via config.
- Extendable validation via ScriptRegistry integration.
- All logic designed for interactive web-based (or API) assistant tools.

---

## See Also

- `AiRequestPool`
- `ScriptRegistry`
- `WorkflowValidationIssue`

---

## License

MIT License (c) 2025 JC Technolabs

---

## Summary Table

| Method                      | Function                                                         |
|-----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `SetBroadcastFn`            | Set WebSocket or client broadcast handler                        |
| `ExplainAsync`              | Explain JCWF to natural language                                 |
| `GenerateAsync`             | Generate JCWF from prompt (optionally modify existing JCWF)      |
| `FixFailedScriptAsync`      | Fix failed script using AI, includes error output                |
| `TestAiInterface`           | Probe AI interface for API connectivity                          |
| `Shutdown`                  | Shut down all background activity                               |
| `ValidateJcwf` (static)     | Validate JCWF JSON, return summary and issues                    |

---

*This class is central to JCWF-oriented AI workflow generation, validation, and assistant integration. It is a critical bridge between interactive assistant/UX and backend AI reasoning for workflow development and troubleshooting.*


---

### 69_doc_application_web_mcpKeyManager_h

<a id="69-doc-application-web-mcpkeymanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/69_doc_application_web_mcpKeyManager_h/docu.output.md`



# McpKeyManager Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Purpose:** Secure, persistent management of MCP API keys and enrollment tokens, with encryption-at-rest. Designed for managing per-user identity, roles, disk quotas, and audit information.

## Overview

`McpKeyManager` provides storage, enrollment, activation, authentication, renewal, updating, and revocation of API keys used by users and systems. All secrets are encrypted on disk with AES-256-GCM (via PBKDF2), and only secure hashes of keys are stored. Enrollment tokens allow admin-approved, audited self-service key issuance.

## Key Concepts

- **Raw API Key:**  
  Format: `mcp_` + 64 hex chars (32 random bytes). Only shown to user once at issue/renewal.
- **Key ID:**  
  Format: `mcp_` + 8 hex chars (prefix of full key). Safe for logs/metadata.
- **Enrollment Token:**  
  Format: `enroll_` + 64 hex chars. Used for audit-controlled key creation.
- **Encryption:**  
  All key/token records are encrypted-at-rest. Only SHA-256 hashes of secrets are stored.

## Structures

### Record

Describes a stored API key and its metadata.
- `m_KeyId`: Key ID (`mcp_abcdefgh`)
- `m_KeyHash`: SHA-256 hash (hex) of full API key
- `m_User`: Identity (e.g., `alice@company.com`)
- `m_Role`: User role (`admin`, `operator`, `viewer`)
- `m_AdhocEnabled`: Whether adhoc jobs allowed
- `m_DiskQuotaMb`: Disk quota in MB
- `m_DefaultCleanupPolicy`: Cleanup policy ID
- `m_CreatedAt`: Created timestamp (ISO-8601 UTC)
- `m_ExpiresAt`: Expiry timestamp (ISO-8601 UTC)
- `m_LastUsedAt`: Last used timestamp (ISO-8601 UTC); empty if never
- `m_Enabled`: Whether key is active
- `m_Description`: Free-text notes

### EnrollmentRequest

Describes an enrollment token creation request.
- `m_User`, `m_Role`, `m_AdhocEnabled`, `m_DiskQuotaMb`, `m_DefaultCleanupPolicy`, `m_Description`: As above
- `m_KeyExpiryDays`: How long the eventual API key will be valid (default: 90 days)
- `m_EnrollmentTtlMinutes`: How long this token is valid for activation (default: 30 min)
- `m_CreatedBy`: Requesting admin identity

### EnrollmentRecord

Stored enrollment token (encrypted-at-rest, SHA-256 hash stored).
- All fields correspond with `EnrollmentRequest` plus:
- `m_ExpiresAt`: When this token expires
- `m_CreatedAt`: Timestamp

### UpdateFields

Partial update structure for `UpdateKey`.
- Each member is `std::optional`, so only fields present are applied.

## Main Methods

### Storage Lifecycle

- `bool Load(path, masterPassword)`: Load encrypted key store from disk.
- `bool Save(path, masterPassword)`: Save encrypted key store to disk.
- `bool IsLoaded() const`: Returns true if store is loaded.

### Enrollment and Key Issuance

- `std::string CreateEnrollment(const EnrollmentRequest&)`:  
  Admin issues a new enrollment token for a user. Returns the one-time-use token.
- `std::optional<ActivateResult> ActivateEnrollment(const std::string& token)`:  
  Activates an enrollment token, creating the API key. Returns key material if successful.

### API Key Authentication and Renewal

- `std::optional<AuthResult> Authenticate(const std::string& rawKey)`:  
  Authenticate a supplied API key. Returns metadata and days-to-expiry, if valid.
- `std::optional<ActivateResult> CreateBootstrapAdminKey()`:  
  Create the **first** `admin` key directly (only allowed if the store is empty).
- `std::optional<RenewResult> SelfRenew(const std::string& currentRawKey)`:  
  User self-service key rotation. Issues new key, sets 24h grace period on old.

### Admin Operations

- `std::vector<Record> ListKeys() const`:  
  List all stored keys (hashes only, no API secrets).
- `bool UpdateKey(const std::string& keyId, const UpdateFields&)`:  
  Update metadata for a key.
- `bool RevokeKey(const std::string& keyId)`:  
  Remove key from the store (permanent).

### Disk Quota Tracking

- `void RecordDiskUsage(const std::string& user, size_t bytes)`:  
  Update disk usage for a user.
- `size_t GetDiskUsage(const std::string& user) const`:  
  Query disk usage for a user.

### Enrollment Cleanup

- `void PurgeExpiredEnrollments()`:  
  Remove enrollment tokens whose TTLs have elapsed.

## Security

- All secrets (API keys, enrollment tokens) are only stored as SHA-256 hashes.
- Encryption-at-rest is enforced via `KeyEncryption` (AES-256-GCM + PBKDF2).
- Key IDs are safe to log and reference, but never reveal the full key.
- First admin key can only be created if the key store is empty. All subsequent keys use the enrollment process.

## Usage Pattern

1. **Bootstrap:**  
   - Call `CreateBootstrapAdminKey()` after initial master password setup to get the first admin key.
2. **Create Enrollment:**  
   - Admin creates token with `CreateEnrollment()`, sends to user.
3. **Activate Key:**  
   - User activates with `ActivateEnrollment(token)`, receives API key.
4. **Authenticate:**  
   - API key is verified via `Authenticate(key)`.
5. **Renew:**  
   - User can rotate their own key with `SelfRenew(oldKey)`.

## Thread Safety

- All public and internal state-modifying functions are protected by `std::mutex`.

## Notes

- Key and enrollment secret values are **only** displayed at creation.
- Expiry and last-used timestamps are ISO-8601 UTC strings.
- All update and removal operations are tracked by key ID (never by raw key).
- The store is versioned (JSON field `"version": 1`).

---

**Copyright (c) 2026 JC Technolabs, License: GPL-3.0**

### 70_doc_application_web_webServer_h

<a id="70-doc-application-web-webserver-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/70_doc_application_web_webServer_h/docu.output.md`



# Documentation: `AIAssistant::WebServer`

## Overview

The `AIAssistant::WebServer` class implements the HTTP and WebSocket server for the JarvisAgent platform, providing REST APIs, static file serving, and real-time broadcast functionality for the web-based dashboard and workflow editor. It supports secure authentication, workflow management, live workflow run updates, AI call lifecycle events, log streaming, key and session management, settings/configuration APIs, and various extensions for studio vs engine editions.

- Uses [Crow](https://crowcpp.org/) for HTTP/WebSocket.
- Manages authentication/authorization for both web UI/admin and API clients.
- Exposes endpoints for workflow jobs, runs, monitoring, key management, log access, and settings.
- Handles websocket connections for real-time dashboard updates (runs, logs, AI calls).
- Integrates with MCP keys, provider secrets, session management, and OAuth flows.
- Used by both the core server (Engine) and the Studio edition (enables editor and assistant features).
- Includes per-tier rate limiting and brute-force lockout protections.


## Main Responsibilities

- **HTTP API**: Registers and implements endpoints for all core system functionality (workflows, runs, logs, keys, settings, providers, cloud connections, etc).
- **WebSocket API**: Allows browser dashboards/clients to subscribe to live updates (workflow runs, logs, AI states).
- **Security**: Enforces role-based access control (admin/operator/viewer), with strong per-IP and per-user rate limiting and credential lockout.
- **File Serving**: Provides UI/SPA assets, artifact downloads, secure script file checks.
- **Key and Session Management**: Manages MCP key store, API key lifecycle, dashboard user sessions.
- **Configuration**: Allows CRUD for various settings and connections, exposing status and health.
- **Event Broadcast**: Buffers and delivers messages to connected WebSocket clients.
- **Extension**: Allows integration with additional modules (e.g., AI assistant in Studio).

## Key Methods

### Lifecycle

- `WebServer()` — Constructor; registers routes and initializes members.
- `~WebServer()` — Destructor; stops the server cleanly.
- `bool Start()` — Starts the HTTP/WebSocket server (optionally with TLS).
- `void Stop()` — Stops the server and all WebSocket connections.
- `void SignalStop()` — Signals shutdown (called before `WaitStop()`).
- `void WaitStop()` — Blocks until the server thread terminates.

### WebSocket Broadcast

- `void Broadcast(const std::string& jsonMessage)` — Queue a string message for all clients.
- `void BroadcastJSON(const std::string& jsonString)` — Queue a JSON message for all clients.
- `void DrainPendingBroadcasts()` — Deliver all pending broadcasts to clients (should be called periodically from the main thread).
- `void EnqueueLogLine(const std::string& line)` — Queue a log line for streaming to clients.

### Workflow Integration

- `void SetWorkflowRegistry(WorkflowRegistry*)`
- `void SetWorkflowRuntimeManager(WorkflowRuntimeManager*)`
- `void SetTriggerEngine(TriggerEngine*)`

Sets pointers to workflow subsystems, required for workflow execution, monitoring, and editor APIs.

- `void BroadcastWorkflowRunsSnapshot()`
- `void BroadcastWorkflowRunsLastSnapshot()`

Notifies all clients with the current status of workflow runs.

### AI Call State

- `void BroadcastAiCallStarted(const std::string& probName, const std::string& interfaceName)`
- `void BroadcastAiCallCompleted(const std::string& probName, int32_t inputTokens, int32_t outputTokens, int32_t totalTokens, const std::string& finishReason)`
- `void BroadcastAiCallFailed(const std::string& probName, int errorKind, int httpStatus, const std::string& errorMessage)`

Broadcasts AI task lifecycle events to all clients.

### Python Engine Status

- `void BroadcastPythonStatus(bool pythonRunning)` — Broadcasts Python subsystem status.

### MCP/Session Management

- `bool IsMcpConnected()` — Checks if the MCP sidecar has sent a recent heartbeat.

### Security and Authentication

- `Authenticate(const crow::request&)` — Performs request authentication and returns an `AuthResult` with error/user/role.
- `CheckAuth(const crow::request&, std::string_view minRole)` — Checks if the request meets a required role.
- `CheckAdminAuth(const crow::request&)` — Shorthand for requiring admin role.
- `HasRole(const AuthResult&, std::string_view requiredRole)` — Role comparison.
- Implements session, API key (MCP), and optional gateway header authentication; supports brute-force lockout and per-tier rate limiting.

### Key and Session Store

- `bool InitMcpKeyStore(std::string_view masterPassword)` — Loads or creates the encrypted MCP key store with the given master password.
- `bool SaveMcpKeyStore()` — Persists in-memory MCP key changes to disk.

### Static File Serving

- `crow::response ServeStaticFile(const std::filesystem::path&)` — Serve a static file with appropriate cache/security headers.
- `crow::response ServeDashboardIndex()` — Serve dashboard SPA index.
- `crow::response ServeDashboardStatic(const std::string&)` — Serve dashboard front-end assets.

### Core HTTP API Endpoints (Highlights)

- `/api/status` — Health/status report for dashboard.
- `/api/workflows` — List, detail, mutate workflows.
- `/api/workflow-runs` — List, monitor, control workflow runs.
- `/api/log` — Log viewing, streaming, analysis.
- `/api/auth/*` — Authentication, key management, session APIs.
- `/api/settings/*` — API interface, provider, config, and connection management.
- `/api/connections/*` — Cloud connection/OAuth endpoints.
- `/api/scripts` — Script discovery.
- `/api/*/validate` — Workflow and script validation.
- `/api/*/files` — Artifact and result downloads for completed or running workflow runs.
- WebSocket route (`/ws`) — Real-time update hub for dashboards.

### Studio-Only (J9T_STUDIO)

- Registers `/editor` and `/ws/assistant` for workflow editor and assistant features.
- Provides workflow create/update/delete/validation endpoints.
- Handles AI assistant lifecycle (if compiled as Studio edition).

## Notable Internal Mechanisms

- **Thread safety**: Uses mutexes/atomics to safely manage connections, queues, and shared members.
- **Rate limiting**: Unauthenticated and authenticated rate limit buckets by IP and user, with cleaning/eviction.
- **WebSocket batching**: Batches/sanitizes broadcasts to avoid malformed UTF-8 and avoid async write conflicts.
- **Security headers**: Enforces various security HTTP headers in all relevant responses.
- **File/path safety**: Carefully normalizes and checks all file paths, prevents path traversal, symlink exploits, and serves only from allowed roots.
- **Extensible**: Via conditional compilation, allows features to be enabled/disabled for Studio vs Engine builds.


## Security Model

- **Auth**: Session cookie, MCP API key, or gateway-injected headers.
- **Roles**: "admin", "operator", "viewer" drive route access.
- **Rate limiting**: Per-IP (unauthenticated), per-user (authenticated), with lockout on brute force.
- **Lockout**: Too many failed attempts block further requests for a set time.
- **Sensitive data**: API keys, tokens, secrets, and internal files are never exposed through insecure endpoints.

## Extension Points

- External modules (e.g., workflow runtime, assistant, editor) are integrated via `Set...()` methods.
- Additional WebSocket channels and API endpoints may be conditionally compiled.
- Key, provider, and settings sources can be adapted in subclasses if necessary.

## Usage

1. Construct `WebServer` instance.
2. Attach workflow, runtime, trigger engine as needed via `SetWorkflowRegistry()`, etc.
3. Call `Start()` to launch the server.
4. Regularly call `DrainPendingBroadcasts()` from main thread to flush messages.
5. Call `Stop()` / `SignalStop()` and `WaitStop()` for shutdown.

## Dependencies

- [Crow](https://crowcpp.org/) for HTTP/WebSocket.
- OpenSSL for crypto (HMAC/SHA256).
- simdjson for fast JSON parsing.
- Standard C++14–17 features.

## See Also

- `WorkflowRegistry`, `WorkflowRuntimeManager`, `TriggerEngine`, `AdhocWorkflowManager`, `McpKeyManager`, `WebSessionManager`
- `core.h`, `engine.h`, `workflow/*`, `web/*`
- Studio features under `J9T_STUDIO` define.


## Summary Table

| API Group             | Responsible Methods                   | Roles Required            |
|-----------------------|---------------------------------------|---------------------------|
| Health/Status         | `/api/status`, `HandleStatusGet`      | none                      |
| Auth/Sessions         | `/api/auth/*`                         | varies                    |
| Workflow CRUD         | `/api/workflows/*`                    | viewer+, admin for mutate |
| Workflow Runs         | `/api/workflow-runs/*`                | viewer+, operator for ctl |
| Key Management        | `/api/auth/mcp-keys*`                 | admin                     |
| Log Viewing           | `/api/log*`                           | operator/admin            |
| Settings/Providers    | `/api/settings/*`, `/api/connections*`| admin                     |
| WebSockets            | `/ws`                                 | authenticated (any role)  |
| Studio Editor         | `/editor`, `/ws/assistant`            | varies, studio only       |

---

**Note:** For full endpoint list, view the registered CROW_ROUTE instances in the implementation. All methods implement robust error handling and detailed security auditing. The class is not intended for inheritance or extension by users outside the main application.


---

### 71_doc_application_web_webSessionManager_h

<a id="71-doc-application-web-websessionmanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/71_doc_application_web_webSessionManager_h/docu.output.md`



# WebSessionManager (C++ Class)

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Purpose:** Manages server-side web sessions for dashboard login, using cookie-backed (HttpOnly+Secure+SameSite) session IDs. Sessions are in-memory only; all sessions are lost on server restart.

---

## Overview

`WebSessionManager` handles user authentication sessions for a web dashboard. It differentiates from other session managers in the codebase and is specifically used for managing dashboard (web) logins, not workflow runtime sessions.

- Sessions are created on successful authentication.
- Session data is stored in memory, not persisted.
- Sessions expire after a set idle timeout.
- Secure random 256-bit session IDs are generated for each session.
- Thread-safe operations with mutex locking.

---

## Structs

### Session

- `std::string m_SessionId`  
  Unique 256-bit hex session identifier.

- `std::string m_User`  
  User identifier from authentication.

- `std::string m_Role`  
  User role; e.g., `"admin"`, `"operator"`, `"viewer"`.

- `std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point m_CreatedAt`  
  Time the session was created.

- `std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point m_LastActivity`  
  Time the session was last used.

### LoginResult

- `std::string m_SessionId`  
  Session identifier for the new session.

- `std::string m_User`  
  User identifier.

- `std::string m_Role`  
  User role.

---

## Public Member Functions

### LoginResult Create(const std::string& user, const std::string& role)

Creates a new session for the given user and role. Registers the session and returns its identifying details.

### std::optional<Session> Validate(const std::string& sessionId)

Validates the provided session ID:

- Returns active `Session` on success, and updates its last activity time.
- Returns `std::nullopt` if the session is missing or expired.

### void Destroy(const std::string& sessionId)

Removes the session with the given ID, logging the user out.

### void PurgeExpired()

Scans all sessions and removes those expired due to inactivity. Intended to be called periodically, e.g., from a background task.

### void SetTimeoutHours(int hours)

Sets the session idle expiration timeout in hours. Defaults to 8 if given value is invalid or non-positive.

### int GetTimeoutHours() const

Retrieves the current session timeout interval (in hours).

---

## Private Members

- `mutable std::mutex m_Mutex`  
  Ensures thread-safe access to session data.

- `std::unordered_map<std::string, Session> m_Sessions`  
  Session storage mapping session IDs to `Session` objects.

- `int m_TimeoutHours{8}`  
  Session idle timeout, in hours (default: 8).

---

## Implementation Notes

- Session IDs are cryptographically strong (generated via OpenSSL's `RAND_bytes`).
- Validating a session refreshes its activity window ("sliding window" expiration).
- All session operations are protected by a mutex for safe concurrent access.
- Destroying or letting a session expire fully removes it from storage.
- Changing timeout via `SetTimeoutHours` is thread-safe.

---

## Limitations

- All sessions are kept in memory. On server shutdown or crash, all sessions are lost.
- No persistence or session migration across processes or hosts.

---

## Use Case

This class is intended for managing short-lived, in-memory user sessions for a secure web interface, allowing efficient and secure user authentication with automatic expiration.

---

## License

GPL-3.0 - See source file header for full license text.


---

### 72_doc_application_workflow_adhocWorkflowManager_h

<a id="72-doc-application-workflow-adhocworkflowmanager-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/72_doc_application_workflow_adhocWorkflowManager_h/docu.output.md`



# AdhocWorkflowManager C++ Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`

**Purpose:**  
Manages ad-hoc ("one-shot") workflow runs submitted through `POST /api/workflows/run-adhoc`. Handles the staging, execution tracking, disk quota enforcement, output manifesting, run attribution, and scheduled cleanup (reaper) of workflow run folders.

---

## Overview

- Each ad-hoc run has its own isolated directory under `_adhoc/<user_slug>/<run_folder>/`.
- Run folders encodes user attribution and deletion policy in the filesystem.
- Designed to be restart/restart-safe and allow artifact access policy enforcement at the filesystem and API level.
- Provides disk-usage tracking and enforcement per user.
- Supports cleanup policies: auto-removal after TTL, on completion, or manual retention.
- Includes a background reaper thread or manual reaping.
- Does not use file watching for queue management; handles outputs directly.

---

## Directory Structure

When a run is staged:

```
_adhoc/<user_slug>/<ISO8601>_<counter>_del-<ISO8601>/
  workflows/
    _adhoc_<ISO8601>_<counter>.jcwf
    _adhoc_<ISO8601>_<counter>/
      global.json
      _adhoc_<ISO8601>_<counter>.json
  queue/
  meta.json
  manifest.json
```

- **meta.json**: persistent run attribution (user, role, policy, owner slug)
- **manifest.json**: completed run output inventory

---

## Public API

### Constructor/Destructor

- `AdhocWorkflowManager(McpKeyManager& keyManager, WorkflowRegistry& registry)`
- `~AdhocWorkflowManager()`

_Non-copyable and non-assignable._

---

### Initialization

- `void Init(const std::filesystem::path& adhocBaseFolder);`
    - Sets up base directory, restores counters and per-user disk usage from existing runs.
    - Ignores legacy runs (in root of `_adhoc/`), which are reaped via file age.

---

### Staging

#### StageRequest Structure

- Fields:
    - `std::string m_JcwfJson;` — workflow JSON (canvas)
    - `std::string m_User;` — WHO submitted
    - `std::string m_Role;` — submitter's role
    - `std::string m_CleanupPolicy;` — "on_completion", "ttl_1h", "ttl_24h", "ttl_48h", "ttl_72h", or "retain"
    - `int m_DiskQuotaMb;` — User's disk quota in MB

#### StageResult Structure

- Fields:
    - `std::string m_RunId;` — Stable adhoc run ID (e.g. `adhoc_20260416T143022_0001`)
    - `std::string m_WorkflowId;` — Internal workflow ID (e.g. `_adhoc_20260416T143022_0001`)
    - `std::filesystem::path m_FolderPath;` — Filesystem folder for the run

#### API

- `std::variant<StageResult, std::string> Stage(const StageRequest& req);`
    - Stages a new run. Returns `StageResult` on success, or error string if failed.
    - Enforces disk quota, writes workflow and attribution.

---

### Completion

- `void OnRunCompleted(const std::string& runId);`
    - Called once the workflow run finished.
    - For `on_completion` policy: deletes folder immediately.
    - For other policies: writes manifest for output inventory.

---

### Reaper / Cleanup

- `void Reap();`
    - One-shot scan. Deletes folders past TTL (by encoded "delete-at" time).
- `void StartReaperThread();`
    - Starts background thread that calls `Reap()` every 60 seconds.
- `void StopReaperThread();`
    - Stops reaper thread.

---

### Disk Quota / Usage

- `size_t GetUserDiskUsageBytes(const std::string& user) const;`
    - Returns user's current disk usage in bytes.
- `bool WouldExceedQuota(const std::string& user, int diskQuotaMb, size_t additionalBytes) const;`
    - Returns true if this user would exceed their quota with `additionalBytes`.

---

### Run and Output Accessors

- `size_t GetActiveRunCount() const;`
    - Number of live staged runs (not yet deleted/reaped).
- `size_t GetTotalDiskUsageBytes() const;`
    - Returns sum of all known users' disk usage.
- `std::optional<RunInfo> GetRunInfo(const std::string& runId) const;`
    - Returns persistent attribution for a run, including folder path.

#### RunInfo Structure

- Fields:
    - `std::string m_User;`
    - `std::string m_Role;`
    - `std::string m_OwnerSlug;`
    - `std::string m_CleanupPolicy;`
    - `std::filesystem::path m_FolderPath;`

---

### Utilities

- `static std::string SanitizeUserSlug(const std::string& user);`
    - Returns a filesystem-safe string as user slug.
    - Allowed: `[A-Za-z0-9._@-]`
    - Max 64 bytes; others replaced by `_`.

---

## Internal Details

- Generates run folder name as `"<ISO8601>_<counter>_del-<deleteat>"`, where `deleteat` encodes TTL or "retain".
- Attribution and folder names ensure:
    - Unique per-run isolation,
    - Secure user/role tagging,
    - Efficient lookup and access control,
    - Safe, stateless reaping by folder name.
- Quotas and attributions are restart-safe — restored on `Init()`.
- Manifest is generated at completion for efficient artifacts listing.

---

## Error Handling

- Errors in stage/creation/cleanup are logged.
- All mutating operations are thread-safe via internal mutex.
- Disk quota is enforced at both pre-stage and post-completion.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::AdhocWorkflowManager manager(...);
manager.Init("_adhoc");

// Stage a run
AIAssistant::AdhocWorkflowManager::StageRequest req = { ... };
auto result = manager.Stage(req);
if (std::holds_alternative<AIAssistant::AdhocWorkflowManager::StageResult>(result)) {
    // Use StageResult
} else {
    // Handle error (get error string from std::get<std::string>(result))
}
```

---

## Threading Model

- All mutable access is protected by `m_Mutex`.
- Reaper thread can run in background or be controlled/tested via direct `Reap()` calls.
- Destruction (`~AdhocWorkflowManager`) is safe (stops any background work).

---

## License

GPL-3.0 (see file header for details).


---

### 73_doc_application_workflow_aiCallEvents_h

<a id="73-doc-application-workflow-aicallevents-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/73_doc_application_workflow_aiCallEvents_h/docu.output.md`



# AiCallStartedEvent, AiCallCompletedEvent, and AiCallFailedEvent Classes

## Overview

These classes represent event types for tracking the lifecycle of AI calls in an application. Each event inherits from the `Event` base class and provides information relevant to the AI call's state.

---

## Classes

### 1. AiCallStartedEvent

Represents the start of an AI call.

**Constructor**

- `AiCallStartedEvent(std::string probName, std::string interfaceName)`
  - `probName`: Name of the problem being solved or processed.
  - `interfaceName`: Name of the AI interface being used.

**Methods**

- `std::string const& GetProbName() const`
  - Returns the problem name.
- `std::string const& GetInterfaceName() const`
  - Returns the interface name.

---

### 2. AiCallCompletedEvent

Represents a successfully completed AI call.

**Constructor**

- `AiCallCompletedEvent(std::string probName, AiUsage usage, std::string finishReason)`
  - `probName`: Name of the problem processed.
  - `usage`: Usage statistics or information about the AI call (type `AiUsage`).
  - `finishReason`: Reason the call finished (e.g., "completed", "stopped").

**Methods**

- `std::string const& GetProbName() const`
  - Returns the problem name.
- `AiUsage const& GetUsage() const`
  - Returns usage details.
- `std::string const& GetFinishReason() const`
  - Returns finish reason.

---

### 3. AiCallFailedEvent

Represents a failed AI call.

**Constructor**

- `AiCallFailedEvent(std::string probName, AiError error)`
  - `probName`: Name of the problem processed.
  - `error`: Error details (type `AiError`).

**Methods**

- `std::string const& GetProbName() const`
  - Returns the problem name.
- `AiError const& GetError() const`
  - Returns error details.

---

## Usage

Use these events with an event-based system to monitor and react to AI call lifecycle changes, such as start, completion, or failure. Each class provides accessors to relevant data for logging, analytics, or workflow control.

---

## License

GPL-3.0 License. See the source code header for details.

### 74_doc_application_workflow_aiCallTaskExecutor_h

<a id="74-doc-application-workflow-aicalltaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/74_doc_application_workflow_aiCallTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# AiCallTaskExecutor C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`AiCallTaskExecutor` is a final class in the `AIAssistant` namespace implementing the `ITaskExecutor` interface. It is responsible for the execution of AI call workflow tasks, including handling task file preparation, input resolution, context materialization, prompt building, and synchronous dispatch to AI interfaces (such as LLM providers). The class focuses on accurate file handling, prompt construction, API dispatch, and task lifecycle management in workflow automation scenarios.

---

## Class Definition

```
class AiCallTaskExecutor final : public ITaskExecutor
{
public:
    AiCallTaskExecutor();
    virtual ~AiCallTaskExecutor();

    bool Execute(const WorkflowDefinition& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun& workflowRun, const TaskDef& taskDefinition,
                 TaskInstanceState& taskState) override;

    static bool WriteTextFile(const std::string& filePath, const std::string& fileContent, std::string& outErrorMessage);

private:
    static std::string BuildProbFilename(int64_t requestId, int64_t timestampNs);

    static std::optional<std::string> TryExtractStringParam(const std::string& rawParamsJson,
                                                            const std::string& fieldName, std::string& outErrorMessage);

    static std::string ApplySimpleTemplate(const std::string& templateText, const TaskInstanceState& taskState);
    static std::string TryBuildPromptFromParams(const TaskDef& taskDefinition, const TaskInstanceState& taskState);

    static bool WriteInlineQueueBindingFiles(const QueueBinding& queueBinding, std::string& outErrorMessage);
};
```

---

## Main Responsibilities

- **Execute AI workflow tasks**, orchestrate input file handling, materialization, input mapping, prompt building, and dispatch.
- **Input file handling**: Processes static files, context (CNTX), and problem definition (PROB) files, including glob expansion, Office document conversion, and path resolution.
- **Prompt generation**: Builds the prompt for AI models either from explicit `prompt_template` or by combining definitions and inputs.
- **Dispatch and chunking**: Submits constructed AI request envelopes, handling context chunking when prompt size exceeds model limits, with reduce/fallback logic.
- **Task lifecycle**: Updates task state and error reporting throughout the process, ensuring deterministic file and output mapping for workflow tracking.

---

## Public Methods

### AiCallTaskExecutor()
Default constructor.

### ~AiCallTaskExecutor()
Default destructor.

### bool Execute(const WorkflowDefinition& workflowDefinition,
                WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
                const TaskDef& taskDefinition,
                TaskInstanceState& taskState) override

- Executes the AI call task for the current workflow.
- Resolves working directories and file paths.
- Localizes and writes input, context, and problem files.
- Converts Office files to Markdown if needed.
- Handles glob patterns and input value substitutions in file paths/content.
- Prepares and registers a pending request in the AI request pool.
- Builds and dispatches the AI prompt (with chunking/reduce if required).
- Updates `taskState` with progress, error messages, and external request tracking.
- Returns `true` if execution started successfully, `false` otherwise.

---

## Static Utility Methods

### static bool WriteTextFile(const std::string& filePath,
                             const std::string& fileContent,
                             std::string& outErrorMessage)
- Writes `fileContent` to the file at `filePath`.
- Ensures parent directories exist.
- Outputs error messages on failure.
- Returns `true` on success, `false` otherwise.

### static std::string BuildProbFilename(int64_t requestId, int64_t timestampNs)
- Returns a deterministic PROB file name in the format `PROB_<id>_<timestamp>.txt`.

### static std::optional<std::string> TryExtractStringParam(const std::string& rawParamsJson,
                                                           const std::string& fieldName,
                                                           std::string& outErrorMessage)
- Extracts a string parameter by `fieldName` from a JSON string.
- Returns `std::optional<std::string>`; empty if not found.
- Outputs an error message if JSON is invalid or access fails.

### static std::string ApplySimpleTemplate(const std::string& templateText,
                                          const TaskInstanceState& taskState)
- Expands a simple template string with keys from `taskState.m_InputValues`.

### static std::string TryBuildPromptFromParams(const TaskDef& taskDefinition,
                                               const TaskInstanceState& taskState)
- Constructs the user prompt:
  - Uses `prompt_template` if set in `taskDefinition.m_ParamsJson`, expanded via `ApplySimpleTemplate`.
  - Otherwise, builds a fallback prompt from parameters and inputs.

### static bool WriteInlineQueueBindingFiles(const QueueBinding& queueBinding,
                                            std::string& outErrorMessage)
- Writes all inline files from the queue binding (environment, context, task, problem) to disk.
- Returns `true` on success, `false` on failure.

---

## Key Concepts

- **Context and Problem Files**: Handles context (CNTX) and problem (PROB) files, expands glob patterns, converts Office files to Markdown, and materializes all referenced files for prompt construction.
- **Template Expansion**: Supports both simple and advanced template placeholders for dynamic file paths and content.
- **Prompt Chunking**: If prompt size exceeds provider model limits, automatically splits context, dispatches in multiple requests, and aggregates responses.
- **Provider/Model Parameterization**: Provider and model settings can be set per-task and are resolved (including defaults) and written to sidecar PROV files for audit/tracing.
- **Robust Error Handling**: At each step (file missing, conversion failure, dispatch error), errors are logged and reflected in the task state.

---

## Usage Context

This class is intended for use within an AI workflow/task orchestration system where "ai_call" steps dispatch synthesized prompts to remote AI providers, handle diverse context and problem file requirements, and manage deterministic output tracking for automation and reproducibility.

---

## Dependencies

- C++17 or later
- [simdjson](https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson) for fast JSON parsing
- STL: `<filesystem>`, `<fstream>`, `<sstream>`, `<unordered_map>`, `<vector>`, `<optional>`, `<string>`, etc.
- Application-specific headers for core engine (`Core`), agent (`JarvisAgent`), file writing, request pool, and template engine.

---

## License

This software is licensed under the MIT License (see header for details).

---

## See Also

- `ITaskExecutor`: Base interface.
- `WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, `TaskDef`, `TaskInstanceState`: Core workflow/task data structures.
- `QueueBinding`, `QueueFileRef`: File binding abstractions for context/problem payloads.

---

## Related Functions (Internal/Support, not public API)

- Path resolution and localization
- Office file detection and Markdown conversion via external tool (`markitdown`)
- Glob pattern expansion and simple matching
- Workflow default value expansion via templates
- Output slot mapping and deterministic file naming

---

## Summary

`AiCallTaskExecutor` enables robust, reproducible, and scalable execution of AI prompt dispatch tasks within a workflow engine, supporting flexible input mapping, dynamic context handling, and reliable intervention-free automation for both human operators and AI agents.


---

### 75_doc_application_workflow_aiInvocation_h

<a id="75-doc-application-workflow-aiinvocation-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/75_doc_application_workflow_aiInvocation_h/docu.output.md`



# Documentation: AIAssistant C++ Classes

## Overview

This header defines types and structures for configuring and invoking AI assistant requests. It is intended for use in systems that facilitate communication between humans and AI models, providing structured messaging, configuration, and retry logic.

---

## Namespace: `AIAssistant`

### Enum: `MessageRole`
Represents the role of a message in a conversation.
- `System`
- `User`
- `Assistant`

---

### Struct: `Message`
Represents a single message exchanged with the AI assistant.
- `MessageRole m_Role`  
  Role of the message sender. Defaults to `User`.
- `std::string m_Content`  
  Content of the message.

---

### Struct: `AiSettings`
Configures AI model parameters.
- `double m_Temperature`  
  Sampling temperature for response diversity. Default is `0.0`.
- `std::optional<int64_t> m_Seed`  
  Optional random seed for reproducibility.
- `std::optional<int32_t> m_MaxTokens`  
  Optional maximum number of tokens in the response.

---

### Struct: `AiRetryPolicy`
Configures retry behavior for AI invocations.
- `int m_HttpMaxAttempts`  
  Max HTTP retry attempts. Default is `3`.
- `int m_OutputSchemaMaxAttempts`  
  Max attempts for schema validation retries. Default is `3`.
- `int m_TotalMaxAttempts`  
  Overall maximum attempts. Default is `10`.
- `std::chrono::milliseconds m_BackoffMs`  
  Backoff duration between retries. Default is `500` ms.

---

### Enum: `StructuredMode`
Determines how structured responses are handled.
- `None`
- `NativeJsonSchema`
- `ForcedToolShim`

---

### Struct: `AiInvocation`
Encapsulates all parameters for a single AI request.
- `std::string m_InterfaceName`  
  Name of the AI interface to use.
- `std::optional<std::string> m_ModelOverride`  
  Optional override for model selection.
- `AiSettings m_Settings`  
  AI generation settings.
- `std::vector<Message> m_Messages`  
  Conversation history/messages for the invocation.
- `std::optional<std::string> m_OutputSchemaJson`  
  Optional JSON schema for output validation.
- `StructuredMode m_StructuredMode`  
  Mode for structured response validation. Default is `None`.
- `std::chrono::milliseconds m_Timeout`  
  Timeout duration for the request. Default is `120000` ms (2 minutes).
- `AiRetryPolicy m_Retry`  
  Retry policy for the invocation.
- `std::filesystem::path m_QueueFolder`  
  Folder path for queuing requests, if applicable.
- `std::string m_ProbName`  
  Name of the originating problem/task.
- `std::string m_ProvName`  
  Name of the provider or provenance source.
- `std::optional<int32_t> m_ChunkIndex`  
  Optional chunk index for batch processing.
- `std::optional<int32_t> m_ChunkCount`  
  Optional total number of chunks for batch processing.
- `int m_SchemaAttemptNumber`  
  Internal use: tracks schema validation attempts. Reset by users; incremented on retry.

---

## Usage

These types are designed to be composed to form an AI request, configure execution parameters, set up conversation context, and systematically handle retries and output schema validation. The structures are intended for integration with AI backends conforming to messaging and structured output conventions.


---

### 76_doc_application_workflow_aiReply_h

# AIAssistant Namespace Documentation

This namespace defines data structures for handling AI assistant responses, errors, and usage statistics.

---

## Structures

### `AiUsage`

Tracks token usage details in AI interactions.

| Member           | Type    | Description                 |
|------------------|---------|-----------------------------|
| m_InputTokens    | int32_t | Number of input tokens used |
| m_OutputTokens   | int32_t | Number of output tokens used|
| m_TotalTokens    | int32_t | Total tokens (input + output)|

---

### `AiError`

Describes an error that occurred during an AI operation.

#### Enums

- `Kind`
  - `None`: No error
  - `Http`: HTTP error
  - `Parse`: Parsing error
  - `SchemaValidation`: Schema validation error
  - `Timeout`: Operation timed out
  - `Transport`: Transport-layer error
  - `Provider`: Provider error

| Member        | Type                | Description                |
|---------------|---------------------|----------------------------|
| m_Kind        | Kind                | Error type                 |
| m_HttpStatus  | int                 | HTTP status code (if any)  |
| m_Message     | std::string         | Error message              |

---

### `AiReply`

Represents a response from the AI assistant.

#### Enums

- `Kind`
  - `Text`: Plain text reply
  - `Structured`: Structured (e.g., JSON) reply
  - `Error`: Error occurred

| Member              | Type           | Description                                    |
|---------------------|----------------|------------------------------------------------|
| m_Kind              | Kind           | Type of reply                                  |
| m_Text              | std::string    | Textual reply content                          |
| m_StructuredJson    | std::string    | Structured reply in JSON format                |
| m_Error             | AiError        | Error details (if `Kind::Error`)               |
| m_Usage             | AiUsage        | Token usage stats                              |
| m_FinishReason      | std::string    | Reason the reply finished                      |
| m_SystemFingerprint | std::string    | Fingerprint of system generating the reply     |
