### 77_doc_application_workflow_aiRequestPool_h

<a id="77-doc-application-workflow-airequestpool-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/77_doc_application_workflow_aiRequestPool_h/docu.output.md`



# AiRequestPool Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `workflow/aiRequestPool.h`  
**Purpose:**  
`AiRequestPool` tracks, manages, and completes pending AI requests ("ai_call"s), typically for an automated workflow system. It coordinates request registration, status tracking, output artifact completion, error handling, and dispatch to AI providers, supporting both human and automated (AI) callers.

---

## Overview

- **Non-blocking:** Designed for tick-based, non-blocking runtimes, but supports direct wait as well.
- **Completion & Timeout:** Completions and failures (including timeouts) are queued and can be drained efficiently.
- **Output Handling:** Ensures outputs are written to filesystem and correctly mapped to workflow slots.
- **Workflows:** Integrates with workflow task lifecycle; supports binding requests to tasks and signaling completions.
- **Concurrency Safe:** Internally synchronized for multi-threaded use.

---

## Main Types

### `AiRequestHandle`
Identifies an AI request.

- **Fields:**
    - `int64_t requestId`
    - `int64_t requestTimestampNs`
- **Methods:**
    - `bool IsValid() const`

### `AiRequestCompletion`
Describes the completion (success or failure) of a workflow-bound AI request.

- **Fields:**
    - `std::string m_WorkflowId`
    - `std::string m_RunId`
    - `std::string m_TaskId`
    - `bool m_WasFailed`
    - `std::string m_ResponseText`
    - `std::string m_ErrorMessage`
    - `std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string> m_OutputValues` (all values are file paths)

---

## Core Methods

### Lifecycle & Registration

- **`int64_t AllocateRequestId()`**  
  Returns a new, unique request ID.

- **`AiRequestHandle RegisterPending(const AiRequestHandle& handle)`**  
  Registers a pending request with the default timeout (5 minutes).

- **`AiRequestHandle RegisterPending(const AiRequestHandle& handle, uint64_t timeoutMs)`**  
  Registers a pending request with explicit timeout.

- **`AiRequestHandle RegisterPendingWorkflowTask(const AiRequestHandle& handle, std::string workflowId, ... , uint64_t timeoutMs, std::string expectedOutputPath = "")`**  
  Registers a workflow-bound request. Associates with workflow/run/task, output file paths, slot names. Enables deterministic completion signaling.

- **`void Shutdown()`**  
  Marks all requests as completed with failure ("shutdown"), notifies any waiters, and clears state.

- **`void Forget(const AiRequestHandle& handle)`**  
  Removes any registered pending entry by handle (safe if not found).

---

### Completion and Error Handling

- **`bool OnOutputFileCreated(const std::string& fullFilePath)`**  
  Called after a .output.* file is written; completes the associated request if matched.

- **`bool OnRequestFailed(const std::string& expectedOutputPath, const std::string& errorMessage)`**  
  Fails the request associated with the given expected output path when an error occurs before output file creation (e.g. HTTP/parse failures).

---

### Watchdogs & Timeouts

- **`void KickFileActivityWatchdog(const AiRequestHandle& handle)`**  
  Resets the file-activity watchdog timer for the given request (call after each file write). Detects executor stalls.

- **`void Update()`**  
  Runs maintenance: processes file-activity timeouts and main request deadlines. Should be called periodically (e.g., main update/tick).

---

### Results Retrieval

- **`bool TryConsumeResult(const AiRequestHandle& handle, bool& outWasFailed, std::string& outResponseText, std::string& outErrorMessage)`**  
  Non-blocking. Retrieves and removes completed result if available.

- **`bool TryPopCompletion(AiRequestCompletion& outCompletion)`**  
  Non-blocking. Pops the next completed workflow task completion entry (if any).

- **`bool WaitForCompletion(const AiRequestHandle& handle, uint64_t timeoutMs, std::string& outResponseText, std::string& outErrorMessage)`**  
  Blocking. Waits for request completion or timeout; returns result if available.

---

### Direct AI Provider Dispatch

- **`bool Submit(const AiInvocation& envelope, ReplyCallback onReply)`**  
  Submits an AI invocation envelope to the configured provider.

    - Builds the HTTP request and dispatches asynchronously.
    - Handles retries for schema validation failures (if requested).
    - Writes .output.* files and signals completion.
    - Invokes `onReply` when done.

---

### Observability & Metrics

- **`size_t GetDirectDispatchInflight() const`**  
  Number of direct submissions currently in progress.

- **`uint64_t GetStructuredSubmissions() const`**  
- **`uint64_t GetSchemaValidationRetries() const`**  
- **`uint64_t GetSchemaValidationFailures() const`**  
- **`uint64_t GetChunkedDispatches() const`**  
- **`uint64_t GetFenceStrips() const`**  
  Counters for debugging, dashboard, and status API.

---

## Usage Summary

- **Register** a request before dispatch; bind to workflow (if relevant) for task completions.
- **Submit** AI calls via `Submit()`; provide callback for response.
- **On file output or errors**, invoke `OnOutputFileCreated` or `OnRequestFailed` to signal completion/failure.
- **Periodically call `Update()`** to process watchdogs/deadlines.
- **Drain results** non-blocking via `TryConsumeResult` / `TryPopCompletion`, or wait with `WaitForCompletion`.
- **Use metrics** for monitoring (optional).
- **Shutdown** safely when terminating system.

---

## File Output & Workflow Binding

- **Output files are always used** for deterministic artifact passing (never raw result text in memory for workflow tasks).
- **Output values map** workflow slots to file paths.
- **Direct and chunked dispatches are handled** (chunked writes multiple files; completion tied to the aggregate).

---

## Error/Timeout Handling

- Automatic failure on:
    - File activity stall (no curl dispatch within window after a queue file is written).
    - Deadline exceeded (request not completed in time).
    - Provider or parse errors.
- All failures are detectable and signaled to the workflow or caller.

---

## Thread Safety

- All public methods are concurrency safe.
- Internally uses mutexes and atomics for all shared state.

---

## License

MIT License (see file header)

---

## See Also

- `AiInvocation`, `AiReply`, `Workflow` system
- `workflow/aiInvocation.h`, `workflow/aiReply.h`
- File naming conventions: `.output.txt`, `.output.json`, etc.
- Core engine update/tick integration for timeout handling

---


---

### 78_doc_application_workflow_aiTranscript_h

<a id="78-doc-application-workflow-aitranscript-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/78_doc_application_workflow_aiTranscript_h/docu.output.md`



# AiTranscript C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

The `AIAssistant::AiTranscript` class manages writing and updating transcript JSON files that log request/response interactions between users and AI models. Each transcript records a sequence of turns, appending new requests and their AI responses in a standardized JSON array format for reproducibility and review.

## File Output

- Writes JSON transcript files named `<prob>.transcript.json` (or `<prob>_chunk<N>.transcript.json` for chunked phases) next to their corresponding output files.
- Each file contains an array of entries as documented:

```json
[
  {"kind":"request", ...},
  {"kind":"response", ...}
]
```

## JSON Format

### Request Entry

```json
{
  "kind": "request",
  "interface": "<string>",
  "model": "<string>",
  "messages": [
    {"role": "user/system/assistant", "content": "<string>"}
  ],
  "settings": {
    "temperature": <float>,
    "seed": <int, optional>,
    "max_tokens": <int, optional>
  },
  "timestamp": "<ISO 8601 string>",
  "chunk": {"index": <int>, "count": <int>} // present if chunked
}
```

### Response Entry

```json
{
  "kind": "response",
  "text": "<string>",              // present for Kind::Text
  "structured": {...},             // present for Kind::Structured
  "error": {                       // present for Kind::Error
    "kind": "<string>",
    "http_status": <int>,
    "message": "<string>"
  },
  "usage": {
    "input": <int>,
    "output": <int>,
    "total": <int>
  },
  "finish_reason": "<string>",           // optional
  "system_fingerprint": "<string>",      // optional
  "timestamp": "<ISO 8601 string>"
}
```

## Class Members

### Static Methods

#### `bool AppendRequest(const std::filesystem::path& transcriptPath, const AiInvocation& envelope, const std::string& resolvedModel);`

- Appends a new request entry to the given JSON transcript file at `transcriptPath`.
- Accepts:
  - `transcriptPath`: Path to the `.transcript.json` file.
  - `envelope`: The `AiInvocation` object containing interface name, settings, messages, and optional chunk information.
  - `resolvedModel`: The name of the AI model used.
- Returns `true` on success; `false` on failure.

#### `bool AppendResponse(const std::filesystem::path& transcriptPath, const AiReply& reply);`

- Appends a new response entry to the transcript file at `transcriptPath`.
- Accepts:
  - `transcriptPath`: Path to the `.transcript.json` file.
  - `reply`: The AI's reply including its type (`Text`, `Structured`, or `Error`), usage data, finish reason, and optional fingerprint.
- Returns `true` on success; `false` on failure.

## Internal Helpers (Not Public API)

- Timestamping in ISO8601 with ms precision.
- File read/write utilities, appending new entries as needed.
- Sanitization for JSON-safe output.

## Notes

- If the transcript file does not exist, a new array is created.
- Handles chunked requests with separate per-chunk transcript files and aggregates if needed.
- Ensures all output is valid JSON and maintains append-only semantics for turn-wise logging.

## License

- GPL-3.0 License.
- See top of file for license statement and warranty disclaimer.

### 79_doc_application_workflow_dataflowResolver_h

<a id="79-doc-application-workflow-dataflowresolver-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/79_doc_application_workflow_dataflowResolver_h/docu.output.md`



# DataflowResolver C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`DataflowResolver` is a utility class in the `AIAssistant` namespace that collects and resolves input values required by a task instance in a workflow. It determines these values by following explicit dataflow connections, inspecting the workflow context, using default values, and expanding template expressions. This class does not execute tasks, only resolves their input values.

## Main Types

### `TaskResolvedInputs`
- Stores a mapping (`m_StringValues`) from input names to their resolved values for a given task.
- Holds an error message (`m_ErrorMessage`) if any required input could not be resolved.

## Key Public Methods

### `std::optional<TaskResolvedInputs> ResolveInputsForTask(const WorkflowDefinition&, const WorkflowRun&, const TaskDef&, const std::string&) const;`
- Resolves all declared input fields for a given task.
- Uses the following resolution order for each input:
  1. Explicit dataflow edges (connections from other tasks' outputs)
  2. Workflow context values
  3. Input-level default values
- Required inputs that cannot be resolved result in an error message.
- Expands template expressions (e.g., `{{inputs.field}}`) in resolved values.
- Returns an optional `TaskResolvedInputs`; returns `std::nullopt` if any template expansion fails.

### `bool TryResolveFromDataflowEdges(const WorkflowDefinition&, const WorkflowRun&, const std::string& targetTaskId, const std::string& targetInputName, std::string& resolvedValueOut) const;`
- Checks if any dataflow edge defines a value for a given task input.
- If found, sets `resolvedValueOut` to the resolved value and returns `true`; otherwise returns `false`.

### `bool ExpandTemplates(const std::string& rawValue, const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& inputValues, std::string& expandedOut) const;`
- Expands template expressions inside `rawValue` using `inputValues`.
- Writes the result to `expandedOut`.
- Returns `true` on success, `false` on failure.

## Notes

- Required inputs that are not resolved trigger error logging and result in an error.
- Optional inputs that are not resolved are omitted.
- Default values and context-based resolution are supported.
- Template expansion is strict and logs any parsing errors.
- DataflowResolver is not responsible for executing tasks, only for resolving inputs.

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::DataflowResolver resolver;
auto resolvedInputs = resolver.ResolveInputsForTask(definition, run, taskDef, taskId);
if (resolvedInputs) {
    // Use resolvedInputs->m_StringValues
}
```

## License

GPL-3.0 (see file header for details)  
Copyright (c) 2025 JC Technolabs

### 80_doc_application_workflow_filter_filterEngine_h

<a id="80-doc-application-workflow-filter-filterengine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/80_doc_application_workflow_filter_filterEngine_h/docu.output.md`



# FilterEngine C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

The `AIAssistant::FilterEngine` class evaluates filter definitions to generate structured lists of items (as `FilterItem`s) from various data sources (CSV, text lines, queries, Polarion queries). It is used to expand per-item tasks in workflows based on filtered, optionally processed data.

---

## Main Types

### FilterItem

- Represents a single data item resulting from a filter evaluation.
- **Fields:**
  - `m_Index`: 0-based index within the result set.
  - `m_SourceIndex`: Original source index (row, line, or hit number).
  - `m_Values`: Key-value pairs for item properties (fields/columns), excluding any binding prefix.
  - `m_SourcePath`: Path to the original data source (used for cache/freshness).
  - `m_Key`: Stable identity for the item (e.g., first field value, content hash).

---

## Public API

### Methods

#### `std::vector<FilterItem> Evaluate(FilterDef const& filter, std::string const& workflowBaseDir, std::string& errorMessage) const`
- Evaluates a filter of a certain kind and returns matching items.
- Supported source kinds: `"csv"`, `"text_lines"`, `"query"`, `"polarion_query"`.
- On error, returns an empty vector and sets `errorMessage`.

---

## Data Source Evaluation Details

### CSV Evaluation

#### `std::vector<FilterItem> EvaluateCsv(FilterDef const& filter, std::string const& resolvedPath, std::string& errorMessage) const`
- Reads a CSV file.
- Supports custom delimiters, optional header row, row range selection, and max item limits.
- Extracts field values by column position and header name (if present).
- Provides stable `m_Key` (first column value or row number).

### Text Lines Evaluation

#### `std::vector<FilterItem> EvaluateTextLines(FilterDef const& filter, std::string const& resolvedPath, std::string& errorMessage) const`
- Reads a plain text file, one item per line.
- Can skip empty lines.
- Supports max item limits.
- Sets item key as the line content.

### Query Evaluation

#### `std::vector<FilterItem> EvaluateQuery(FilterDef const& filter, std::string const& workflowBaseDir, std::string& errorMessage) const`
- Reads a line-delimited file, where each line is tab-separated key-value pairs.
- Applies a query filter using a `QueryParser` AST.
- Extracts fields per the filter definition or all if unspecified.
- Supports `max_items`.
- Sets item key as the `id` field, if present, or line number.

### Polarion Query Evaluation

#### `std::vector<FilterItem> EvaluatePolarionQuery(FilterDef const& filter, std::string const& workflowBaseDir, std::string& errorMessage) const`
- Fetches items using the `PolarionClient`.
- Supports resolving connection settings from named cloud connections.

---

## Helper Functions

### Range Parsing

#### `static bool ParseRange(std::string const& range, size_t& outStart, size_t& outEnd)`
- Parses a range string (e.g., `"10-20"`, `"5-"`, `"-50"`) as 1-based inclusive indices.
- Sets `outStart` and `outEnd`. For "all", sets `outStart=1`, `outEnd=SIZE_MAX`.
- Returns `false` if the input is malformed.

### CSV Line Splitter

#### `static std::vector<std::string> SplitCsvLine(std::string const& line, char delimiter)`
- Splits a CSV line into columns.
- Respects double-quote escape sequences.

### Whitespace Trimming

#### `static std::string Trim(std::string const& s)`
- Removes leading and trailing whitespace from a string.

### Add Padded Indices

#### `static void AddPaddedIndices(std::vector<FilterItem>& items)`
- Adds zero-padded fields (`index_padded`, `row_number_padded`) to each item for readability in downstream processes.

---

## Usage Overview

To use `FilterEngine`, create an instance and call `Evaluate()` with a filter definition, base directory, and error message reference. Items will be extracted and processed as per the filter's configuration, ready for downstream workflow expansion.

---

## License

GPL-3.0 License (c) 2026 JC Technolabs. See source code header for license details.

### 81_doc_application_workflow_filter_filterManifest_h

<a id="81-doc-application-workflow-filter-filtermanifest-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/81_doc_application_workflow_filter_filterManifest_h/docu.output.md`



# FilterManifestManager C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`FilterManifestManager` manages filter manifests for workflow filters. It builds manifests from filter evaluation results, serializes/deserializes them to/from JSON files, and compares new manifests to previous ones to detect changes. Its methods are designed for efficient detection of item additions, removals, and modifications.

Namespace: `AIAssistant`

## Related Structures

- **FilterManifestEntry**: Holds metadata for one filter item (index, key, source path, modification time).
- **FilterManifest**: Aggregates all manifest entries from one evaluation, including metadata (filter id, time, query hash, item count).
- **FilterManifestDiff**: Describes changes detected when comparing two manifests: items added, removed, modified, or unchanged.

## Public Methods

### BuildManifest

```cpp
FilterManifest BuildManifest(
    std::string const& filterId,
    std::vector<FilterItem> const& items,
    FilterDef const& filterDef
) const;
```

Builds a new `FilterManifest` from filter evaluation results. Each item is represented as a `FilterManifestEntry`, capturing its index, key, source path, and current source modification time.

---

### WriteManifest

```cpp
bool WriteManifest(
    FilterManifest const& manifest,
    std::string const& workflowBaseDir,
    std::string& errorMessage
) const;
```

Serializes the manifest as JSON and writes it to `<workflowBaseDir>/<filterID>/<filterID>.manifest.json`. Ensures the directory exists. Returns `true` on success, `false` on error (with `errorMessage`).

---

### ReadManifest

```cpp
bool ReadManifest(
    std::string const& filterId,
    std::string const& workflowBaseDir,
    FilterManifest& manifestOut,
    std::string& errorMessage
) const;
```

Reads a previously written manifest from disk and deserializes it into `manifestOut`. Returns `false` on failure (e.g., file missing or malformed), setting `errorMessage`.

---

### CompareManifests

```cpp
FilterManifestDiff CompareManifests(
    FilterManifest const& previous,
    FilterManifest const& current
) const;
```

Compares a new manifest (`current`) to a previous manifest (`previous`). Returns a `FilterManifestDiff` indicating:
- If the filter query/expression changed (`m_ExpressionChanged`)
- New items (indices)
- Removed items (keys)
- Items with changed source modification times (indices)
- Unchanged items (indices)

If the query hash changed, all items are considered new.

---

### ComputeQueryHash *(static)*

```cpp
static std::string ComputeQueryHash(FilterDef const& filterDef);
```

Computes a SHA-256 hash (hex string) of the normalized filter expression based on relevant fields in `filterDef`. Used to detect filter configuration changes. Falls back to `std::hash` if OpenSSL SHA not available.

---

### ManifestPath *(static)*

```cpp
static std::string ManifestPath(
    std::string const& filterId,
    std::string const& workflowBaseDir
);
```

Forms the full path to a manifest file for a given filter id and workflow directory.

---

## Private Static Methods

- **NowIso8601()**: Returns the current UTC time as an ISO 8601 string.
- **FileMtimeString(path)**: Returns the file's last modification time as string (ISO 8601), or empty if unavailable.
- **JsonEscape(s)**: Escapes a string for safe JSON output.

---

## Typical Usage Flow

1. **Build a Manifest** after evaluating a filter:  
   `auto manifest = manager.BuildManifest(filterId, items, filterDef);`

2. **Write** the manifest to disk:  
   `manager.WriteManifest(manifest, workflowBaseDir, errorMsg);`

3. **Read** a previous manifest:  
   `manager.ReadManifest(filterId, workflowBaseDir, previousManifest, errorMsg);`

4. **Compare** new and previous manifests to detect added/removed/modified items:  
   `auto diff = manager.CompareManifests(previousManifest, currentManifest);`

---

## File Formats

Manifests are stored as JSON files, containing:
- filter_id
- evaluated_at (ISO 8601 UTC)
- query_hash (SHA-256)
- item_count
- items[] (`index`, `key`, `source_path`, `source_mtime`)

---

## Error Handling

- Most methods return a boolean status and populate an `errorMessage` string on failure.
- JSON parsing uses `simdjson`.
- Directory creation and file system operations are guarded and error-reported.

---

## License

Copyright (c) 2026 JC Technolabs  
License: GPL-3.0

---

## Summary Table

| Method             | Purpose                        |
|--------------------|--------------------------------|
| BuildManifest      | Create manifest from results   |
| WriteManifest      | Save manifest as JSON          |
| ReadManifest       | Load manifest from JSON file   |
| CompareManifests   | Diff manifests for changes     |
| ComputeQueryHash   | Canonical filter hash          |
| ManifestPath       | Get manifest file path         |
| NowIso8601         | ISO-8601 current time          |
| FileMtimeString    | File mod time as string        |
| JsonEscape         | Escape string for JSON         |


---

### 82_doc_application_workflow_filter_polarionClient_h

<a id="82-doc-application-workflow-filter-polarionclient-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/82_doc_application_workflow_filter_polarionClient_h/docu.output.md`



# PolarionClient C++ Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**License:** GPL-3.0 (© 2026 JC Technolabs)

## Overview

`PolarionClient` provides methods to interact with the Polarion ALM REST API, supporting work item queries, CRUD operations, file and attachment management, and traceability traversal. Authentication is performed via bearer token (Personal Access Token) resolved by KeyManager. Results are paginated, written per-item to disk, and full query results are parsed and returned as `FilterItems`.

## Usage Highlights

- **Work item fetching**: Paginated, auto-stopping when page size is not reached or max items hits.
- **Per-item file output**: Writes each API work item to its own JSON file under `<workflowBaseDir>/<filterID>/`.
- **REST API operations**: Implements GET, POST, PATCH methods; supports binary attachment download/upload.
- **Error handling**: All HTTP/file/parse errors are reported via output error messages.

---

## Public Interface

### Work Item Query

#### `std::vector<FilterItem> FetchAll(const FilterDef& filter, const std::string& workflowBaseDir, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Fetches all work items matching the filter from Polarion using REST API pagination.  
- Returns a vector of `FilterItem` with parsed work items.
- Writes each item as a JSON file: `<workflowBaseDir>/<filterId>/<filterId>-<index>.json`.
- `errorMessage`: Empty on success, nonempty on failure.

### HTTP Operations

#### `bool HttpGet(const std::string& url, const std::string& bearerToken, std::string& responseBody, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Performs an HTTP GET with Bearer token.  
- Fills `responseBody` with returned data.
- Returns true on success.

#### `bool HttpRequest(const std::string& method, const std::string& url, const std::string& bearerToken, const std::string& requestBody, std::string& responseBody, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Performs an HTTP request of any method (e.g., PATCH, POST), optionally with JSON body.  
- For create/update operations.  
- Returns true on success.

#### `bool HttpDownloadFile(const std::string& url, const std::string& bearerToken, const std::string& outputPath, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Downloads a binary file from URL to `outputPath`.  
- Returns true on success.

#### `bool HttpUploadFile(const std::string& url, const std::string& bearerToken, const std::string& filePath, const std::string& fileName, std::string& responseBody, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Uploads a file as a multipart form POST attachment.  
- Returns true on success.

### Work Item Management

#### `bool UpdateWorkItem(const std::string& baseUrl, const std::string& projectId, const std::string& workItemId, const std::string& bearerToken, const std::string& jsonApiPatchBody, std::string& responseBody, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Updates a Polarion work item by PATCH.  
- Returns true on success.

#### `bool CreateWorkItem(const std::string& baseUrl, const std::string& projectId, const std::string& bearerToken, const std::string& jsonApiPostBody, std::string& responseBody, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Creates a new work item by POST.  
- Returns true on success.

### Attachment Management

#### `bool DownloadAttachment(const std::string& baseUrl, const std::string& projectId, const std::string& workItemId, const std::string& attachmentId, const std::string& bearerToken, const std::string& outputPath, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Downloads a work item attachment's content.  
- Returns true on success.

#### `bool UploadAttachment(const std::string& baseUrl, const std::string& projectId, const std::string& workItemId, const std::string& bearerToken, const std::string& filePath, const std::string& fileName, std::string& responseBody, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Uploads a file as a work item attachment.  
- Returns true on success.

### Traceability / Linked Items

#### `bool FetchLinkedWorkItems(const std::string& baseUrl, const std::string& projectId, const std::string& workItemId, const std::string& bearerToken, std::string& responseBody, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Retrieves all linked work items for a given item.  
- Returns true on success.

### Utility

#### `static std::string UrlEncode(const std::string& value)`

URL-encodes a string using cURL.

---

## Private Static Helpers

- **`BuildPageUrl`**: Constructs REST work item URL for a single pagination page using `FilterSource` and page number.
- **`ParseJsonApiPage`**: Parses a paginated JSON:API page from the response, extracting work items as `FilterItem`s.
- **`WriteItemFile`**: Writes a single item as compact JSON.

---

## Configuration & Constants

- `DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE`: 100  
- `MAX_RETRIES`: 3  
- `TIMEOUT_SECONDS`: 30

---

## Implementation Notes

- **Authentication**: Bearer tokens are loaded from KeyManager via the `key_name` in `FilterSource`.
- **Error Handling**: All HTTP, I/O, and JSON errors are surfaced in `errorMessage`; fetch functions return `false`/empty on error.
- **JSON Parsing**: Uses simdjson for fast extraction of fields from API responses.
- **Disk Output**: Each fetched item is written as an individual, filename-safe JSON file, minimally escaped.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::PolarionClient client;
std::string error;
auto items = client.FetchAll(myFilterDef, "/tmp/workflow", error);
if (!error.empty()) {
    // handle error
}
// items now contains FilterItem objects for each work item
```

## Dependencies

- cURL (libcurl)
- simdjson
- Project-specific classes: `FilterDef`, `FilterItem`, `FilterSource`, `KeyManager`
- Filesystem and threading (C++17+)

---

## License

GPL-3.0. See source for license terms.


---

### 83_doc_application_workflow_filter_queryParser_h

<a id="83-doc-application-workflow-filter-queryparser-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/83_doc_application_workflow_filter_queryParser_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::QueryParser C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::QueryParser` provides parsing and evaluation for a Lucene-style query language. It supports logical operators, field-value matching, wildcards, and range queries. The class can convert a human-readable query string into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) and evaluate if data documents match the parsed query.

---

## Supported Query Syntax

- **Logical operators:**  
  - `AND`, `OR`, `NOT`
  - `-field:value` is shorthand for `NOT field:value`  
- **Grouping:**  
  - Parentheses group subexpressions: `( ... )`
- **Field matching:**  
  - `field:value`
  - Wildcard match: `field:prefix*`
- **Ranges:**  
  - Inclusive: `field:[lo TO hi]`
  - Exclusive: `field:{lo TO hi}`
- **Values:**  
  - Unquoted words or `"quoted strings"`

---

## Key Types

### `enum class QueryNodeType`
AST node types:
- `And`         — logical AND
- `Or`          — logical OR
- `Not`         — logical NOT
- `FieldMatch`  — field:value or field:prefix*
- `FieldRange`  — field:[lo TO hi] or field:{lo TO hi}

### `struct QueryNode`
Represents a node in the parsed query AST.
- `QueryNodeType m_Type`
- `std::vector<std::unique_ptr<QueryNode>> m_Children` — for `And`/`Or`
- `std::unique_ptr<QueryNode> m_Child` — for `Not`
- `std::string m_Field`, `std::string m_Value`, `bool m_IsWildcard` — for `FieldMatch`
- `std::string m_RangeLo`, `m_RangeHi`, `bool m_RangeLoInclusive`, `m_RangeHiInclusive` — for `FieldRange`

### `using QueryDocument = std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>`
Represents a document as a mapping from field to value.

---

## Main API

### `std::unique_ptr<QueryNode> Parse(const std::string& queryString, std::string& errorMessage) const`
- Parses a query string into its AST.
- On syntax error, returns `nullptr` and sets `errorMessage`.

### `static bool Matches(const QueryNode& node, const QueryDocument& doc)`
- Evaluates if a document matches the parsed query AST.

---

## Tokenizer (internal)

### `enum class TokenType`
Types of tokens recognized by the parser (words, punctuation, keywords).

### `struct Token`
Represents a token with type and text content.

---

## Parsing Algorithm

- Recursive descent based on the following grammar:
    ```
    query     := orExpr
    orExpr    := andExpr ("OR" andExpr)*
    andExpr   := unaryExpr ("AND" unaryExpr)*
    unaryExpr := "NOT" unaryExpr | "-" fieldExpr | "(" query ")" | fieldExpr
    fieldExpr := FIELD ":" ( rangeExpr | value )
    rangeExpr := "[" value "TO" value "]" | "{" value "TO" value "}"
    value     := WORD | QUOTED_STRING
    ```
- Implicit AND and OR chaining.
- Wildcards allowed at the end of values (prefix matches).
- Range queries use `[lo TO hi]` or `{lo TO hi}` (`[` and `]` are inclusive).

---

## Evaluation

- Logical nodes (`And`, `Or`, `Not`) apply recursively to children.
- `FieldMatch`: checks document field for exact or prefix match.
- `FieldRange`: compares field value numerically if possible, falling back to lexicographical order.
- If a field is missing in the document, evaluation returns false for that field condition.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
using namespace AIAssistant;

QueryParser parser;
std::string error;
auto root = parser.Parse(R"(name:Alice AND age:[18 TO 29])", error);
if (!root) {
    // handle error
}
QueryDocument doc = {{"name", "Alice"}, {"age", "23"}};
bool isMatch = QueryParser::Matches(*root, doc); // true
```

---

## License

GPL-3.0  
Copyright (c) 2026 JC Technolabs

---

## Summary Table

| Feature               | Support    |
|-----------------------|------------|
| AND, OR, NOT logic    | Yes        |
| Parentheses/grouping  | Yes        |
| Field:value match     | Yes        |
| Wildcards             | Yes, prefix only (`*`)  |
| Range queries         | Yes (`[lo TO hi]`, `{lo TO hi}`) |
| String and quoted support | Yes  |
| Negation via `-field:value` | Yes |
| Document format       | `std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>` |
| Type of comparison    | Numeric if possible, else lexicographical |


---

### 84_doc_application_workflow_internalTaskExecutor_h

<a id="84-doc-application-workflow-internaltaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/84_doc_application_workflow_internalTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# InternalTaskExecutor C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::InternalTaskExecutor` is a class that executes "internal tasks" defined by a workflow. It implements the `ITaskExecutor` interface, coordinating task registry, input/output files, and execution flow.

## Purpose

- Runs internal tasks described in a workflow, leveraging a registry of available internal actions.
- Resolves and prepares input/output file paths and ensures correct working directories.
- Validates inputs, manages outputs, and handles task-specific parameters.
- Handles task errors, logging, and state management (e.g., task failed/succeeded).

## Key Components

### Constructor

```cpp
explicit InternalTaskExecutor(std::shared_ptr<IInternalTaskRegistry> const& internalTaskRegistryPtr);
```
* **internalTaskRegistryPtr**: Shared pointer to the internal task registry used to retrieve task implementations.

### Destructor

```cpp
virtual ~InternalTaskExecutor() = default;
```

### Execute Method

```cpp
bool Execute(
    WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition,
    WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
    TaskDef const& taskDefinition,
    TaskInstanceState& taskState
) override;
```
* Executes a single internal task; returns `true` on success, `false` on failure.
* Main steps:
  1. Resolves workflow and task directories.
  2. Ensures working and output directories exist.
  3. Resolves and validates all input files.
  4. Checks output directories, creating them if necessary.
  5. Parses `m_ParamsJson` to extract the "action" field.
  6. Looks up the action in the internal task registry.
  7. Calls the associated internal task's `Execute`.
  8. Sets `taskState` according to success or failure.

## Internal Functions

- **TryGetActionFromParamsJson**: Parses JSON parameters, extracting the `"action"` string and performing validation.
- **ValidateExistingInputs**: Checks that all required input files exist before task execution.
- **EnsureOutputParentDirectories**: Creates any parent directories needed for output files.

## Error Handling

- On any step failure (e.g., missing inputs, invalid parameters, unregistered actions), the appropriate error message is set in `taskState.m_LastErrorMessage`, the state is set to `Failed`, and the method returns `false`.
- Extensive logging is performed for debugging and tracing.

## Requirements

- Expects JSON-encoded parameters with at least an `"action"` field.
- Relies on `IInternalTaskRegistry` to map action names to executable task implementations.
- Uses file system utilities to resolve and manage paths.

## Usage Example

1. Construct with a pointer to a task registry.
2. Call `Execute()` with workflow, task, and state arguments.
3. Check the returned boolean for task success or failure.

## Thread Safety

No guarantees provided; instance should not be used concurrently from different threads unless otherwise handled externally.

## Related Types

- `ITaskExecutor`: Interface implemented by `InternalTaskExecutor`.
- `IInternalTaskRegistry`: Provides internally registered tasks.

### 85_doc_application_workflow_jcwfContainer_h

<a id="85-doc-application-workflow-jcwfcontainer-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/85_doc_application_workflow_jcwfContainer_h/docu.output.md`



# JcwfContainer Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `jcwfContainer.h`  
**Purpose:** Provides static methods to manage JCWF containers (workflow zip archives).

---

## Overview

The `JcwfContainer` class provides utilities for handling `.jcwf` container files. These are ZIP archives used to store workflow definitions, metadata, and sub-workflows for the AIAssistant system.

A `.jcwf` container typically has this structure:

- `global.json` : Workflow metadata (triggers, version, author, defaults)
- `<name>.json` : Root workflow task DAG
- `<subfolder>/` : Sub-workflow folders
  - `<subfolder>.json` : Sub-workflow DAG
  - `<nested>/` : Nested sub-workflows (recursive structure)

All functionality is provided as static methods. No object instantiation is required.

---

## Public Static Methods

### 1. `Extract`

```cpp
static bool Extract(const std::filesystem::path& jcwfPath, const std::filesystem::path& targetDir,
                    std::string& errorMessage);
```

- Unpacks a `.jcwf` zip archive to the specified target directory.
- Creates the target directory if it does not exist.
- Returns `true` on success; `false` on failure (error reason in `errorMessage`).

---

### 2. `Pack`

```cpp
static bool Pack(const std::filesystem::path& sourceDir, const std::filesystem::path& jcwfPath,
                 std::string& errorMessage);
```

- Compresses `sourceDir` (recursively) into a `.jcwf` zip file at `jcwfPath`.
- Returns `true` on success; `false` on failure (error reason in `errorMessage`).

---

### 3. `ReadFile`

```cpp
static bool ReadFile(const std::filesystem::path& jcwfPath, const std::string& internalPath,
                     std::string& outContent, std::string& errorMessage);
```

- Reads a single file from a `.jcwf` zip archive without full extraction.
- `internalPath` uses forward slashes (e.g. `"global.json"` or `"sub/sub.json"`).
- Returns `true` and writes file contents to `outContent` on success; `false` on failure (error reason in `errorMessage`).

---

### 4. `ListEntries`

```cpp
static std::vector<std::string> ListEntries(const std::filesystem::path& jcwfPath);
```

- Lists all file and directory entries in a `.jcwf` zip archive.
- Returns vector of entry paths (strings). Returns empty vector on error.

---

### 5. `IsValidContainer`

```cpp
static bool IsValidContainer(const std::filesystem::path& jcwfPath);
```

- Checks whether the specified file is a readable ZIP (valid `.jcwf` container).
- Returns `true` if valid, `false` otherwise.

---

### 6. `IsExtractedStale`

```cpp
static bool IsExtractedStale(const std::filesystem::path& jcwfPath,
                            const std::filesystem::path& extractedDir);
```

- Determines if the target extracted folder is stale (i.e., the `.jcwf` file is newer than the directory).
- Returns `true` if directory is missing or older, `false` otherwise.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
#include "jcwfContainer.h"

using namespace AIAssistant;

std::string error;
if (!JcwfContainer::Extract("workflow.jcwf", "unpacked_dir", error)) {
    // handle error
}
```

---

## Dependencies

- C++ `<filesystem>`, `<string>`, `<vector>`, `<fstream>`
- [miniz](https://github.com/richgel999/miniz) library for ZIP handling
- Logging macro: `LOG_APP_INFO`

---

## License

GPL-3.0 (see class header for full details).


---

### 86_doc_application_workflow_pythonTaskExecutor_h

<a id="86-doc-application-workflow-pythontaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/86_doc_application_workflow_pythonTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# PythonTaskExecutor Class Documentation

## Overview

`AIAssistant::PythonTaskExecutor` is a C++ class that implements the `ITaskExecutor` interface. It executes workflow tasks defined to run Python functions or scripts. It manages task directories, validates inputs, sets up execution contexts, invokes the Python engine, handles standard output/error logs, and collects results for workflow automation.

---

## Class Declaration

```cpp
namespace AIAssistant
{
    class PythonTaskExecutor : public ITaskExecutor
    {
    public:
        virtual ~PythonTaskExecutor() = default;
        bool Execute(
            WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition,
            WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
            TaskDef const& taskDefinition,
            TaskInstanceState& taskState
        ) override;
    };
}
```

---

## Public Methods

### Execute

**Signature:**

```cpp
bool Execute(
    WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition,
    WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
    TaskDef const& taskDefinition,
    TaskInstanceState& taskState
) override;
```

**Description:**

Runs a Python-based workflow task as defined by the inputs and associated context. Manages input validation, prepares execution environments, injects relevant metadata, launches Python tasks via a Python engine pool, and captures both outputs and logs.

**Parameters:**

- `workflowDefinition`: The workflow structure to which the task belongs.
- `workflowRun`: The current run instance, containing the context and run information.
- `taskDefinition`: The definition/specification of the task to execute, including input/output files and parameters.
- `taskState`: The execution state for the task instance, updated with output values, state, error messages, and captured logs.

**Returns:**

- `true` if the task was completed successfully.
- `false` if the task failed (e.g., input validation failed, Python execution error).

---

## Detailed Execution Flow

1. **Python Engine Pool Lookup**  
   Checks for the availability of a `PythonEnginePool` instance. Aborts if not present.

2. **Working Directory Preparation**  
   Resolves the working directory for the task. Tries to create the directory if it does not exist. Logs success or failure, and aborts if creation fails.

3. **Input Validation**  
   Verifies that all file inputs required by the task exist in the working directory. Aborts if any are missing.

4. **Context Preparation**  
   - Aggregates workflow context values and internal IDs (workflow ID, run ID, directories).
   - Binds input file paths as `_file_input_x` in context for Python scripts.

5. **Call Argument Preparation**  
   Prepares function arguments, including:
   - Input values from the workflow state.
   - Output file paths for tasks with declared output slots.

6. **Execution (Python Function/Script)**  
   Calls `ExecuteWorkflowTask` on the Python engine pool with all arguments, capturing `stdout`, `stderr`, and output values.

7. **Output Capture and Logging**  
   - Stores full `stdout` and `stderr` logs as files in the working dir.
   - Truncates and saves logs in state for UI/tooltips.

8. **Error Handling**  
   Updates the task state and logs if validation, directory setup, or the Python task itself fails.

9. **Outputs and Postprocessing**  
   - Transfers result output values from Python execution to the task state.
   - Fills any missing output slots by deriving values based on the file structure.
   - Marks task as succeeded if execution completes.

---

## Typical Usage Context

- Designed for use within the `AIAssistant` framework.
- Supports automated, reproducible execution of Python functions/scripts as workflow steps.
- Handles environment management and data exchange between C++ workflows and Python code.

---

## Licensing

- Copyright (c) 2025 JC Technolabs
- Licensed under GPL-3.0

---

## Related Components

- `ITaskExecutor` (interface)
- `PythonEnginePool`
- `TaskDef`, `WorkflowDefinition`, `WorkflowRun`, `TaskInstanceState`
- `TaskPathResolver` (path and file resolution for tasks)

---

## Notes

- Standard output/error are captured and written to files for debugging.
- All error conditions are logged and reported in the task state.
- Workflow context and I/O are flexible via the input/output slot mechanism.

---


---

### 87_doc_application_workflow_scriptCatalog_h

<a id="87-doc-application-workflow-scriptcatalog-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/87_doc_application_workflow_scriptCatalog_h/docu.output.md`



# ScriptCatalog Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `workflow/scriptCatalog.h`  
**License:** GPL-3.0 (see full license in source comments)  
**Copyright (c) 2026 JC Technolabs**

---

## Overview

`ScriptCatalog` maintains a catalog of executable scripts (shell, python, powershell) in a specified directory. It scans the scripts, extracts standardized metadata from comment blocks, and exposes structured information to allow querying, listing, and selecting available scripts for workflow automation.

---

## Metadata Extraction

The class expects scripts to include metadata in comment blocks, recognized by tags such as `@jarvis-script`, `@short:`, `@params:`, `@description:`, and `@outputs:` in the first 50–60 lines. Formats supported: shell (`.sh`, `.ps1`) and Python (`.py`).

---

## Public Types

### `struct Entry`

Describes a discovered script.

| Member            | Description                                                                               |
|-------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `m_Path`          | Script path, relative to launch (e.g. `"scripts/foo.sh"`).                               |
| `m_Type`          | Script type: `"shell"` or `"python"`.                                                    |
| `m_Module`        | Python module name (e.g. `"scripts.extractChapters"`), empty for shell scripts.          |
| `m_Short`         | One-line description from `@short:` tag.                                                 |
| `m_Description`   | Multi-line description from `@description:` tag.                                         |
| `m_Params`        | List of parameter names from `@params:` tag.                                             |
| `m_Outputs`       | Output description from `@outputs:` tag.                                                 |
| `m_HasShebang`    | Whether the script file starts with a shebang line.                                      |
| `m_HasJarvisMarker` | True if `@jarvis-script` marker found in metadata.                                     |
| `m_Executable`    | True if file is marked executable by filesystem permissions.                             |

---

## Public Methods

### `void Refresh(const std::filesystem::path& scriptsBaseFolder)`

Re-scans the given directory (and subdirectories), rebuilding the catalog with updated scripts and metadata. Thread-safe.

**Parameters:**
- `scriptsBaseFolder`: Path to the scripts directory.

---

### `std::vector<Entry> List(const std::string& typeFilter = {}) const`

Returns a vector of all cataloged scripts. Optionally filter by type ("shell" or "python").

**Parameters:**
- `typeFilter`: Script type filter; empty string returns all scripts.

**Returns:**  
`std::vector<Entry>`

---

### `std::optional<Entry> GetByPath(const std::string& relativePath) const`

Looks up a script by its relative path.

**Parameters:**
- `relativePath`: Path relative to catalog root (e.g. `"scripts/foo.sh"`).

**Returns:**  
`std::optional<Entry>` (empty if not found)

---

### `std::optional<Entry> GetByModule(const std::string& moduleName) const`

Looks up a Python script by its module name.

**Parameters:**
- `moduleName`: Python module name (e.g. `"scripts.bar"`).

**Returns:**  
`std::optional<Entry>` (empty if not found)

---

### `std::filesystem::path GetRootDirectory() const`

Returns the scripts directory currently in use.

**Returns:**  
`std::filesystem::path`

---

### `size_t Size() const`

Returns the number of cataloged scripts.

**Returns:**  
`size_t`

---

## Thread Safety

All public methods are thread-safe using an internal mutex.

---

## Internal Details

- Only the first 50–60 lines of each file are read for metadata (cheap even with large script folders).
- Handles multiline descriptions and output tags with indented continuation lines.
- `__init__.py` files are skipped unless they describe a package.
- Only `.sh`, `.py`, and `.ps1` files are considered.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::ScriptCatalog catalog;
catalog.Refresh("scripts/");
auto all_scripts = catalog.List();
auto python_scripts = catalog.List("python");
auto entry = catalog.GetByModule("scripts.extractChapters");
```

---

## Use Cases

- Workflow engines and agents can discover and select scripts based on descriptive metadata.
- Enables dynamic composition and execution of automation tasks.  
- Human and AI users can query the catalog for available scripts and their parameters/outputs.

---


---

### 88_doc_application_workflow_shellTaskExecutor_h

<a id="88-doc-application-workflow-shelltaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/88_doc_application_workflow_shellTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# ShellTaskExecutor C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`ShellTaskExecutor` is a class within the `AIAssistant` namespace that implements the `ITaskExecutor` interface to execute shell tasks defined in workflows. It securely runs scripts (e.g., `.sh`, `.ps1`) as part of a workflow system, provides output capturing, safety checks, and supports cross-platform execution (Windows and POSIX).

---

## Class Definition

```cpp
namespace AIAssistant
{
    class ShellTaskExecutor : public ITaskExecutor
    {
    public:
        virtual ~ShellTaskExecutor() = default;

        bool Execute(
            WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition,
            WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
            TaskDef const& taskDefinition,
            TaskInstanceState& taskState) override;

    #if defined(_WIN32)
        static void ProbeWindowsShell(bool useBashConfig);
        static WindowsShell GetWindowsShell();
    #endif

    private:
        bool ValidateScriptPath(std::string const& path) const;
        bool IsSafeArgument(std::string const& argument) const;

    #if defined(_WIN32)
        static WindowsShell s_WindowsShell;
    #endif
    };
}
```

---

## Purpose

- Executes external script files (shell commands) as defined by workflow/task descriptions.
- Ensures scripts are executed safely, with output logging and environmental isolation as needed.
- Provides cross-platform compatibility, using Bash or PowerShell on Windows as available.

---

## Key Methods

### Public

#### `bool Execute(const WorkflowDefinition&, WorkflowRun&, const TaskDef&, TaskInstanceState&) override;`

Executes a shell script as specified by the task definition.

- **Parameters:**
  - `workflowDefinition`: Defines the overall workflow context.
  - `workflowRun`: Workflow runtime data (used for broader context, not modified).
  - `taskDefinition`: Details of the shell task including command, arguments, inputs, and outputs.
  - `taskState`: Mutable state for this task instance (updated with results).

- **Behavior:**
  - Resolves working directory and validates input/output file existence.
  - Parses the command and arguments (supports JCWF `{{...}}` template expansion).
  - Validates that scripts only run from allowed paths (`scripts/` folder).
  - Checks argument safety (guards against typical shell injection vectors).
  - Runs the command in the specified environment, capturing stdout/stderr to files and logging.
  - Handles timeouts (inactivity cutoff, if configured).
  - Updates `taskState` with execution result, output file locations, return code, and sample output.

- **Returns:**  
  `true` if the command executed and completed successfully (exit code 0); otherwise, `false`.

#### `#if defined(_WIN32)`

##### `static void ProbeWindowsShell(bool useBashConfig);`

- Determines the preferred Windows shell for script execution: Bash (if present and requested) or PowerShell.
- Probes the system for Bash availability if `useBashConfig` is `true`.
- Should be called once at program startup.

##### `static WindowsShell GetWindowsShell();`

- Returns the cached Windows shell selection (Bash or PowerShell) determined by `ProbeWindowsShell`.

### Private

#### `bool ValidateScriptPath(std::string const& path) const;`

- Verifies that the given script path starts with `scripts/` and does not escape the allowed directory, even after normalization.

#### `bool IsSafeArgument(std::string const& argument) const;`

- Checks that a command-line argument contains only safe characters (rejects common shell injection characters and control characters).

---

## Safety and Security

- **Script Path Restriction:** Only scripts under the `scripts/` directory may be executed.
- **Argument Sanitization:** Forbids use of dangerous shell meta-characters in command arguments.
- **Template Expansion:** Uses a strict template engine to expand arguments from variables, inputs, and outputs.
- **Working Directory Isolation:** Runs each task inside a dedicated working directory; ensures required parent directories exist.
- **Input/Output Checks:** Fails early if required input files are missing or output directories cannot be created.
- **Timeouts:** Supports inactivity-based execution timeouts (POSIX only).

---

## Platform-specific Behavior

- **Windows:**  
  - Default shell is PowerShell. If Bash is requested/configured and available, Bash will be used.
  - If a `.sh` script is specified but PowerShell is active, will look for a `.ps1` sibling script automatically.

- **POSIX:**  
  - Uses `/bin/sh` for command execution.
  - Supports inactivity timeouts via fork/exec with output polling.

---

## Output Handling

- Captures both stdout and stderr of the shell process.
- Logs output lines in real-time through the application logger.
- Stores full outputs in `stdout.txt` and `stderr.txt` in the task working directory.
- Keeps a truncated (up to 1024 chars) version of output for UI frontend display.

---

## Typical Usage Pattern

1. Probe and set up the shell environment (`ProbeWindowsShell` on Windows).
2. For each shell task, call `Execute()` with the prepared workflow and task data.
3. Examine `taskState` for success/failure, error messages, and outputs after execution.

---

## Licensing

- GPL-3.0 License.
- See comments in source file for license terms.

---

## See Also

- `ITaskExecutor`: Parent interface.
- `TaskDef`, `TaskInstanceState`, `WorkflowDefinition`: Data structures for task specification and tracking.
- `TemplateEngine`: Used to expand arguments with variable/data references.

---

## Summary Table

| Method                          | Description                                                              |
|----------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `Execute`                       | Runs the shell task as described, manages I/O and state.                 |
| `ProbeWindowsShell` (Windows)    | Sets preferred shell (Bash/PowerShell) for execution.                    |
| `GetWindowsShell` (Windows)      | Returns cached shell selection.                                          |
| `ValidateScriptPath`             | Ensures script path is in allowed directory.                             |
| `IsSafeArgument`                 | Checks that arguments are free from dangerous characters.                |

---

## Limitations

- Does not sandbox scripts beyond path/argument validation.
- Windows shell detection must be performed before execution.
- Only scripts in `scripts/` can be launched.
- Template expansion is strict; malformed templates cause task failure.

---

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::ShellTaskExecutor executor;
bool ok = executor.Execute(workflowDef, workflowRun, taskDef, taskState);

if (ok) {
    // Task succeeded; outputs are in taskState
} else {
    // Task failed; check taskState.m_LastErrorMessage
}
```

---

## Contact

For questions, see the header for copyright and licensing information.


---

### 89_doc_application_workflow_subWorkflowTaskExecutor_h

<a id="89-doc-application-workflow-subworkflowtaskexecutor-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/89_doc_application_workflow_subWorkflowTaskExecutor_h/docu.output.md`



# SubWorkflowTaskExecutor (C++ Class)

## Overview

`SubWorkflowTaskExecutor` is a final class in the `AIAssistant` namespace that implements the `ITaskExecutor` interface. It handles execution of a "sub-workflow" task by enqueuing a child workflow during the run of a parent workflow. It requires references to a `WorkflowRegistry` and a `WorkflowRuntimeManager` to operate.

## Key Responsibilities

- Executes tasks that represent sub-workflows.
- Resolves the child workflow file path.
- Looks up the child workflow in the registry.
- Enqueues the child workflow and sets up parent-child run relationships.
- Handles error checking and state transitions for task execution.

## Public Methods

### Constructor

```cpp
explicit SubWorkflowTaskExecutor(WorkflowRegistry const* workflowRegistry)
```
Initializes the executor with a reference to a workflow registry.

### Destructor

```cpp
virtual ~SubWorkflowTaskExecutor() = default;
```
Default destructor.

### SetRuntimeManager

```cpp
void SetRuntimeManager(WorkflowRuntimeManager* runtimeManager)
```
Sets the runtime manager reference. Must be called after construction but before execution.

### Execute

```cpp
bool Execute(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition,
             WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
             TaskDef const& taskDefinition,
             TaskInstanceState& taskState) override
```
- Executes the sub-workflow task.
- Resolves the workflow file for the child.
- Looks up the child workflow in the registry.
- Enqueues a run for the child workflow.
- Registers parent-child linkage with the runtime manager.
- On success, sets task state to `WaitingExternal` and stores the run ID.
- On failure, sets task state to `Failed` and logs an error.

## Internal State

- `WorkflowRegistry const* m_WorkflowRegistry`: Non-owning reference to the workflow registry.
- `WorkflowRuntimeManager* m_RuntimeManager`: Reference to the runtime manager, set via `SetRuntimeManager`.

## Usage

1. Construct with a pointer to a `WorkflowRegistry`.
2. Call `SetRuntimeManager` with a pointer to a `WorkflowRuntimeManager` after registration.
3. Call `Execute` for tasks representing sub-workflows.

## Error Handling

- If the runtime manager, workflow registry, or workflow file is unset/invalid, execution fails and logs an error.
- If child workflow lookup or enqueue fails, execution fails and logs an error.

## State Transition

- On success: `taskState.m_State = WaitingExternal`, with the child run ID captured for coordination.
- On failure: `taskState.m_State = Failed`, with error message set and logged.

## Logging

Logs errors and info during each major step for observability and debugging.

## Dependencies

- `ITaskExecutor`
- `WorkflowRegistry`
- `WorkflowRuntimeManager`
- `WorkflowDefinition`
- `WorkflowRun`
- `TaskDef`
- `TaskInstanceState`

---

**Copyright © 2026 JC Technolabs — License: GPL-3.0**

### 90_doc_application_workflow_taskExecutor_h

<a id="90-doc-application-workflow-taskexecutor-h"></a>

# ITaskExecutor Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`

## Overview

`ITaskExecutor` is an abstract base class that defines an interface for executing individual tasks within a workflow system.

## Purpose

This class serves as the standardized entry point for executing defined tasks (`TaskDef`) as part of a workflow (`WorkflowDefinition`), maintaining the state (`TaskInstanceState`) within each workflow run (`WorkflowRun`).

## Class Definition

```cpp
class ITaskExecutor
{
public:
    virtual ~ITaskExecutor() = default;

    // The unified executor entry point.
    virtual bool Execute(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun& workflowRun,
                         TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState& taskState) = 0;
};
```

## Methods

### `virtual bool Execute(const WorkflowDefinition&, WorkflowRun&, const TaskDef&, TaskInstanceState&) = 0;`

- **Description:**  
  Pure virtual method that acts as the unified entry point for task execution. Must be overridden by derived classes.

- **Parameters:**
  - `const WorkflowDefinition& workflowDefinition`  
    The definition of the workflow containing the task.
  - `WorkflowRun& workflowRun`  
    The current run instance of the workflow.
  - `const TaskDef& taskDefinition`  
    The definition of the specific task to be executed.
  - `TaskInstanceState& taskState`  
    The current state of the task instance.

- **Returns:**  
  `bool` — `true` if task execution succeeded, `false` otherwise.

## Inheritance

- Inherit from this class to provide concrete implementations for task execution in workflow management systems.

## Related Types

- `WorkflowDefinition`
- `WorkflowRun`
- `TaskDef`
- `TaskInstanceState`

(Definitions for these types are in included headers: `workflowTypes.h`, `workflowRegistry.h`.)

## Licensing

- Licensed under GPL-3.0.
- See source file comments for details.

### 91_doc_application_workflow_taskExecutorRegistry_h

<a id="91-doc-application-workflow-taskexecutorregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/91_doc_application_workflow_taskExecutorRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



# TaskExecutorRegistry Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`

## Overview

`TaskExecutorRegistry` manages registration and execution of task executors for various task types in an AI workflow system. It serves as a singleton registry mapping `TaskType` to corresponding `ITaskExecutor` implementations, allowing modular execution of tasks based on their type. It handles pre-execution logic such as materializing input files into the task's working directory.

---

## Public Interface

### Static Methods

#### `static TaskExecutorRegistry& Get();`
Returns the singleton instance of the registry.

---

### Member Functions

#### `void RegisterExecutor(TaskType type, std::shared_ptr<ITaskExecutor> const& executorPtr);`

Registers an executor instance for a specified `TaskType`.  
- **Parameters:**
  - `type`: Type of the task.
  - `executorPtr`: Shared pointer to the executor implementing `ITaskExecutor`.

#### `bool Execute(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun& workflowRun, TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState& taskState);`

Executes the specified task using its registered executor.  
- **Parameters:**
  - `workflowDefinition`: Definition of the current workflow.
  - `workflowRun`: Current run instance of the workflow.
  - `taskDefinition`: Definition of the task to execute.
  - `taskState`: State and context of the task instance.
- **Returns:**  
  `true` if the task executed successfully, `false` otherwise. Sets error state in `taskState` on failure.

---

## Internal Details

- Uses an internal map:  
  `std::unordered_map<TaskType, std::shared_ptr<ITaskExecutor>> m_Executors;`  
  Mapping each `TaskType` to its corresponding executor instance.

- On `Execute`:
    - Looks up the executor for the task type.
    - If not found, logs an error and marks the task as failed.
    - Before execution, calls an internal file materialization step to ensure required input files are prepared in the task's working directory.
    - On materialization failure, marks the task as failed.
    - Calls the registered executor's `Execute` function with the provided parameters.

---

## File Materialization

Before the executor runs, `MaterializeFiles` ensures any specified input files are copied and templated into the task's working directory, enabling flexible file handling for different task types.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
// Register a new executor
TaskExecutorRegistry::Get().RegisterExecutor(MyTaskType, std::make_shared<MyTaskExecutor>());

// Execute a task
bool ok = TaskExecutorRegistry::Get().Execute(workflowDef, workflowRun, taskDef, taskState);
```

---

## Thread Safety

Singleton pattern ensures a single registry instance. No explicit thread safety guarantees for executor registration or execution.

---

## License

Licensed under GPL-3.0 by JC Technolabs, 2025.

---

## Related Classes

- `ITaskExecutor`
- `WorkflowDefinition`
- `WorkflowRun`
- `TaskDef`
- `TaskInstanceState`

---

## Summary

`TaskExecutorRegistry` abstracts registration and dispatch of task executors by type, adds pre-execution file handling, and centralizes error tracking for task execution in AI workflow systems.


---

### 92_doc_application_workflow_taskFreshnessChecker_h

<a id="92-doc-application-workflow-taskfreshnesschecker-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/92_doc_application_workflow_taskFreshnessChecker_h/docu.output.md`



# TaskFreshnessChecker (C++ Class)

Determines whether a workflow "task" is up to date by comparing input and output file timestamps, following Makefile-style logic extended to handle chained dependencies (“upstream outputs”).

## Overview

The `TaskFreshnessChecker` class checks if a task's outputs are current with respect to all its declared inputs and outputs from its upstream dependencies in a workflow. It is intended for usage within build systems, workflow engines, and task pipelines.

## Core Concepts

- **Up-to-date logic:** 
  - All task outputs must exist.
  - The oldest output must be at least as new as the newest input or any upstream output.
  - Inputs are defined as both the task's declared inputs and all outputs from tasks it depends on (recursively).

## Key Structures

### ResolvedPaths

- `m_InputPaths`: List of fully resolved input file paths for the task.
- `m_OutputPaths`: List of fully resolved output file paths for the task.

### TimedPath (Private)

- `m_Path`: Filesystem path.
- `m_Time`: File timestamp (last-write time).

## Main Types

- **ResolveOutputPathsFn:**  
  A function type:  
  `bool(const std::string& taskId, std::vector<std::filesystem::path>& outPaths)`  
  Used to resolve (fill) the list of output paths for the given task.

## Public Methods

### IsTaskUpToDate

```cpp
bool IsTaskUpToDate(
    WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition,
    std::string const& taskId,
    ResolvedPaths const& resolvedPaths,
    ResolveOutputPathsFn const& resolveOutputPaths,
    std::filesystem::path* comparedInputPath = nullptr,
    std::filesystem::path* comparedOutputPath = nullptr
) const;
```

**Purpose:**  
Determines if the specified task is up to date.

**Parameters:**
- `workflowDefinition`: Complete description of the workflow with all tasks and dependencies.
- `taskId`: The task identifier.
- `resolvedPaths`: ResolvedPaths struct defining all inputs and outputs.
- `resolveOutputPaths`: Function for resolving outputs for a given task id.
- `comparedInputPath` (optional): If not null, receives the path of the "newest input" used in the determination.
- `comparedOutputPath` (optional): If not null, receives the path of the "oldest output" used in the determination.

**Returns:**  
`true` if the task is up to date (all outputs exist and oldest output is newer than or equal to the newest input or upstream output), `false` otherwise.

## Private Methods

### CollectUpstreamOutputTimes

```cpp
bool CollectUpstreamOutputTimes(
    WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition,
    std::string const& taskId,
    std::unordered_set<std::string>& visitedTasks,
    std::vector<TimedPath>& outTimes,
    ResolveOutputPathsFn const& resolveOutputPaths
) const;
```

**Purpose:**  
Recursively collects file timestamps for all outputs of tasks the current task depends on (directly or indirectly), avoiding cycles.

## Usage Scenario

- Used inside workflow or build orchestration tools to prevent unnecessary task execution when outputs are already current.
- Useful for reproducibility, cache checks, or incremental processing.

## Behavior Summary

- If any output is missing, the task is not up to date.
- If any input or upstream output is missing, the task is not up to date.
- If oldest output is older than newest input or any upstream output, the task is not up to date.
- Otherwise, the task is considered up to date.

## Dependencies

- Uses C++17 `<filesystem>` for file and time operations.
- Expects external definitions of `WorkflowDefinition`, `TaskDef`, and logging utilities.

## License

GPL-3.0. See source header for terms.


---

### 93_doc_application_workflow_taskPathResolver_h

<a id="93-doc-application-workflow-taskpathresolver-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/93_doc_application_workflow_taskPathResolver_h/docu.output.md`



# TaskPathResolver (C++ Class) Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`

**Purpose:**  
`TaskPathResolver` provides static methods to resolve and manage file system paths for workflow and task execution in an AI-assisted workflow system. It is used to determine absolute and scoped paths for workflow files, tasks, and file freshness checks.

---

## Public Static Methods

### 1. `ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory`
**Signature:**  
`static std::filesystem::path ResolveWorkflowBaseDirectory(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition);`

**Description:**  
Resolves the base directory of a workflow based on a set fallback order:
- `m_WorkflowBaseDirectoryAbsolute`
- `m_WorkflowBaseDirectory`
- `m_WorkflowFileDirectoryAbsolute`
- `m_WorkflowFileDirectory`
- `m_WorkflowFilePathAbsolute.parent_path()`
- `m_WorkflowFilePath.parent_path()`

If the result is relative, it is resolved against `launchCWD`. Returns an empty path if all fields are empty.

---

### 2. `ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath`
**Signature:**  
`static std::filesystem::path ResolveTaskWorkingDirectoryPath(std::filesystem::path const& workflowBaseDirectoryPath, std::string const& taskWorkingDirectoryText);`

**Description:**  
Determines the absolute working directory for a task.  
If the task working directory is empty, it defaults to the workflow base directory.

---

### 3. `ResolveTaskScopedPath`
**Signature:**  
`static std::filesystem::path ResolveTaskScopedPath(std::filesystem::path const& taskWorkingDirectoryPath, std::string const& pathText);`

**Description:**  
Resolves a given path (as string) to an absolute path scoped to the task working directory.  
Relative paths are resolved against the task's working directory.

---

### 4. `ResolvePath`
**Signature:**  
`static std::filesystem::path ResolvePath(std::filesystem::path const& baseDirectoryPath, std::filesystem::path const& path);`

**Description:**  
Resolves a path relative to a specified base directory, if the path is not already absolute.  
Handles cases where the path already contains the base directory segment.

---

### 5. `BuildOutputSlotMap`
**Signature:**  
`static void BuildOutputSlotMap(TaskDef const& taskDefinition, TaskInstanceState const& taskState, std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>& outputSlotMapOut);`

**Description:**  
Builds a mapping from task output slot names to their values using the current task state, limited to declared output fields.  
- Searches outputs first (`m_OutputValues`)
- Falls back to inputs (`m_InputValues`) if output is missing (for backward compatibility)

---

### 6. `ResolveFreshnessPathsForTask`
**Signature:**  
`static bool ResolveFreshnessPathsForTask(WorkflowDefinition const& workflowDefinition, WorkflowRun const& workflowRun, TaskDef const& taskDefinition, std::string const& taskId, std::vector<std::filesystem::path>& inputPathsOut, std::vector<std::filesystem::path>& outputPathsOut);`

**Description:**  
Resolves all file input and output paths (absolute) for a given task, including template expansion using current dataflow values and produced outputs.  
- Handles tasks with inline queue content (forces freshness to depend on workflow file)
- Fills provided vectors with resolved absolute paths
- Returns `false` on failure (e.g. template expansion errors)

---

## Typical Use Cases

- Determining execution directories for workflow and tasks.
- Locating all file dependencies for a workflow step or task.
- Building output mappings for inspection or downstream processing.
- Ensuring correct absolute path resolution in complex, layered workflows.
- Enabling orchestrators to check input/output file "freshness" efficiently.

---

## Notes

- All methods are static; this class is not intended to be instantiated.
- Relies on structures like `WorkflowDefinition`, `TaskDef`, `TaskInstanceState`, and dependencies (`DataflowResolver`, etc.).
- Uses standard `std::filesystem::path` for consistent cross-platform path handling.
- Extensive logging is performed for debugging path resolutions.

---

## License

MIT License (Copyright JC Technolabs)
