### 94_doc_application_workflow_templateEngine_h

<a id="94-doc-application-workflow-templateengine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/94_doc_application_workflow_templateEngine_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::ExpandTemplate — Documentation

## Overview

`ExpandTemplate` is a C++ function from the `AIAssistant` namespace. It processes a template string with embedded `{{...}}` placeholders and expands them using data from provided input, output, and variable maps. It supports strict and lenient expansion modes for handling unresolved placeholders.

---

## Supported Placeholders

- `{{inputs}}`: Expands to a space-separated list of file inputs.
- `{{outputs}}`: Expands to a space-separated list of file outputs.
- `{{input[N]}}`: Expands to the N-th file input (0-based index).
- `{{output[N]}}`: Expands to the N-th file output (0-based index).
- `{{slot.NAME}}`: Looks up `NAME` in the `inputValues` map.
- `{{env.NAME}}`: Looks up `NAME` in the `envVariables` map.
- `{{inputs.NAME}}`: Looks up `NAME` in `inputValues` (dataflow cross-reference).
- `{{anything_else}}`: Bare key lookup in `inputValues`.

---

## Template Modes

- **Strict:**  
  - Unknown keys or invalid indices cause the function to fail, set an error message, and return `false`.
  - Use for shell tasks or when all values must be present.

- **Lenient:**  
  - Unknown keys expand to an empty string.
  - Use for AI calls or flexible template filling.

---

## API Reference

### Enum: `AIAssistant::TemplateMode`

```cpp
enum class TemplateMode
{
    Strict,
    Lenient
};
```

### Struct: `AIAssistant::TemplateContext`

Holds the data sources for template expansion.

| Field                 | Type                                                        | Description                                 |
|-----------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|
| `m_FileInputs`        | `const std::vector<std::string>*`                           | Pointer to file input list                  |
| `m_FileOutputs`       | `const std::vector<std::string>*`                           | Pointer to file output list                 |
| `m_InputValues`       | `const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>*`       | Pointer to mapping of input slot values     |
| `m_EnvVariables`      | `const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::string>*`       | Pointer to environment variable map         |

### Function: `ExpandTemplate`

```cpp
bool ExpandTemplate(
    std::string const& templateText,
    TemplateContext const& context,
    TemplateMode mode,
    std::string& expandedOut,
    std::string& errorMessage
);
```

#### Parameters

- `templateText`: Input template string containing `{{...}}` placeholders.
- `context`: Struct holding all the available data for placeholder resolution.
- `mode`: `Strict` or `Lenient` interpretation for missing/unresolved keys.
- `expandedOut`: Output, receives the expanded text.
- `errorMessage`: Output, receives an error message on failure (`Strict` mode).

#### Returns

- `true` if expansion succeeded.
- `false` on error (only in `Strict` mode) with `errorMessage` set.

---

## Error Handling

- **Malformed placeholders:**  
  Missing closing `}}` causes an error in strict mode.

- **Out-of-range indices:**  
  Invalid indices for `input[N]` or `output[N]` cause an error in strict mode.

- **Unknown keys:**  
  If a key is not resolvable, fails in strict mode; expands to an empty string in lenient mode.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::TemplateContext ctx;
ctx.m_FileInputs = &myInputs;
ctx.m_FileOutputs = &myOutputs;
ctx.m_InputValues = &mySlotValues;
ctx.m_EnvVariables = &myEnvVars;

std::string expanded, error;
bool ok = AIAssistant::ExpandTemplate(
    "cp {{input[0]}} {{outputs}} # by {{env.USER}}",
    ctx,
    AIAssistant::TemplateMode::Strict,
    expanded,
    error);

if (!ok) {
    std::cerr << error << std::endl;
}
```

---

## License

GPL-3.0 — JC Technolabs, 2026

---

## Summary

`ExpandTemplate` enables dynamic, context-based expansion of strings with flexible, configurable handling of missing or invalid placeholders. It is suitable for both scripting environments and AI-centric workflows.


---

### 95_doc_application_workflow_triggerEngine_h

<a id="95-doc-application-workflow-triggerengine-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/95_doc_application_workflow_triggerEngine_h/docu.output.md`



# AIAssistant::TriggerEngine

## Overview

`TriggerEngine` is a class responsible for registering, managing, and firing workflow triggers in an automation/workflow engine. It supports multiple trigger types and manages all aspects of their lifecycle, evaluation, and event delivery for both human users (CLI/Web UI) and automation systems.

**Key features:**
- Registers triggers: cron, auto, file-watch, manual, webhook, and several cloud storage/email polling types.
- Evaluates cron schedules and handles event-driven triggers.
- Delivers events via user-supplied callback when triggers fire.
- Thread-safe via internal mutex.

---

## Trigger Types

- **Auto Trigger:** Fires automatically once on registration (optionally only the first time, not after reload).
- **Cron Trigger:** Fires according to a cron expression and optional IANA timezone.
- **File-Watch Trigger:** Fires on file creation/modification/deletion under a watched path (debounced).
- **Manual Trigger:** Fires on explicit user request via CLI or Web UI.
- **Webhook Trigger:** Fires on verified HTTP webhook request with HMAC-based shared secret.
- **S3/OneDrive/Email/Azure Blob/GCS Watch Triggers:** Poll remote storage or mailboxes for changes, firing on polling intervals.

---

## API Summary

### Construction and Lifetime

```cpp
explicit TriggerEngine(TriggerCallback const& triggerCallback);
~TriggerEngine();
```
- `TriggerCallback` is invoked with a `TriggerFiredEvent` when a trigger fires.
- Not copyable/movable.

### Trigger Registration

- **Auto:**  
  `AddAutoTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, isEnabled, fireImmediately = true)`
- **Cron:**  
  `AddCronTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, expression, timezone, isEnabled)`
- **File-Watch:**  
  `AddFileWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, path, events, debounceMilliseconds, isEnabled)`
- **Manual:**  
  `AddManualTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, isEnabled)`
- **Webhook:**  
  `AddWebhookTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, secret, isEnabled)`
- **S3 Watch:**  
  `AddS3WatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, bucket, prefix, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)`
- **OneDrive Watch:**  
  `AddOneDriveWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, folder, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)`
- **Email Watch:**  
  `AddEmailWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, folder, subjectFilter, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)`
- **Azure Blob Watch:**  
  `AddAzureBlobWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, container, prefix, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)`
- **GCS Watch:**  
  `AddGcsWatchTrigger(workflowId, triggerId, connectionName, bucket, prefix, pollIntervalSeconds, isEnabled)`

Each registration method associates a trigger ID to a workflow and sets its configuration and enablement status.

### Trigger Removal

- `ClearWorkflowTriggers(workflowId)`: Remove all triggers for a given workflow.
- `ClearAll()`: Remove all triggers from the engine.

### Run-Time Operations

- `Tick(now)`: Call periodically to evaluate due cron and polling triggers.
- `NotifyFileEvent(path, fileEventType, now)`: Notify engine of a file event (called by FileWatcher).
- `FireManualTrigger(workflowId, triggerId)`: Fire a manual trigger on user request.
- `GetWebhookTrigger(workflowId) const`: Retrieve webhook trigger info for HTTP handler validation.

---

## Internal Structures

- **TriggerFiredEvent:** Contains the workflow ID and trigger ID to identify which workflow/run to start.
- Each trigger type has a corresponding `*TriggerInstance` struct holding configuration, state, and bookkeeping.
- File-watch and webhook triggers use acceleration (lookup) structures for efficient event/lookup dispatch.

---

## Thread Safety

All public methods are thread-safe. Internally, data is guarded by `std::mutex`.

---

## Cron Support

- Supports simple 5-field cron expressions: minute, hour, day-of-month, month, weekday. Only '*' or single integer allowed per field.
- Cron time resolution uses either C++20 chrono time zones or date.h for platform compatibility.
- Computes next fire time after a reference timestamp and given timezone.

---

## Event Firing

A user-supplied callback is invoked whenever any trigger fires, always providing the workflow and trigger ID.

---

## Typical Usage Pattern

1. Construct `TriggerEngine` with a callback.
2. Register triggers via `Add*Trigger()` methods.
3. Periodically call `Tick()` in your application loop.
4. Call `NotifyFileEvent()` upon receiving file system events from the FileWatcher.
5. Call `FireManualTrigger()` for manual workflow launches.
6. Use `GetWebhookTrigger()` to validate incoming webhook requests.

---

## Limitations

- Cron expression support is limited to single values or wildcards per field.
- Poll-based triggers must poll at intervals of at least 60 seconds.

---

## Example

```cpp
AIAssistant::TriggerEngine engine([](const TriggerEngine::TriggerFiredEvent& event) {
    // Handle workflow run
});

engine.AddCronTrigger("my_workflow", "daily_trigger", "0 3 * * *", "UTC", true);
engine.AddManualTrigger("my_workflow", "manual1", true);

// In your application's main loop:
engine.Tick(std::chrono::system_clock::now());
```

---

## License

GPL-3.0. See source for license text.


---

### 96_doc_application_workflow_workflowFileIndex_h

<a id="96-doc-application-workflow-workflowfileindex-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/96_doc_application_workflow_workflowFileIndex_h/docu.output.md`



# WorkflowFileIndex C++ Class Documentation

## Overview

`WorkflowFileIndex` is a thread-safe utility class that scans a directory tree, typically containing workflow files, and builds indices for fast file lookups. It allows searching by file basename, by relative path, and provides information about all indexed files. It is part of the `AIAssistant` namespace.

---

## Public Member Functions

### void ScanDirectory(const std::filesystem::path& rootDir)
Recursively scans the specified directory, indexing all non-hidden regular files. Hidden files or directories (starting with `.`) are ignored. This updates the internal indices.

### std::vector<std::filesystem::path> FindByBasename(const std::string& basename) const
Returns a vector of absolute paths to all files that have the specified basename (filename only, case-sensitive). Returns an empty vector if the basename is not found.

### std::filesystem::path FindByRelativePath(const std::string& relativePath) const
Resolves the given relative file path against the scanned root directory. Returns the absolute path if the file exists, or an empty path if not found.

### std::filesystem::path GetRootDirectory() const
Returns the absolute path to the root directory that was last scanned.

### std::vector<std::filesystem::path> GetAllFiles() const
Returns a vector of all indexed file absolute paths.

### size_t Size() const
Returns the number of indexed files.

### std::string SerializeMarkdownListing() const
Produces a concise Markdown list of all indexed files, relative to the root directory. Each line has the format `- relative/path/to/file`. Returns "(no files indexed)" if no files have been indexed.

---

## Internal Design

- **Thread Safety**: All public methods are protected by a mutex for thread-safe operation.
- **Indices**:
  - Basename index: `std::unordered_map` mapping file basename to all matching absolute paths.
  - File list: Vector of all absolute paths.
- **Hidden Files**: Files and directories starting with `.` are skipped during indexing.

---

## Use Cases

- Quickly locating workflow or log files by name or relative path.
- Generating listings for AI code generation or summary prompts.
- Reliable, thread-safe file indexing in multi-threaded applications.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::WorkflowFileIndex index;
index.ScanDirectory("/path/to/workflows");
auto allFiles = index.GetAllFiles();
auto matches = index.FindByBasename("example.log");
std::string markdown = index.SerializeMarkdownListing();
```

---

## License

MIT License – see source code for details.


---

### 97_doc_application_workflow_workflowJsonParser_h

<a id="97-doc-application-workflow-workflowjsonparser-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/97_doc_application_workflow_workflowJsonParser_h/docu.output.md`



# WorkflowJsonParser Class Documentation

**Namespace**: `AIAssistant`  
**Purpose**: Efficiently parses JCWF (JC Workflow Format) JSON documents into strongly-typed C++ workflow structures.

---

## Overview

`WorkflowJsonParser` reads, validates, and parses workflow definitions from JSON using the [simdjson](https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson) library.  
It produces a `WorkflowDefinition` object, supporting both full workflow ("monolithic" JSON) and multi-file split workflows (`global.json`/canvas JSONs).  
All error states are reported via return values and error message outputs, enabling robust, predictable error handling.

---

## Main Usage

- **ParseWorkflowJson**: Parses a full workflow JSON document (metadata + tasks, dataflow, triggers, etc.).
- **ParseGlobalJson**: Parses only high-level metadata from a `global.json` file (no task/dataflow, etc.).
- **ParseCanvasJson**: Parses task/dataflow/filter/control structure from a "canvas" JSON; metadata is ignored.
- **Sub-parsers**: Handle workflow components (tasks, triggers, dataflows, filters, etc.).
- **Utilities**: Provides helpers for JSON→string conversion, enum mapping, raw JSON extraction.

---

## Public API Reference

### bool ParseWorkflowJson(const std::string& jsonContent, WorkflowDefinition& workflowOut, std::string& errorMessage) const

- Parses a JCWF workflow JSON (full document).
- On success, populates `workflowOut`; returns true.
- On failure, returns false and sets `errorMessage`.

### bool ParseGlobalJson(const std::string& jsonContent, WorkflowDefinition& workflowOut, std::string& errorMessage) const

- Parses only high-level metadata (from a global.json).
- Ignores tasks, dataflow, etc.
- Returns true on success; false on failure.

### bool ParseCanvasJson(const std::string& jsonContent, WorkflowDefinition& workflowOut, std::string& errorMessage) const

- Parses canvas files (tasks, dataflow, filters, controlflow).
- Ignores/overrides metadata if present.
- Returns true on success; false on failure.

---

## Noteworthy Internal Behaviors

- **Version Validation**: Enforces "major.minor" version matching; warns if workflow version is newer than supported.
- **Automatic Defaults**: If workflow/task-level timeout/retry parameters are missing, applies values from workflow defaults.
- **Auto Trigger**: If no trigger is specified, adds a default "auto" trigger for compatibility.
- **Reference Validation**: Verifies that every task's `filter` field matches an actual filter id.
- **Robust Error Handling**: All failures immediately set an actionable error message.
- **Path Handling**: Logs and tracks whether task/file/queue paths are relative.

---

## Key Private Methods (Parsers)

- **ParseRootObject**: Entry point for the root workflow object.
- **ParseTriggers/ParseTrigger**: Parses the triggers array/individual trigger.
- **ParseTasks/ParseTask**: Parses the tasks object/individual task (including nested structures and type/enum mapping).
- **ParseTaskEnvironment, ParseTaskQueueBinding**: Parses environment/queue bindings for tasks.
- **ParseDataflow/ParseSingleDataflow**: Parses the dataflow array/individual edges.
- **ParseFilters/ParseFilter/ParseFilterSource**: Parses filters and their sources.
- **ParseControlNodes/ParseControlflow**: Parses control node and controlflow structures.
- **ParseDefaults/ParseRetries**: Parses workflow/task defaults and retry policy blocks.

---

## Utility Methods

- **ExtractRawJson**: Serializes a simdjson value to a raw JSON string.
- **ElementToString**: Converts a JSON value to its string or number/bool representation.
- **StringToTaskType / StringToTaskMode / StringToTriggerType**: Converts string fields to their corresponding enum types.

---

## Supported JCWF JSON Structure

Typical structure parsed by this class:

```json
{
  "version": "1.0",
  "id": "...",
  "label": "...",
  "doc": "...",
  "triggers": [ ... ],
  "tasks": { ... },
  "dataflow": [ ... ],
  "filters": [ ... ],
  "defaults": { ... }
  // Optionally: "control_nodes", "controlflow"
}
```

---

## Error Handling

- All parsing methods are non-throwing.
- Methods return `false` on failure and fill `errorMessage`.
- Unknown fields in recognized objects are logged and ignored, unless critical.
- Fails fast on missing required fields.

---

## Integration

- Requires simdjson for parsing.
- Consumes and produces only in-memory objects (no I/O).
- Extensible—more fields and workflow versions may be parsed/validated in future.

---

## Thread Safety

- The class itself is stateless, except for helper methods; safe for concurrent use if underlying data is managed appropriately.

---

## See Also

- `WorkflowDefinition`: Target output struct (see workflowTypes.h).
- `TaskDef`, `WorkflowTrigger`, `QueueBinding`, etc.: Workflow substructures.

---

## Copyright

MIT License, 2026 JC Technolabs.  
See code header for details.

---

**Summary**:  
`WorkflowJsonParser` enables fast, robust, and schema-aware loading of workflow definitions authored in JCWF JSON, for both monolithic and split configurations, with strong validation, enum mapping, defaults support, and clear error reporting.


---

### 98_doc_application_workflow_workflowJsonParserDetails_h

<a id="98-doc-application-workflow-workflowjsonparserdetails-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/98_doc_application_workflow_workflowJsonParserDetails_h/docu.output.md`



# WorkflowJsonParser Documentation

## Overview

The `WorkflowJsonParser` class provides functions to parse workflow-related JSON data into C++ structures. It is designed to efficiently convert and validate workflow configurations such as triggers, tasks, control nodes, dataflows, filters, environments, and other workflow graph elements, using [simdjson](https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson) for fast JSON processing.

All parsing functions take a simdjson `ondemand::value` or `ondemand::object`, the relevant output variable(s), and an error message string reference. On parse failure, the function returns `false` and sets `errorMessage`.

---

## Functionality

### Triggers

#### `bool ParseTriggers(simdjson::ondemand::value& jsonValue, std::vector<WorkflowTrigger>& triggersOut, std::string& errorMessage) const`

Parses an array of triggers from `jsonValue`. Each entry must be an object and is parsed into a `WorkflowTrigger`. On success, populates `triggersOut`.

---

### Control-Flow Graph

- **Control Nodes**  
  `bool ParseControlNodes(simdjson::ondemand::value&, std::vector<ControlNodeDef>&, std::string&) const`  
  Parses an array of control nodes. Each must have `id` and `type` fields and is stored as `ControlNodeDef`.

- **Controlflow Edges**  
  `bool ParseControlflow(simdjson::ondemand::value&, std::vector<ControlflowEdgeDef>&, std::string&) const`  
  Parses an array of controlflow edges between nodes. Each must have `from`, `to`, and `kind` fields.

---

### Tasks

- **Tasks**  
  `bool ParseTasks(simdjson::ondemand::value&, std::unordered_map<std::string, TaskDef>&, std::string&) const`  
  Parses an object where each key maps to a `TaskDef`. If missing, task `id` uses the map key.

- **Task Inputs**  
  `bool ParseTaskInputs(simdjson::ondemand::value&, TaskIOMap&, std::string&) const`  
  Parses the `inputs` object for a task, mapping each field to a `TaskIOField`.

- **Task Outputs**  
  `bool ParseTaskOutputs(simdjson::ondemand::value&, TaskIOMap&, std::string&) const`  
  Parses the `outputs` object for a task, mapping each field to a `TaskIOField`.

- **Task Environment**  
  `bool ParseTaskEnvironment(simdjson::ondemand::value&, TaskEnvironment&, std::string&) const`  
  Parses the `environment` object for a task, extracts `name`, `assistant_id`, and a map of `variables`.

- **Task Queue Binding**  
  `bool ParseTaskQueueBinding(simdjson::ondemand::value&, QueueBinding&, std::string&) const`  
  Parses task queue binding configuration using `AIAssistant::ParseTaskQueueBinding`.

---

### Dataflow

#### `bool ParseDataflow(simdjson::ondemand::value&, std::vector<DataflowDef>&, std::string&) const`

Parses an array of dataflow connections. Each must have `from_task`, `from_output`, `to_task`, `to_input`. May also parse a `mapping` object for variable assignment.

#### `bool ParseSingleDataflow(simdjson::ondemand::object&, DataflowDef&, std::string&) const`

Parses a single dataflow entry object.

---

### Filters

- **Filters**
  `bool ParseFilters(simdjson::ondemand::value&, std::vector<FilterDef>&, std::string&) const`
  Parses an array of filter objects. Each filter requires `id`, `source`, and `binding`.

- **Filter**
  `bool ParseFilter(simdjson::ondemand::object&, FilterDef&, std::string&) const`
  Parses a single filter definition.

- **Filter Source**
  `bool ParseFilterSource(simdjson::ondemand::object&, FilterSource&, std::string&) const`
  Parses a filter source object; supported fields include `kind`, `path`, `delimiter`, `has_header`, `range`, `skip_empty`, `index_path`, `query`, `fields`, `connection`, `base_url`, `project_id`, `key_name`, `page_size`.

---

### Error Handling

If any field is missing, not an expected type, or has parse/JSON errors, the function returns `false` and sets `errorMessage` with a descriptive message.

Unknown fields result in logging a warning but do not cause a failure.

---

## Usage Example

```cpp
simdjson::ondemand::parser parser;
auto doc = parser.iterate(json_data);
std::string error;
std::vector<WorkflowTrigger> triggers;

if (!workflowJsonParser.ParseTriggers(doc["triggers"], triggers, error)) {
    // Handle error
}
```

---

## Dependencies

- [simdjson](https://github.com/simdjson/simdjson): efficient JSON parsing
- Logging macros: `LOG_CORE_WARN`, `LOG_APP_INFO`
- Types: `WorkflowTrigger`, `TaskDef`, `TaskIOMap`, `TaskEnvironment`, `QueueBinding`, `ControlNodeDef`, `ControlflowEdgeDef`, `FilterDef`, `DataflowDef`, and supporting enums/classes.

---

## Notes

- All functions validate structure and type per field.
- Some utility functions like `ElementToString` are assumed to extract string values from JSON elements.
- Unknown/extra fields are logged but ignored in parsing.
- The parser is strict: required fields must be present and of the correct type.

---

### 99_doc_application_workflow_workflowRegistry_h

<a id="99-doc-application-workflow-workflowregistry-h"></a>

*Source doc:* `../../../queue/jarvisCppDocu/99_doc_application_workflow_workflowRegistry_h/docu.output.md`



# WorkflowRegistry C++ Class Documentation

**Namespace:** `AIAssistant`  
**Header:** `workflow/workflowRegistry.h`  
**License:** GPL-3.0  
**Copyright:** © 2025 JC Technolabs

## Purpose

`WorkflowRegistry` manages workflow definitions (.jcwf containers) for an AI assistant platform. It loads, validates, saves, updates, removes, and indexes workflows and their dependencies. Supports error reporting for workflows that fail to load or parse.

---

## Public API Summary

### Load/Unload

- **`bool LoadDirectory(const std::filesystem::path& workflowsDirectoryPath)`**  
  Loads all `.jcwf` container files from a directory into the registry. Clears existing data first. Broken or unparseable workflows are tracked.
  - Returns `true` on success (including empty but valid directories), `false` on error.

- **`void Clear()`**  
  Removes all workflows and broken workflow records from the registry.

---

### Validation

- **`bool ValidateAll() const`**  
  Validates internal consistency of all loaded workflows, checking ID consistency and task dependencies.
  - Returns `true` if all workflows are valid, `false` if any issues are found.

---

### Query

- **`std::vector<std::string> GetWorkflowIds() const`**  
  Returns a sorted list of all loaded workflow IDs.

- **`std::optional<WorkflowDefinition> GetWorkflow(const std::string& workflowId) const`**  
  Returns a copy of the workflow definition for the given ID if it exists, else `std::nullopt`.

- **`std::optional<std::string> TryGetWorkflowFilePathAbsolute(const std::string& workflowId) const`**  
  Returns the absolute path to the workflow file for a given workflow ID if known.

- **`std::optional<std::string> TryGetWorkflowIdByFilePath(const std::string& absoluteFilePath) const`**  
  Returns the workflow ID for a given absolute file path, if any workflow is associated with that path.

- **`std::unordered_map<std::string, std::vector<std::string>> GetSubWorkflowDependencyGraph() const`**  
  Returns a map where each workflow ID maps to a list of child workflow IDs (sub-workflow dependencies).

---

### Error Reporting

- **`const std::vector<BrokenWorkflow>& GetBrokenWorkflows() const`**  
  Returns a list of broken workflows that failed to load or parse.  
  Each `BrokenWorkflow` includes:  
  - `m_ContainerPath`: Absolute path to the `.jcwf` file  
  - `m_Stem`: Filename stem (used as a display ID)  
  - `m_Error`: Error message with details

---

### Save/Update/Remove

- **`bool SaveOrUpdateWorkflowFromJson(const std::string& workflowJson, const std::filesystem::path& workflowFilePathAbsolute, std::string& errorMessage)`**  
  Parses workflow JSON, writes files to disk in canonical `.jcwf` format, and adds/updates the registry. Updates the error message string on failure.
  - Returns `true` on success, `false` on error.

- **`bool RemoveWorkflow(const std::string& workflowId, bool deleteFile, std::string& errorMessage)`**  
  Removes a workflow from the registry. If `deleteFile` is true, deletes the workflow file from disk if its path is known. Error reason is written to errorMessage on failure.
  - Returns `true` on success, `false` if workflow not found or file deletion fails.

---

## Internal/Private

- Load container and sub-workflow files recursively (`LoadContainer`, `LoadContainerSubWorkflows`), parsing metadata and workflow tasks.
- Tracks broken workflows and last container error for diagnostics.

---

## Notes & Behavior

- Workflows are stored as zip containers (`.jcwf`), each containing `global.json` (metadata) and one or more canvas JSON files (tasks/dataflow).
- Sub-workflows are discovered recursively and linked to their parent via deterministic IDs.
- Validates uniqueness, task definitions, and dependencies.
- Broken workflows are not silently dropped: full metadata and error cause is tracked for UI or logging.
- Supports reverse lookup (find workflow by file path).
- Designed for robust operation in presence of malformed or incomplete workflow files.

---

## Key Types

- **`WorkflowDefinition`**: Structure containing workflow metadata, tasks, paths, triggers, etc.
- **`BrokenWorkflow`**: Tracks information for workflows that failed to parse or load.

---

## Example Usage

```cpp
AIAssistant::WorkflowRegistry registry;
registry.LoadDirectory("/path/to/workflows");

auto ids = registry.GetWorkflowIds();
for (auto const& id : ids) {
    auto workflow = registry.GetWorkflow(id);
    // use workflow...
}

auto broken = registry.GetBrokenWorkflows();
for (const auto& bw : broken) {
    std::cout << "Failed: " << bw.m_ContainerPath << " reason: " << bw.m_Error << std::endl;
}
```

---

## Thread Safety

Not thread safe. Clients should ensure serialized access if using from multiple threads.

---

## Summary Table

| Function                                          | Purpose                                     |
|---------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|
| LoadDirectory                                     | Load all workflows from directory           |
| ValidateAll                                       | Check internal consistency                  |
| GetWorkflowIds                                    | List available workflow IDs                 |
| GetWorkflow                                       | Get workflow definition by ID               |
| GetBrokenWorkflows                                | Access broken workflow info                 |
| TryGetWorkflowFilePathAbsolute                    | Get workflow file path by ID                |
| TryGetWorkflowIdByFilePath                        | Reverse lookup: file path → workflow ID     |
| GetSubWorkflowDependencyGraph                     | Sub-workflow dependencies                   |
| SaveOrUpdateWorkflowFromJson                      | Save or update workflow from JSON           |
| RemoveWorkflow                                    | Remove workflow and optionally delete file  |
| Clear                                             | Clear all loaded data                       |
